# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1978

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Kīrtanānanda Swami

Vyāsa-pūjā, worship of the spiritual master, is not some modern invention. It has existed since history began. Śrīla Vyāsa is the incarnation of Godhead who compiled the *Vedas* over five thousand years ago. So “Vyāsa-pūjā” refers to worshiping the bona fide *guru* who is following in disciplic succession from Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

People sometimes question why the spiritual master who appears as an ordinary human being is worshiped in the same way as we worship the Supreme Lord Himself. This is not very difficult to understand even by material example. For instance, the viceroy or representative of the king is given all respects that are due to the king. Sometimes presentations are even made to him when in fact they are meant for the king, but he never keeps such gifts for himself, nor does he consider that since all these people are honoring him that he has become as good as the king. He always remains the king’s obedient servant.

Similarly, the spiritual master is the most obedient servant of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. He never thinks that his disciples are worshiping him. He is simply accepting it all on behalf of Kṛṣṇa. As far as the disciple is concerned, he has no other means of approaching Kṛṣṇa save and except through the medium of the bona fide spiritual master. Śrīla Viṣvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, a great *ācārya* in the line of Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu, has written, “By the mercy of the spiritual master, one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, no one can make any advancement.” Therefore, all respect, worship and humble service are due to the spiritual master, who is addressed as “His Divine Grace”, because without his help no one can become free from the cycle of repeated birth and death.

Our spiritual master, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, has practically demonstrated the potency of a bona fide representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. In twelve short years he preached all over the world to people who had never heard of Kṛṣṇa. Now, in every continent of the glove there are temples filled with devotees chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare and following the rules and regulations of devotional service.

In appreciation of his divine work, his disciples have compiled these humble offerings in an attempt to glorify their eternal well-wisher, His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Kīrtanānanda Swami*

## Introduction

**“Who is a Great Soul?”**

The life of a great soul should be approached humbly. We cannot assess his activities as we would those of an ordinary man. But who is a great soul? The communists will argue that Karl Marx was a great soul for his speculation on how to distribute wealth to the economically oppressed masses. Or one may consider Mahatma Gandhi to be a great soul. (The Sanskrit work *mahātmā* means “great soul”. But what is the criterion for becoming a *mahātmā,* aside from granting someone the name “Mahatma” by rubber stamp?) The enthusiasts of action and nationalism may want to say Caesar or Napoleon was a *mahātmā.* The humanists will say some of the great classical composers, painters, and literary authors were great souls. And there are so many famous saints of the East as well as the West. And there are many famous philosophers, scientists, and humanitarians. Certainly one of the important criteria for deciding on greatness is the contribution of work which is beneficial for all humanity. From the Vedic literature, the person who is a pure representative of God is declared to be the most auspicious personality for the world. The atheist or agnostic humanist will disagree unless we define worship of God according to their mundane interpretation as “attainment of political freedom,” “worship of the poor,” “worship of art,” “philosophical speculation,” etc. But the Vedic literature pursues the definition of *mahātmā* as the servant of God by giving us absolute information of the Personality of Godhead. Knowledge of the Godhead is then seen as valuable beyond comparison to all else, and therefore he who has realized this science of God and works to distribute it to the world is the real *mahātmā.* If we study the relative contributions, we will see that the world help offered by the distribution of God consciousness is the highest humanitarian welfare activity. But even those who would declare themselves as servants of God will have to qualify according to standard criteria. According to the *Bhagavad-gītā* (7.3), “Out of many thousands among men one may endeavor for perfection, and out of those who have reached perfection, hardly one can actually know the Personality of Godhead.” In the sixteenth century Rūpa Gosvāmi rendered the following praise unto the greatest of all *bhaktas* (pure devotees), Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who appeared in India at that time:

*namo mahā-vadānyāya*  
*kṛṣṇa-prema-pradāya te*  
*kṛṣṇāya kṛṣṇa-caitanya-*  
*nāmne gaura-tviṣe namaḥ*  
*\[Cc. Madhya 19.53\]*

**“O most munificent incarnation, I offer my obeisances unto You, who are freely distributing what was never given to humanity before, pure love of Kṛṣṇa.”**

Thus while the worldly and the theological atheists claim that God is dead, and while the impersonalist meditator would tell us that the highest realization of the Absolute contains no personal existence, the pure devotee nevertheless immediately delivers knowledge of God. And what is rarer still, pure love of God, to all who will receive Him.

The *Caitanya-carit*āmṛ*ta* invites those who would contest humanitarian activities of the pure devotee of God: “If you are indeed interested in logic and argument, kindly apply to the mercy of Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu. If you do so, you will find it to be strikingly wonderful.” (*Cc. Ādi* 8.15) The crucial superiority of God conscious humanitarian activities is that they are based on the spirit soul, which is eternal. Everyone else is engaged in humanitarian activities on the basis of the body, but the material body is ultimately subject to destruction, whereas the spirit soul is eternal. The pure devotee’s philanthropic activities are performed in connection with the eternal soul. However one tries to benefit the body, it will be destroyed, and he will have to accept another body according to his present activities. If one considers the body to be all in all, his intelligence is not very advanced. Thus the pure devotee, without neglecting the necessities of the body, imparts spiritual advancement to purify the existential condition of humanity.

To distribute real good necessitates knowing the actual interest of the living being. According to the *Bhagavad-gītā,* all suffering is stemming from a basic ignorance of the self as no more than the temporary body. In the *Gītā,* Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, tells His bewildered disciples Arjuna that the real self, ā*tm*ā, is permanent, while the body is always changing. Even within one lifetime one experiences the transmigration of self from body to body. One is born as a baby then later takes the body of youth, then old age and finally death. But throughout these changes on experiences his identity to be the same “I”. As he changes bodies in this life, so at the time of death the ā*tm*ā, which is eternal, does not die with the body but takes another body. One who cannot understand this basic principle of transmigration, who takes the body as the self, is ignorant of the very *ABC* of existence and cannot help anyone.

The constitutional position of the living being is eternity, bliss, and knowledge. He has come to the material world out of a misuse of his free will and a mistaken notion that he can be happy in temporary existence. The nature of material life, sometimes described as survival of the fittest, is to forget one’s spiritual nature and the supremacy of God and to attempt for supremacy in a field where happiness or even survival is impossible. The real goal of human life is to realize one’s spiritual nature and to make an end to all the sufferings by returning to the life of eternity, bliss, and knowledge. When the whole society is based on this goal, the one’s life is truly auspicious, and without ultimate self-realization as the goal, various attempts at liberation from the worldly disorders will be failures. Persons who have no experience of spiritual life will remain trying to make stop-gap solutions in a world which is by nature miserable and temporary. But when a pure devotee, not simply by speaking theory but by actual deliverance of the process of love of God, delivers the people of the world and brings them to the original state of eternity, bliss, and knowledge even while in this material world, then we must recognize him as the one and only *mahātmā*. His contribution is beyond the bodily intellectual plane. And it is very difficult to find such a person in this world. As stated in the Śrī*mad-Bh*ā*gavatam* (6.14.4), “Only a few desire liberation from the material world. Among many thousands who desire liberation, one may actually achieve liberation, giving up material attachment to society, friendship, love, country, wife, and children. And among many thousands of such liberated persons, one who can understand the true meaning of liberation is very rare. O great sage, among many millions who are liberated and perfect in knowledge of liberation, only one may be found as a devotee of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Such devotees who are fully peaceful are extremely rare.”

Distribution of love of God is sometimes seen as an emotional affair for the less intelligent, but this view is completely superficial. The science of *bhakti*, distribution of devotional service to God, when distributed by a *mahātmā* is at once sublime and easy. It is sublime for those pure devotees who are realizing direction association with God, and yet it is easy for the people in general who can be brought at once to the threshold of the house of *bhakti*. To achieve contact with the Supreme Personality of Godhead is a great science, but it is open to all living beings, regardless of education or social or economic class. All corollary spiritual and material ends can be seen to follow the pure and scientific execution of *bhakti*. Pure *bhakti* is also expressed in the first great commandment of Lord Jesus Christ: “To love the Lord thy God with thy whole heart, thy whole mind, and thy whole soul.” Only if mankind can be brought to this first point, by the pure devotees, can they realize the second commandment of Jesus: “And the second is like unto it, ‘Love thy neighbor as thy self.’”

The special feature of the pure devotee is that his work is entirely selfless, because he works entirely for the pleasure of God. Everyone else acts in this world to maintain family or paraphernalia with some self-interest and personal gratification. The gratification may be concentrated, or it may be extended. One may be out to become famous himself or to become a great enjoyer, or he may wish to extend material benefits to his family or to humanity at large. But all these are still on the platform of selfish bodily interest. The criterion of perfection is to act in God consciousness and not with a view to enjoying the fruits of work, either locally, nationally or internationally. To act in God consciousness is the duty of every living entity, because we are all constitutionally parts and parcels of the Supreme. It is only the atheistic propaganda which has turned men to think that they are dead-end material entities unto themselves, and that there is no good except working futilely at objectives within this material life. The greatest disservice has been rendered by those who have turned men away from their natural connection with God, who is readily seen as the proprietor of everything in the universe. As the limbs of the body constitutionally work for the satisfaction of the whole body, so the living entity who acts for the satisfaction of the Supreme Whole is the perfect, selfless worker. Even among religionists, the *yogīs* and impersonal philosophers, although acting on a level more elevated than the ordinary worker, are still not without self-interest. Either they desire to become liberated in the impersonal spiritual existence, or they desire to cease material activities in order to gain mystic perfections for the satisfaction of the personal self. But a pure devotee (the skeptic asks, “Where does such purity exist?”), acting in God consciousness, works for the satisfaction of the whole without self-interest. He has no desire for self-satisfaction. His criterion of success is the satisfaction of Kṛṣṇa. Thus Lord Caitanya prays, “O almighty Lord, I have no desire to accumulate wealth, nor to enjoy beautiful women, nor do I want any number of followers, but what I want only is the causeless mercy of Your devotional service in my life, birth after birth.” \[*Cc. Antya* 20.29\] Working in this pure consciousness and with the potent possession of love of God, such a worker can actually deliver anyone to the highest happiness. It is therefore stated in the Vedic scriptures that even a moment’s association with a pure devotee can grant one all success. An unfortunate person will doubt that such purity exists or whether a devotee has the ability to give God consciousness to the masses. But one who actually meets a pure devotee can be given these highest gifts from him and will realize it without a doubt. Although they are very rare, such great souls have walked the earth throughout history. To hear of their activities is very beneficial, but it must be done with true understanding and not in the mood of psychological analysis or faultfinding, which we may apply to an ordinary person.

## Prabhupada Nectar

We all know Śrīla Prabhupāda was *mahātm*ā*.* We have faith in him. We like to hear a philosophical discourse proving Śrīla Prabhupāda in intellectual language. And it will benefit our preaching to know the *śāstric* references; how he is *śaktyāveśa-avatāra*, how he has a spiritual body, and how his knowledge is never imperfect. It will strengthen us to know these things. As Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja has said, one should not avoid argumentative logic in defense of Kṛṣṇa and His devotees. But that is not the only way we know he was a pure devotee. We know because we were with him and saw what he did; because he entered our hearts ad planted the seed of the devotional that is still growing for each of us. I am not saying the new disciples to Kṛṣṇa consciousness cannot know this. In fact, by the feelings-in-separation (*vipralambha*) of his disciples in *paramparā*, new *bhaktas* today are knowing Śrīla Prabhupāda just as sweet, sometimes sweeter, than those who were on the planet at the same time he was. It is a fact, association with Śrīla Prabhupāda is growing sweeter, and we are realizing his presence even more, day by day, by the power of devotional service in disciplic succession. His Holiness Kīrtanānanda Swami helped me realize this when I spoke to him and confided that I had so much intimate association with Śrīla Prabhupāda in the beginning, in 1966, but later my physical intimacy diminished. He said, “But now you are more closely with Prabhupāda with every breath than you were even then.” I happily admitted it was a fact. Although I very proudly consider it my greatest personal fortune that I was there in 1966, it is a fact I was then less Prabhupāda conscious and less Kṛṣṇa conscious than I am now. So it is also a fact that new devotees, the disciples of his disciples, may find themselves bathing in more Prabhupāda *kathā* and relishing his inspiration as grandfather *guru* even more than anyone has before. (Wasn’t Kṛṣṇa dāsa Kavirāja relishing Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu only after His disappearance by hearing from Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmī and reading the notebooks of Murāri Gupta and Svarūpa Dāmodara, and reading Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura’s *Caitanya-bh*ā*gavata*?) My point is, let us keep the subject matter of Śrīla Prabhupāda alive and foremost. It will not die with the present generation of disciples. “he reasons ill who says that Vaiṣṇavas die when thou art living still in sound. The Vaiṣṇavas die to live and living try to spread the holy name around.” So as an introduction to these 1978 Vyāsa-pūjā homages to Śrīla Prabhupāda, I would like to churn a little of the ocean of Prabhupāda nectar. Why? Because it is a great transcendental happiness. Because we love him and because those who do not know will derive the greatest benefit. To remember and glorify the Vaiṣṇava is to glorify Kṛṣṇa Himself.

His disciples and his disciples’ disciples know he was *mahātmā*. we are as certain of this as we are certain we have found the truth when we read the pages of the *Bhagavad-g*ītā *As It Is.* We know by knowledge, by service, and by faith. And what is that faith born of? It is the causeless mercy of the devotee and the Lord. And the faith is also coming from the Supersoul in our hearts, rewarding us for past accidental service or our past devotional acts. “Be with Prabhupāda,” the Lord dictates in our hearts. “Know him; approach him; he is a great soul. You can surrender unto him. He will impart knowledge unto you because he has seen the truth.” And by what we hear and learn about Kṛṣṇa we are able to defeat all doubtful speculation, atheism, and illicit desire. We are given strength to preach the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Let us now hear how others knew him.

In Navadvīpa, last March, I approached Śrīla Prabhupāda’s associate over many years, Nārāyana Mahārāja, and he told me many pastimes about Śrīla Prabhupāda. After about an hour, I then asked him what he thought of the plan of a biography.

**Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami:** The disciples are pretty much wanting to hear the compilation of his actual life activities, not just his ideas. Is that all right? Like *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*?

**Nārāyana Mahārāja:** Yes, you can do that also. You can do.

**SG:** Is it all right to get the impression from his leading disciples to tell his story?

**NM:** Yes, I think it will be not proper to go into detail with worldly stories, all these things.

**SG:** Even during his *sannyāsa* and his preaching activities, are they considered worldly?

**NM:** That should be written, but not worldly things.

**SG:** Let’s say, like he was saying, that Svāmījī went to the park and played on the *karatālas* …

**NM:** This thing should be done.

**SG:** Or even more intimate, but still spiritual.

**NM:** How he preached, how he converted, how he persisted, and how he brought all you. This should be done. His mercy should be expressed, how was his mercy. How he loved all wise, and everything. He did not love you for your physical feature or beautifulness or anything. He loved you only to attach you to Kṛṣṇa, bring you to Kṛṣṇa, the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. You are flowers, you are as flowers, and he took you all in his *añjali* and put on the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Ordinary man does not get any of this flower, but ordinary flower. So putting you as flowers, he gets you as a flower at the feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa he worshiped with you. It was his mercy on us.

Now, as before a grand fire *yajña,* devotees are offering their oblations of memories of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda. For if ever a person deserved commemorative praise, it is he. We shall fail to express most of his actual greatness or dearness to Kṛṣṇa. But as Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja says, “Although the sky of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s pastimes is unlimited, we can try to fly as high as we are able.” Śrīla Prabhupāda himself sometimes remembered how he came here on the order of his Guru Mahārāja at such personal risk at seventy years old with no money to speak of, suffering two heart attacks on a one-month-and-ten-day ocean voyage. What was it like then in New York when he was all alone? He recalled some of it for the devotees when he visited New York in 1976. he was driving in a car to go for a morning walk, the car went past Seventy-second Street, where he had stayed for some time, all alone, in 1965, and the following dialogue took place:

**Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Gosvami:** This is Seventy-second Street, Prabhupāda.

**Prabhupāda:**  Yes, I know.

**TKG:** You were going to show us that building?

**Prabhupāda:** It is on the corner of Amsterdam and Seventy-second Street.

**TKG:** That means the next street.

**Prabhupāda:**  This is Amsterdam?

**TKG:** No, Columbus Avenue. The next is Amsterdam.

**Devotee:**  There is 100th.

**TKG:** That’s the building, Prabhupāda?

**Prabhupāda:** Yes.

**Devotee:** It says Watergate. Watergate Hotel.

**TKG:** Which floor did you live on?

**Prabhupāda:** And I was trying to purchase one house here.

**TKG:** Which floor did you live on, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

**Prabhupāda:** I think the third floor. And there was an electrician, he was my friend, one Jewish gentleman.

**Devotee:** And you would walk on this street?

**Prabhupāda:** Yes, there is one building with temperature. Oh, here is Broadway. I was taking bath in this station sometimes. I would take in the station. I think this building is new. I was going to Dr. Misra’s apartment for cooking.

**TKG:** What street did he live on?

**Prabhupāda:** He? Seventy-eight, Riverside Corner. Yes, I was purchasing my goods from this store.

**Devotee:** West End Superette.

P**rabhupāda:** The were charging a little chili powder, twenty-five cents. In India it may be one anna.

**TKG:**  You were purchasing here?

**Prabhupāda:** Yes, because I was going to cook my food there, so whatever I needed I used to …

**TKG:** How come you didn’t cook your food where you were living?

**Prabhupāda:** Eh? There was no place.

**TKG:** What was it like there?

**Prabhupāda:** It was an office. That building is meant for office, not for residential.

**TKG:** You rented a room there?

**Prabhupāda:** Yes. I was paying seventy-two dollars a month.

**TKG:** And where did you sleep? Was there a bed?

**Prabhupāda:** No bed there, where I was living. There was toilet and water, but no bath and no cooking.

**Devotee:** Did you have to go there to bathe also?

**Prabhupāda:** Yes, I was taking in the building there.

**TKG:** Where did you … did you sleep on the floor?

**Prabhupāda:** Yes, I had little platform, so on that platform.

**TKG:** You are the most bold person in the whole world.

**Devotee:** We will never be able to do what you have done.

**Prabhupāda:** There alone I was doing, and then gradually one or two boys began to come.

**TKG:** Did any of them come up here who are still with you now?

**Prabhupāda:** No.

**TKG:** Only when you went downtown did permanent men come?

**Prabhupāda:** Yes.

**Devotee:** That’s amazing. How long were your there, Prabhupāda?

**Prabhupāda:** Here? About six months. Then when my things were stolen, when one boy was coming whose name was Paul Murray, he invited me, “You come to my loft.” He took me to the Bowery Street.

**TKG:** You must have felt very bad when your things were stolen.

**Prabhupāda:** Yes, I had little disappointment, but some friends, they offered me, “Oh, never mind, you take my typewriter.” Somebody, “You take my tape recorder.”

**TKG:** Things were very easy then?

**Devotee:** Not easy.

Surabhi Svāmī met a man in the Vancouver temple who met Prabhupāda on a park bench in 1965 in Prabhupāda’s first days in New York. There must have been hundreds and thousands who contacts Śrīla Prabhupāda in some way, and certainly their lives are benefited more by that association than by any other event in their lives.

**Surabhi Svāmī:** So how did you meet him? He approached you, or you approached him?

**Man:** Well, I sat on the bench with him because I saw that he had a *dhot*ī*.* I know now, but I didn’t know what it was then. But a quite unusual dress. And I usually sat on that bench that he sat on like that. And then he had a special quality about him that was very dignified.

**SS:** Sitting straight?

**Man:** Oh, yes. And he was a very old man, and I didn’t speak right to him, right along. But then I didn’t think he would speak English, the way he dressed. Yet he spoke very nice English. Very well, English.

**SS:** so what did he tell to you? Did you start to speak to him, or he spoke to you first?

**Man:** Well, I actually spoke to him. I asked him if he was from India. And he said yes, he had just come from India. This was a few days here; a day here. I think it was a day before. He was saying he was coming by boat. Long trip.

**SS:** What else did he say the first day you met him?

**Man:** The first thing off the bat, he starts speaking spiritual things right away. “there are the temples here. We have temples.” Like that.

**SS:** Where, in India?

**Man:** No. “We have temples here,” he said. “But it is not the time yet to open them, to enter them. We have temples, we have farms.” Then he mentioned books. He said, “there are a lot of books. Yet they are not open yet. They are not read yet. There is a time that is separating.”

**SS:** He mentioned books? He mentioned farms? He mentioned temples?

**Man:** Temples, books, farms … I think that’s it. He kept on mentioning more to that effect.

In 1966, one boy met His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda and was given full manifestation of his mercy. He went away, but came back years later and is now a very nice devotee named Prabhupāda dāsa. Here is his memory.

**Prabhupāda dāsa:** One time I simply expressed how much I was in difficulty and how much I was not even able to maintain myself, and how much I wanted some help and wanted some shelter. That particular time was pretty much the climax of the whole period for me; then Śrīla Prabhupāda personally offered me a *dhot*ī and told me, he said, “Please join us. We’ll solve all your problems.”

**Puru dāsa:** He handed you a *dhotī* ?

**Prabhupāda dāsa:** Yes, he handed me a *dhotī*. I didn’t quite take it up. I was embarrassed. The idea of putting on a *dhotī*  seemed extremely strange to me, very foreign. I was embarrassed. And I also was obviously not quite ready. Even though the offer was made in such an attractive way by a pure devotee, I was so contaminated that I wasn’t quite able to. I was tempted, I was very much tempted, but I started backing up, looking at Śrīla Prabhupāda, taking backward steps. And in a shuddering voice, I said, “I am not quite ready.” And then Śrīla Prabhupāda walked after me, and I distinctly remember a tear in his eye. He said, “Please come back,” as I left.

Citsukhānanda remembers when Prabhupāda went to Mexico in 1972 and manifested his opulence of nonstop preaching and ecstatic mood.

As usual he was up since 1:30 A.M.; so he must have slept maybe an hour. I think he slept for an hour. From 1:00 to 2:00. And he began his work meditating on Kṛṣṇa at that time. We were all just amazed. His lights were on in his room. And we went into the temple room and chanted *maṇgala-ārati*, and after that Śrīla Prabhupāda asked me again, “What will we do today?” I said, “Oh, we will go to Cuernavaca. We’ve arranged a whole plaza, the whole central plaza of Cuernavaca City. They’re giving it to us today for your lecture, with microphones and a bandstand and everything all ready for you to come.” He said, “Oh, this is very nice.” “And after that we will give a lecture at a hotel with prominent ladies and gentlemen. A very nice hotel, in Cuernavaca. And then we can go to Lakṣmīpriyā dāsī’s house and take *prasādam*.” He said, “Yes, this is very nice.” So Śrīla Prabhupāda gave a wonderful lecture and the whole city was ecstatic, and just then, before going to the hotel lecture, the plaza was filled with people, and we had a very ecstatic *kīrtana* and Śrīla Prabhupāda was very, very happy to be able to speak to so many people. And the questions that the people were asking were very nice. Nothing more enlivening for Śrīla Prabhupāda than to see someone who was trying to understand what he was speaking. So then we went to the hotel after, and there at the hotel Prabhupāda spoke to about sixty or seventy ladies and gentlemen, very nicely dressed, suits and ties, and a very special occasion in this luxurious hotel. And Śrīla Prabhupāda began speaking Kṛṣṇa consciousness as always – simple and nice. So from 8:00 in the morning until 8:00 P.M. he had not taken a bite of food. A little water he had taken. He didn’t want anything. We offered him fruit and things, but he didn’t want. And we offered him to take some feast at Lakṣmīpriyā’s house, but he said, “No, I will go back to the temple.” And then we went back to the temple. At that time he had brought some servants with him when he came from Los Angeles. Now Śrīla Prabhupāda was in his room and he was in great ecstasy. I didn’t get a chance to see it, but from what I understood from Pradyumna, he explained to me, “Prabhupāda’s in ecstasy right now in his room. His eyes are shining and his mouth is very bright.” And he said to them, “This is the way to be happy. Work all day for Kṛṣṇa.” All he wanted was a hot cup of milk with around eight *purīs* and a cup of sugar. He crushed the *purīs* into the sugar and drank the milk. And he drank it with great joy and happiness and he said, “This is our life. To serve Kṛṣṇa work all day for Kṛṣṇa and take a little *prasāda* at night.” He said, “This is happiness. This is life.”

Yaduvara remembers Śrīla Prabhupāda in India:

In March 1972, we were in Calcutta for the first Māyāpur festival. Everyone went to Māyāpur. Prabhupāda stayed in a little grass hut, which still stands at the entrance as you go in, on the left-hand side of the gate. Prabhupāda liked that hut. He said, “You can build me the biggest palace, but still I would prefer to live here,” and he was very comfortable in that little hut. It was actually very cool. This is when Bob Cohen, who is now Brahmatīrtha, first me Prabhupāda, and his famous discussions are in the book *Perfect Questions, Perfect Answers*. Prabhupāda took a liking to him immediately, a very intelligent boy, and of course he later became a devotee, as well as his wife, who is now Bhakti dāsī. Bob Cohen asked: “What do you mean by ‘separated energies’?” Prabhupāda had a glass of milk at that time. He said, “Just like this milk. It is the separate energy of the cow.” All of us were struck with wonder at the simplicity of the perfect example Prabhupāda immediately presented. At that time there was the cornerstone-laying ceremony. Before the cornerstone was laid there was a hole dug, and the ceremony was performed and a little golden Ananta with a jewel was put in the pit. I remember going up to Prabhupāda’s room before. I wanted to photograph that Ananta before He went underneath the ground. So Prabhupāda took the Ananta made of gold from where he had it. Somehow or other he produced it, and as he brought it out, the jewel on His head was glittering. Prabhupāda held it, and it was a wonderful sight.

Kañcanabālā-devī dāsī remembers Śrīla Prabhupāda in Vṛndāvana:

“I always wanted to see His Divine Grace in India, Vṛndāvana-dhāma, as that is his real home, where Śrī Kṛṣṇa appeared, and Śrīla Prabhupāda’s own room in Rādhā-Dāmodara temple, where he translated the first three volumes of Canto One, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. It was ecstatic and overwhelming to finally see this, catching such sublime and transcendental mood. Śrīla Prabhupāda looked so natural, sitting peaceful on his *vyāsāsana* or under the *tamāla* tree in the courtyard of Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandira. And his sacred room in Rādhā-Dāmodara Temple was exquisite, where you could really catch the essence of Prabhupāda’s presence. It was a simple, petite room, but so beautiful.”

And I have sat before His Holiness Śrīdhara Mahārāja in his *mandira* in Navadvīpa-dhāma and heard him speak, moved with ecstasy, describing our Śrīla Prabhupāda, his Godbrother.

Śrīdhara Mahārāja: “I would be immensely proud of his activities, and I heard also that many prominent members of the Gauḍīya Mission, they appreciated very much, greatly appreciated. It is a wonderful thing and superhuman that he has done. Success, in such a small span of time. Indeed, it is some divine power works through him only. Then it is possible. Special, divine power worked within him. So much success is otherwise not possible. That is our conception and confidence. Astounding, not only astonishing but astounding. What began, he has pushed it to the greatest extent. Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura also prophesied, also Mahāprabhu , that “In no time the day will come when Western intellectuals will also flock under the banner of Mahāprabhu.” It is Mahāprabhu’s teachings, it is so plain and so grave and so natural that people will recognize the greatness. Prabhupāda Bhaktisiddhānta also sent his disciples several times in that way to the West, but most unexpectedly after his departure, this one disciple became very successful, most gloriously, and we are all proud of that, very much proud. Hare Kṛṣṇa, Gaura Hari.”

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homage, 1978

This year’s Vyāsa-pūjā publication is unlike others in a number of ways. For the first time we are writing about a person who is no longer physically present on this earth. In his *vapu*ḥ form Śrīla Prabhupāda is no more walking with us. But if anything is the main theme, in almost all the homages this year it is the feeling that *we know he is present in his instructions.* It is very encouraging. The very fact that with no hesitation we are producing the Vyāsa-pūjā book just as we did when he was with us in his *vapuḥ* form is encouraging.

But this year is also the first year that we read the full lamentation of the devotees. In former years only a few expressed a dreadful conjecture, “Will Prabhupāda leave us one day?” Now it has happened. This year we find the devotees lamenting his disappearance: Śrīla Prabhupāda, you’ve gone! This feeling-in-separation is union-in-separation – it is transcendental, and nothing to hide or be ashamed of. The *gopīs* cried for Kṛṣṇa with no social consideration. But that crying is the absolute way of being with Him in the most ecstatic mood.

This year a greater sense of responsibility is expressed than ever before … We are not the “children of the society,” as Śrīla Prabhupāda used to call us in 1966: we cannot simply laugh and play, beholding the activities of our Guru Mahārāja as he preaches his world movement before our gleeful eyes. This is another major theme of this year’s entries: *Now that he is gone, we gave a big responsibility.* “Now we must FOLLOW those instructions!” – that is also stressed by the devotees writing this year.

And for the first time we are greeting and worshiping the new *gurus* personally selected by Śrīla Prabhupāda. *His mercy is being perpetuated by the new initiating gurus* – this is also a part of the 1978 Vyāsa-pūjā to Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is proof of his continuance, proof of his glory.

So this book is a publication of love; it needs no other reason to be. It is our homage to Śrīla Prabhupāda. We know we must go on vigorously preaching in this world, but to ease our separation from Śrīla Prabhupāda and to remember him in love, the yearly Vyāsa-pūjā book is a wonderful tradition – and that was given to us by him.

*Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami*

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from the G.B.C.

## Adikesava Svami

***“Being alone and a widow in the forest, the daughter of Vidarbha began to lament, incessantly shedding tears, which soaked her breasts, and crying very loudly. ‘O best of kings, please get up! Get up! Just see this world surrounded by water and infested with rogues and so-called kings. This world is very much afraid, and it is your duty to protect her.’”***

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

It is impossible to guarantee the authenticity of the emotions of a conditioned soul such as myself, as only too recently I was just another beleaguered swimmer in that ocean of faults that is the Kali-yuga. Only by your divine grace could such a sodden, hopeless entity be retrieved from this condition and set on the course of *mahat-sevāṁ dvāram. Māyā*, in the form of delusion, keeps stepping on the path, and although you give adequate warning, I stray from the pure essence of Kṛṣṇa consciousness due to ignorance that is bred from excessive association with those bound for *tamo-dvāram,* the gateway to hell. Therefore, although you warned to the contrary again and again, I must confess to thinking that your physical manifestation would never end. Such a fool as this can only pray in some cheap mimicry of Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu:

*āmi bālaka-sannyāsī—bhāla-manda nāhi jāni*  
*tomāra āśraya niluṇ, guru kari’ māni*

**“I am a young** ***sannyāsī*** **and actually I have no knowledge of what is good and bad. Therefore I am taking shelter of you and accepting you as my spiritual master.”**

Even in describing the dull pain of lamentation, what to speak of the grace of your *vāṇī* or the ecstasy of your *vipralambha,* I must take the guidance of your instructions within the pages of the *Bhāgavatam* in order to take the proper direction and understand what is a genuine articulation and what is sophomoric sentimentality.

To understand the instructions you have given is to know you to some degree, but who can realize their full potency and unlimited scope? What kind of intelligence is it that conceives and executes such a perfect and complete plan that includes every necessary instruction, including how to remember, how to grieve, and how to carry on this mission even as the physical manifestation of such a personality has just disappeared from this mortal world? Certainly no Einstein had such capabilities, yet you not only surpass but supersede all others by acting out the drama as *ācārya* in perfect absolute detail without any momentary compromise or digression into relativity. Although you have given us the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* as a precept to explain the sentiments of ecstatic separation as displayed by Mahāprabhu in his *līlā*, your very life and activities are the transcendental exemplification of how the disciple remains eternally within the presence of his *guru*, taking his order as life and soul. On Viṣvanātha’s advice you took up the instruction of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura and carried it around the world time and time again, then proved the feat even more inexpressibly impossibly by dedicating tremendous energy to prove the same apparent “globe” to be something entirely different anyway.

By that same example you touched the case-hardened hearts of the iron age men and reinstalled Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda within Their rightful place within the temples of their minds. Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura prays *mama mana mandire raha niśi-din*, but you have taken that prayer not only for yourself, but for every living entity. Certainly you were bemused as the people of the world watched with mouths agape, wondering how such an old man could labor on to bring this prayer to fruition. But your smile was always one of compassion, never cynicism born of *ahaṇkāra.*

I have often walked the streets of New York, trying to retrace your wanderings. Although I know you have blessed me by consigning me for life this overpowering monument to your fearlessness, it is difficult not to feel the flutterings of terror when dark, menacing forms scurry into gloomy alleys while blank joyless eyes follow one’s every step. As I pass like a shadow through the degradation of broken windows and shattered lives or step around some slowly dying Bowery remnant of the hippie generation as he pursues unseen but very real chemical demons, I cannot help wondering what kind of courage you possessed to have come down to this hellish East Side world in order to reclaim societal misfits and inaugurate the spiritual movement meant to save the world. Any romantic notions to the contrary, these beginnings were such hellish austerity (and every day we uncover new evidence of its intensity), full of that special cruelty the inhabitants of the material world reserve for the swanlike *paramahaṁsas* who dare to come and save them. Like any ordinary person, you bore the atmosphere of violence and death, accentuated by the heart-shattering cries of sirens and clanging bells, although you did not have to leave Vṛndāvana, and indeed never did so. Which is greater, your love for your Guru Mahārāja or your compassion and mercy upon us (which is born of that love)? Who could possibly say? But both inspire faith and desire to become courageous on the strength of the *guru’s* order within our hearts.

So, naturally we take up the cry of Vidarbhī, which is the preface to this entreaty, as she waits for her absent *svāmī* who has gone and left her behind. She calls as we have called, pleading for grace as you are needed in this world. Without such presence, cataclysm seems imminent and peace seems impossible. It is difficult not to see Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira’s strong fire-vomiting jackals on the horizon, barking at the sun, no longer fearful of your presence. Like him we might palpitate and tremble in fear as the shrieks of owls and crows obscure your sweet chanting.

But then, O most sublime deceiver, we read on as the *brāhmaṇa-*Supersoul who is also yourself instructs us in the doubtless art of remaining eternally in your presence. The difficulty of maintaining past or present tense in describing your glories becomes clear as your *nitya-līlā* becomes more evident. Only failing to execute your order means separation. Fulfillment means eternal association. The difficulty of writing in singular or plural becomes clear, as your presence is eternally established. The choice is there: lament with Purañjani and take the fire, or catch up the instruction, the mission, and save the people of this land (and ourselves with them). Rescued on the threshold of new bewilderments, this wandering *jīva* is grateful for the directions stretching far into the future, stretching on years beyond our mortal lives, illumined by the munificent radiance of Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

So now, like Arjuna freshly returned from Dvārakā, we can become peaceful with the comforting assurance of being eternally with Your Divine Grace. By your artistry the performance has gone on without a misstep. Like a symphony led by the most expert maestro, we have simply begun another part of the piece, barely pausing for breath in between. There are new conductors, duplicating the insurpassable master with ever-increasing accuracy, but emphasizing his presence more and more with every stroke.

O most glorious master, what is this magic you are working? Even in apparent delinquence, your presence is more evident than ever before. You are surcharging the hearts of your disciples with renewed strength and ardent desire to fulfill Kṛṣṇa’s holy order. You are described as *pāragam,* yet you are giving *others* the potency to bring billions of struggling *aṁśas* over to the other side just as you are taking their saviors back. What wonderful mathematics is this that adds one to a handful of the miserable and promises a sum total of unlimited *siddhas*? Seeing these feats and feeling the vibrancy of your *vāṇī*, I realize that you are unto me, like Devahūti’s Kapiladeva, a transcendental eye which can deliver me from the darkness of ignorance and allow me to fall, humbled, in the dust at your feet.

As your ISKCON spreads across the universe, please give me the strength to remain, always marveling at the wonder you have created, and singing your glories.

*aṁśo bhagavato ‘smy ahaṁ sadā dāso ’smi sarvathā*  
*tat-kṛpāpekṣako nityaṁ tat-preṣṭha-sāt karomi svam*

**“I am a tiny part and parcel of the Supreme Lord and I am always and in every way His eternal servant. As I am always hoping for His mercy and kindness, I offer myself to His most dear servitor, my spiritual master.”**

*Your worthless servant,*  
Adi Keśava Svami

GBC – Northeast U.S.A. and Caribbean

## Atreya Rsi Dāsa

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

At your bedside, seeing you humbler than the straw in the street, our envy and offenses were reduced. We became unified in our desperate plea for your health. We felt helplessly out of control by your refusal to respond to our efforts. We learned the difference between love and artificial sentimentality by your acceptance of our devotion and unswerving indifference toward our alloyed shows of affection.

By your purity you made us aware of our well of poisonous offenses. By your courage we understood the meaning of *abhaya caraṇa*. By your grace and mercy you gave us hope, while showing us the enormous gap between our consciousness and the absolute reality.

You are our eternal teacher and master. Through the *līlā* of your disappearance you are so much more with us every day. I humbly bow to your lotus feet again and again.

*Your servant,*  
Atreya Ṛṣi dāsa

GBC – Middle East

## Balavanta Dāsa

Born to Vaiṣṇavas so clean and pure  
Destined to carry God’s message certain and sure  
His mission was there from the very beginning  
But few could then see its true hidden meaning.

His father realized the son would be great  
And local astrologers foretold his fate  
Temples would open by the touch of his hand  
The world would be rescued by his command.

Eternal servant of Sarasvatī Ṭhākura  
He came on his order to this distant shore  
Preaching Gaurāṅga’s message in the fallen West  
With all his devotion he soared past all tests.

He chanted and lectured on New York’s East Side  
He became our guru, our father, our guide  
To make Vaiṣṇavas of us was his desire  
He thought “Kṛṣṇa can help them; we’ll see what transpires.”

This impossible task he took as his mission  
Serving his guru devoid of conditions  
To hell he would go if that was his duty  
Just marvel the heart of God’s pure devotee

His character was pure; his message was sweet  
Thousands soon fell at his lotus feet  
His arms were held high; his merciful glance  
Brought tears to our eyes and caused us to dance.

The Lord’s holy name was soon on our lips  
“Stop meat-eating, gambling, and LSD trips,  
Stop illicit sex, the four pillars of sin  
Now, God’s sublime service you’re fit to begin.”

He gave us our dhotīs, we shaved our heads clean  
He fed us prasāda, this was certainly life’s cream  
He taught us to worship with love and respect  
The Lord’s Deity form, we must never neglect.

Kīrtana soon echoed from one coast to another  
Kṛṣṇa’s our father, Harā is our mother  
This holy vibration was piercing the night  
The moon began rising, Prabhupāda was the light.

He printed his books, astounding the scholars  
As soon as we sold them, in flowed the dollars  
Distribute his books, protect the Deity  
Just follow his order, God grants all facility.

His next plan was India, holiest of places  
His vision was temples; we saw open spaces  
Alone he determined; this work should be done  
He fought and he conquered; his victory was won.

Big towers are standing in Bombay, in pair  
And everyone’s happy except Mr. Nair  
Prabhupāda’s desire cannot be frustrated  
His service to Kṛṣṇa expands unabated.

He gave this world Kṛṣṇa, the ultimate good  
He showed us the way of real brotherhood  
There’s none greater than he; no one as sublime  
Throughout all of history, throughout all of time.

We bow at his feet and call out his name  
O Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda, you are the same  
The same kind as Kṛṣṇa, in quality one  
Just like the fire is the same as the sun.

We’ve no other hope than your merciful glance  
We’ve no other shelter; we’ve no other chance  
In all the three worlds there’s nothing but thee  
Thy service, thy love, through eternity.

We beg to remain your servants so true  
Through birth after birth if that is our due  
There’s no room in our lives for surrender to others  
You have captured our hearts; our eternal father.

Your teachings must now be our life and our soul  
We are all tiny parts and not the supreme whole  
Only if we keep this thy divine command  
Will your separation be possible for us to withstand.

Balavanta dāsa adhikārī

GBC – Southern U.S.A.

## Bhagavan Maharaja

By your mercy you left Vṛndāvana-dhāma  
To bring the medicine of hari-nāma.

You came to the West to set us free,  
Your Divine Grace was filled with kṛṣṇa-śakti.

Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee, like the honeybee,  
Gave us the sweet taste of kṛṣṇa-bhakti.

The nondevotee’s night, the devotee’s day,  
You gave us – paraṁ dṛṣṭvā nivartate.

By the ignorance of science the world would rot,  
Not knowing the difference between sat and asat.

But by your grace the world has seen  
The Supreme Person Kṛṣṇa, the parāṁ gatim.

Thus on your command we submit to you,  
Though we’re most unqualified to become guru.

You have predicted, as time goes on,  
The world-felt victory of nāma-saṅkīrtana.

Joining together, your dream will come true  
Of a monumental temple for Mahāprabhu.

We have no fear of this world we are in,  
For you still guide us from Goloka Vṛndāvana.

We pray by your grace, that the time will come,  
To once more touch your lotus feet – in Kṛṣṇa’s dhāma.

But even in your leaving us, we feel you not far,  
Our hearts are always with you in vipralambha.

Bhagāvan dāsa  
Initiating *guru*  
GBC – Southern Europe, Israel  
BBT Trustee

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

Appreciation and homage to His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda on the auspicious anniversary of his appearance in this mortal world.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmīn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Personally I have no qualification to offer devotional writings in praise of Your Divine Grace. Please bestow your causeless mercy upon this fallen soul so that this body may become an instrument in the service of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

I remember you always kept me engaged in unalloyed devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, therefore you are known to be a direct manifestation of the Lord, and genuine representative of the disciplic succession, and *ācāryadeva* of the whole world. In his prayers to the spiritual master, Śrila Viṣvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura has confirmed that all the revealed scriptures accept the spiritual master to be identical with the Personality of Godhead because he is a very dear and confidential servant of the Lord. Therefore we Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas worship Your Divine Grace in the light of your being the servitor of the Personality of Godhead. Also, according to all the ancient literatures of devotional service and other unalloyed Vaiṣṇavas, the spiritual master is always considered one of the confidential associates. I offer my respectful obeisances unto Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, who has arisen on the horizon of West Bengal on this most eventful day to dissipate the darkness of ignorance and thus wonderfully bestow benediction upon all of us, being compassionate for the fallen state of the world. As the sun drives away darkness by its appearance, similarly you have dissipated the darkness of the living beings and enlightened everyone with knowledge of the Absolute Truth. You have shown us the easiest path of liberation in this age of Kali by giving the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*. You always said, “Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy.”

O my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your causeless mercy my illusion is now gone, and I have regained my memory and am now firm and free from doubts and am prepared to act according to your divine instructions. I know if you are satisfied, we are satisfied. You were so kind that you blessed us all by saying, “Please chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and follow the regulative principles.” Now if we want to become God conscious and realize our own essence, we only have to understand God in truth, how He appears, how He disappears, and what His functions are. Thus we can become eligible to enter into the kingdom of God. For this we find no one except Your Divine Grace, a real *mahātmā*, who is actually situated in knowledge. This material world is just like a prison house. It is a punishing place meant to bring us to that point of becoming disgusted, surrendering at last to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and going back to home, to our original nature of eternal life of bliss and complete knowledge.

On this holy event of Vyāsa-pūjā day, I was supposed to make an offering unto your lotus feet, but I am coming to beg your mercy, and please pardon me for all the offenses I have committed by not following your instructions. I have heard through your holy lips the most powerful unchanged message of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa: *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is.* And Caitanyadeva said,

*bhārata-bhūmite haila manuṣya-janma yāra*  
*janma sārthaka kari' kara para-upakāra*  
*\[Cc. Ādi 9.41\]*

**"One who has taken his birth as a human being in the land of India \[Bhārata-varṣa\] should make his life successful and work for the benefit of all other people.**

Please give me strength to remain pure and allow me to assist you in your mission of spreading *yāre dekha, tāre kaha kṛṣṇa-upadeśa* all over the world.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. *Koṭi koṭi daṇḍavats* to Your Divine Grace.

*Begging to remain your servant forever,*  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami  
GBC – New Delhi, Northern India

## Bhavānanda Goswami

I offer my most prostrate obeisances unto the lotus feet of my beloved spiritual preceptor, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and pray to Lord Kṛṣṇa that at any hour of the day or night I may be found gathering up the dust of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lotus feet in a mood of loving devotion. Actually that is the proper mood of the disciple in his relationship with his spiritual master. Kṛṣṇa and Kṛṣṇa’s devotee are one. Just like the viceroy of India and the king of England. As the representative of the king, the viceroy was considered to have all the potencies of the king. He was empowered by the king to accept all offerings and tributes which were given by the subjects of India. When they offered a gift to the viceroy, it was considered offered to the king. Similarly Kṛṣṇa’s representative, Śrīla Prabhupāda, when we offer our service, wretched as it may be, unto his lotus feet, when he accepts it, it is understood he is accepting it for Kṛṣṇa. He is empowered by Kṛṣṇa. The same mood with which we make an offering to Kṛṣṇa should also be there in an offering to Śrīla Prabhupāda:

*teṣām satata-yuktānaṁ bhajatāṁ prīti-pūrvakam*  
*dadāmi buddhi-yogaṁ taṁ yena mām upayānti te*

**“To those who are constantly devoted and worship Me with love, I give them the understanding by which they can come to Me.” (Bg. 10.10)**

That same instruction in *Gīta* of Kṛṣṇa holds true for Śrīla Prabhupāda. Those of us, his disciples who can develop a constant flow of devotional service with a loving attitude offered at his divine lotus feet, to those disciples, Śrīla Prabhupāda from within their hearts will give the knowledge by which they can come to him. Actually Śrīla Prabhupāda is already with each of us. That is the beauty of transcendental life, that it is not bound in or limited by any mundane conception of time and space, but the relationship between Śrīla Prabhupāda and his disciples is free from any inebrities or mundane conceptions. As we lend our minds to the spirit of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions, immediately his presence can be felt.

This Vyāsa-pūjā time is the opportunity for all of us to glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda and his activities, and the best representation of Śrīla Prabhupāda is that we follow his instructions, that we become pure devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda once said you can tell the *guru* by his disciples. So if we want Śrīla Prabhupāda to be actually, truly honored all over the world, then it is our duty, our heartfelt obligation, to become one-hundred-percent pure, and in this way Śrīla Prabhupāda’s potency, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s love, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions will be felt all over the world, and the entire world will make great strides toward returning back home, back to Kṛṣṇa, back to the lotus feet of His Divine Grace.

Bhavānanda Goswami

Initiating *guru*; GBC – Māyāpur

## Brahmānanda Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my humble *daṇḍavats* at your holy lotus feet. As you must know, since your departure I have many times composed in my mind my offering for Vyāsa-pūjā, as soon as I think of Your Divine Grace, then I want to glorify you suitably. So, although this is my written Vyāsa-pūjā offering, in my heart I will always go on composing Vyāsa-pūjā offerings. It is my good fortune that my offering is being included in the Vyāsa-pūjā book, which contains offerings by so many of your disciples who act on what they write, who have realized what you have taught, and who follow your instructions. This is the difference between my offering and theirs. If my offering was not included in the Vyāsa-pūjā book, there would be no loss. In fact, I should make my words as short as possible so that you can relish their sentiments rather than being distracted by my official proclamations.

Right from the very beginning, from my first meeting with you, my greatest pleasure was to serve you as a servant. First I offered you my savings and wages, and you have kindly said that your financial difficulties ended when I joined. Together we would walk around Second Avenue looking for a building to buy, and sometimes we went on the bus and even the subway. In the temple I would bring your shoes and kneel down to tuck in your lotus feet, and in your room I would pour you a glass of water to drink while you tirelessly preached to us. These memories make me feel greatly for those intimate days of eternal sweetness, when you changed me from a dog into a devotee.

Unfortunately, deep in my heart I cling to doggish desires. Why is this? You did you level best to purify me – short of force. Now I have to decide to surrender my mind and heart completely. I don’t know if I can actually do this in this lifetime. If I fail, then I have wasted my entire life, because the meaning of a human life is to surrender wholly to Kṛṣṇa and His pure representative.

In your presence I always marveled at how perfectly and purely you performed even trivial tasks. When you used to write your signature on a paper or letter, it was a timeless moment of artistic beauty performed with such appropriate care. And what to speak of your writing a masterpiece, the *Kṛṣṇa* book, certainly the greatest book in all literature. In Boston we used to hear the dictaphone tapes sent from Los Angeles of you speaking all the wonderful descriptions of Kṛṣṇa in Vṛndāvana while in the background we could hear the chirping of the busy morning birds while you worked. In this way you kindly soothed our beastly minds.

Now you have done another wonderful thing. So gracefully and humbly did you leave this world. Just in the same humble manner in which you appeared among us alienated youths of 1960’s America. You have gone back to Kṛṣṇa’s abode, but at the same time you didn’t forget us conditioned souls.

*vāñchā-kalpa-tarubhyaś ca kṛpā-sindhubhya eva ca*  
*patitānāṁ pāvanebhyo vaiṣṇavebhyo namo namaḥ*

You empowered your foremost disciples into spiritual *ācāryas*, your representatives, who can bring us back to Kṛṣṇa just as you were doing. You have manifested yourself in them. This is the most wonderful thing you have done. So now there is another chance for me. Unfortunately, while you were present I did not completely purify myself as all your other many disciples did. I have not qualified for leaving the material world. But, now I can try again. How? By serving the lotus feet of your representatives. Just as I was always eager to serve Your Divine Grace as a servant to his master or as a dutiful son to his father, so now I aspire to serve your eleven spiritual heirs as the elder brother who, although unfortunate himself, wants to help the qualified younger brothers to succeed for the benefit of the family as a whole. I only hope they will give me the opportunity, otherwise without having anybody to serve I am lost. A dog can only be happy when he has a proper master to serve. Only be associating intimately with the initiating *ācāryas* will I too learn to serve you sincerely. I have been with you, but they have heard from you. That is the important difference (*yathā jñāsyasi tac chṛṇu* \[*Bg*. 7.1\]*).* Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for saving me, such an unfortunate soul.

There is no question of you having left. I simply have to remember you with love and devotion or even to read one sentence from your many books, and you are there guiding me on the proper path. And thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for even giving me a life’s mission, spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in Africa. It is blissfully very difficult, just the proper challenge to my capacity. Actually, I may not be able to do it properly in one lifetime, so I am prepared to take birth as long as necessary just for this purpose, either in a Western body or even in an African body if it is best for fulfilling your mission. Africa is so big, so many people, languages, countries and customs, all so different. Even to penetrate the continent by land is difficult, so I am only praying to Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His representatives you have created to kindly assist me in this matter. If they help me, then I think it will be possible to do something even in such a place.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Brahmānanda Swami, GBC – Africa

## Gopāla Kṛṣṇa dāsa

Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā offering at the divine lotus feet of my spiritual master, His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

The *śāstras* say that the genuine spiritual master is always present – either in his personal, or *vapuḥ* form, or in his *vāṇī*, or instruction form. Though you physically left this planet on November 14, 1977, you are still with us and will continue to be with us in your *vāṇī* form.

You appeared in 1896 in response to Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda’s prayer that a powerful personality appear to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness on this planet. Your appearance is similar to Lord Caitanya’s appearance in response to Advaita ācārya’s prayer and Lord Kṛṣṇa’s appearance at the request of the demigods. In the *Gītā,* the Supreme Lord declares that He either appears Himself or sends His representative to establish the principles of religion. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were an empowered incarnation –*śaktyāveśa-avatāra*– just like Śrīla Vyāsadeva, Śrī Śukadeva Gosvāmī, Śrī Nāradji. You appeared to fulfill Lord Caitanya’s prediction that the holy name would one day spread all over the world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were a *guru* in the real sense. You were successful in your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over this world. In a brief span of twelve years, you opened over one hundred temples; you wrote over seventy books, which have been translated in twenty-eight languages and are being used in thousands of institutions; you gave thousands of interviews and illustrious lectures; and you traveled all over the world fourteen times. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your achievements are so great that no one will ever be able to adequately describe them.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were a real *ācārya*, one who teaches by example. Anyone who saw you knew at once that he was seeing a pure devotee of God. You repeatedly reminded the suffering humanity that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. You were bold in your preaching. You did not compromise your *guru’s* instructions. You presented the Supreme Lord’s teachings “as it is”. This is your glory, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The most important science is to know how to get out of the clutches of material existence. Anyone who knows this science is the greatest person. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were one who knew this science**—**a *parama-vidvān***—**the most learned scholar. Pure devotees help the Supreme Personality of Godhead in His mission, and such devotees appear by the will of the Lord.

By your example, you showed your disciples that preaching is the essence. Even when you were physically weak, you would spend hours and hours talking to your scientist disciples, explaining how they could defeat the atheist scientists. You never lost an opportunity to talk of Kṛṣṇa. You knew the real remedy to the problems of materialistic life. Even is someone had no attraction to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, somehow you would succeed in engaging him in the service of Kṛṣṇa or make him favorable to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This was your greatness, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were the crown jewel of this world. Without you, this world is now bereft of its valuable jewel. So while it is natural for us to be sorrowful, we know that you will always live in “sound”, or via your divine instructions. Commenting on the passing of the great devotee Haridāsa Ṭhākura, Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda wrote: “He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die, when thou art living still in sound! The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try to spread the holy name around.”

You dedicated Part I of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Canto I, to your spiritual master by saying: “He lives forever by his divine instructions and the follower lives with him.” On this auspicious day, I beg to pray for your mercy so that I may continue to live with your divine instructions. Jaya, Śrīla Prabhupāda! 108 glories to the wandering mendicant and topmost swanlike devotee, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Gopāla Kṛṣṇa dāsa

GBC – Canada, China  
BBT Trustee

## Gurukrpā Swami

Prabhupāda, let me put my head on your two lotus feet  
The whole world is running mad after a treat  
But all their attempts will only meet defeat  
Until they take shelter of your two lotus feet.

Prabhupāda, your lotus feet have walked all over the earth  
Establishing temples, and recovering the simple  
Dishing out the sauce, that Kṛṣṇa’s the boss  
Teaching what we’ve lost, that we’re all kṛṣṇa-dāsa.

For so many crores, I’ve bee māyā’s clown  
Now I’m throwing myself, down on the ground  
I'm lowly as a snake, in a dark lake  
Please! I need your protection from māyā’s infection.

I'm burning in anguish in this blazing saṁsāra  
When will I tire and put out this fire  
I keep going on, chewing the chewed  
When will I give up this enjoying mood?

Service is the name, and surrender is the game  
Devotional service is always there – when will I care?  
Taking seriously your instructions and stand like a man  
And give all fallen souls a helping hand.

I'm sure you must get tired of this fool hanging around  
The way I walk, the way I talk, just like a proud peacock  
I'm the biggest rascal of all, I'm not on the ball  
But you, Prabhupāda, to me, are the greatest of all.

I love you Prabhupāda  
And I pray on this Vyāsa-pūjā day!  
Give the shade of your sweet lotus feet  
Don’t kick me away, for I am unworthy and insignificant.

Gurukrpā Swami

GBC – Vṛndāvana, Hawaii, Far East

## Haṁsadūta Swami

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. For reasons of your own, you chose to bestow all your blessings on this poor soul and depart from sight. Whatever you have established you left in the hands of your faithful and confidential disciples. At least officially, I am also counted as one of them. This is a great wonder to me.

Śrīla Nārada Muni got the chance to see the Lord face to face, but as suddenly as the Lord appeared before Nārada, again He disappeared, thus leaving Nārada heartbroken in a vacant world. Out of your causeless mercy Your Divine Grace appeared among us and forced us to open our eyes by the power of your spiritual knowledge. No sooner were our eyes getting the glimpse of your lotus feet, when you vanished, leaving us heartbroken and vacant in this material world. This is a great tragedy, at the same time Your Divine Grace is present in your devotees and books, and thus your mercy is being carried in every nook and corner of the world by your pure devotees. Therefore you are simultaneously absent and present in the world; thus it is tragedy and bliss together, and we have somehow survived your disappearance.

In a short time you gathered your devotees from all corners of the world under the ISKCON banner. It was not a luck-chance happening, but a deliberate transcendental reunion, a society of pure devotees organized to bring transcendental knowledge to this dark forgotten world. We are your eternal devotees, and in your absence we are left behind here, working to continue what you started only twelve years ago, namely a Kṛṣṇa conscious world. However, as Your Divine Grace showed us by example how to give up the material body, we will also follow and give up the material body. At that time, because we are your surrendered servants, we will immediately go to you, and again you will attract our minds and hearts to you, and thus we will be meeting and separating again and again. I do not know what I am, except that I am your devotee and follower life after life. Please accept me like that; don’t let me stray away.

Haṁsadūta Swami

Initiating *guru*  
GBC – Northwest U.S.A., Tamil Nadu, Śrī Lanka, Hyderabad  
BBT Trustee

## Harikeśa Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances prostrated in the dust of your lotus feet. All glory to you, who are the perfect representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You are the most perfect servitor of the Lord. In fact, you are the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead, and your every motion is dedicated to fulfilling the desires of the Supreme Lord. You have no desire independent of the Lord, but rather your every thought, word, and deed is directed solely and wholly to causing the mission of Lord Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu to be spread all over the world.

Someone might find it strange that I am writing this letter to you, for you have “departed” and “left” this world of men and matter. But how can I consider that you are no longer here and cannot read my letter? In fact (dare I say it?), you are more present now than before. In fact Your Lordship has expanded yourself far beyond the capacities of any normal *yogī* who has achieved the perfection of expanding himself into eight forms (although each of these forms must merely duplicate the actions of the other seven). You are presently chastising, correcting, instructing, inspiring and associating with me within my heart. (Am I merely sentimentally babbling? Have I gone mad? Am I a *sahajiyāi*?) You are sitting in my heart and you refuse to let me go (I am not sure if I may say this with such confidence, but I also refuse to let you leave me).

If you remain there, you will not have to suffer any inconvenience. I have arranged for a first-class *vyāsāsana* for you, with very soft white velvet and satin cushions with gold borders. It is deep green onyx with a golden streak and curved artistically on a twelve-pointed lotus. The breezes, quite warm and pleasing, are filled with the fresh scent of *pārijāta*, occasionally mixing with the *campaka.* In your golden glass (the larger one, with Kṛṣṇa and Rādhā on the swing carved in relief) resting under the marble and teak table, you will find fresh dob juice, which you may drink at your convenience through the silver straw. The flower garland is of newly grown red and white roses, and some of your favorite Hawaiian magnolias are arranged on the desk, making the whole atmosphere fragrant and pleasing to see.

Lunch will be ready right after your massage and bath (the pure sandalwood oil, very cool and soothing on the head), and I have prepared some special *kacorīs* (with mint chutney), some *śukta* and *śāk* (with potatoes), just as Piśimā taught me.

Please come and sit in the garden. The aroma of camphor is purifying the atmosphere, which is alive with the sound of the peacocks gently cooing as they tiptoe their way through the grass. Sometimes they prance in the forest, searching for a mate, and their loud cries and expanded tail fans combine together tastefully with the sound of the *cataka* birds flying high, close to the clouds, looking to drink their water, although the cloud will not give rain. (they will never accept the water which has touched the ground, although the cloud may not give rain but thunder, just as a devotee will never accept any offerings from *māyā*.)

The *camara* is long and full, with an ornately carved silver handle. (I can fan you for hours; it is my favorite service). Here in my garden there is no disturbance from the sun to cause glare in your eyes, as the *campaka* and pipal trees intertwine to form an umbrella over your head. There are no disturbing insects; only the butterflies softly fluttering from one bush to the other and the bees humming as they collect the pollen from flower to flower in their daily routine. Perhaps it is your desire that I sing for you with *mṛdaṅga*  and *karatālas* the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* with your favorite Bengali melodies?

I hope that you will not be disturbed with the constant stream of precious gifts which I am bringing to you on a silver tray. (I am offering everything to you; please accept it.) in the golden dish, lined with deep green jade, is a freshly ground sandalwood pulp mixed with camphor, saffron, and rose water, a year old, which was made from the finest red roses. Your afternoon garland is made from the finest yellow roses without any stems, points, or gaps, and they are as light as a feather. I think the fragrance will fill the forest, invoking the envy of the lotuses, which are swaying as if offering you their obeisances again and again from their positions in the nearby lake.

Here is a picture of your Guru Mahārāja, garlanded with flowers strung between pearls, and please allow me to place on your lotus hand this precious ring of gold and sapphire. In this white and blue coral box supported by silver Nṛsiṁhadeva lotus feet there are many varieties of two- and three-layered *sandeśa*, some of it first-class dry and some first-class soft, just like we had on the plane when we returned to Bombay from Nairobi. The *rasagullās* are in a crystal jar under your desk, and whenever you want me to serve you, please ring the bell (it will hit the fifth note).

I have also arranged your bed under the trees. The mattress is made from soft leaves and grasses covered by slightly scented flowers. The sheets are the softest silk and velvet, and I have made the pillow just right to hold your lotus head in perfect comfort. You may sleep without any fears as I will stand guard and not allow anything to disturb you, and you may think of your service to Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa constantly. Now and then in your ecstasy of love you might glance upon me sidelong, but as I am so insignificant I shall not disturb you, but if I do, my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, if I do, please, please, beat me severely. I shall never do it again.You are my intelligence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and you have come just to give me strength.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Submitted by your eternal servant,*  
Harikeśa Swami

Initiating *guru*  
GBC – Germany, Scandinavia, Switzerland, Austria, Eastern Europe, Lebanon  
BBT Trustee.

## Hari Śauri dāsa vanacārī

*oṁ ajṅāna-timirāndhasya jṅanaṅjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most revered and auspicious day, we offer our humblest obeisances at the lotus feet of our beloved spiritual master, eternal father, friend, guide and ever well-wisher, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda. The lotus feet of our spiritual master offer all benediction and complete shelter to all who sincerely approach and serve them, and they are a source of all benedictions within this world. Those great souls who are fortunate enough to have taken the petal-like soft soles of Śrīla Prabhupāda upon their heads are certainly the most intelligent among men, and the supreme goal of life, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, is easily obtainable for such *mahātmās.*

The spiritual master is that person who can deliver the fallen souls from the repeated cycle of birth and death. Within the material sphere of activities, no one can be considered fortunate or well off. The result of taking any kind of material body is frustration and misery due to the *janma, mṛtyu, jarā, vyādhi \[*Bg. 13.9\]*.* No one can hope to escape these four factors of material existence, as long as he remains in the illusion of identifying his self to be his body. He may be learned, he may be rich, he may be strong, famous or socially well situated, but actually none of these are his natural state of being. No sooner are they obtained than again they disappear, like a will-o’-the-wisp, and therefore any “happiness” one may experience here is simply ephemeral and a shadow of reality. Being bound up by the result of his activities, every living being is forced to act in a certain way according to the modes of material nature he has contacted, and due to his association with such modes he goes up and down throughout the universe. Being always bewildered and illusioned, the foolish living being struggles uselessly to establish himself by means of family, friends, society, love, etc. Although he should be experienced enough to see that such endeavor is always ultimately defeated, still, due to his being in the darkest ignorance, he blindly continues on and never finds the happiness he so eagerly desires. Just as an ignorant animal, seeking water in the desert, runs further into the desert upon seeing a mirage and ultimately meets his death, so the materially illusioned person becomes more and more enmeshed in bodily activities. The more he tries to enjoy his body and its extensions by way of wife, children, home, community, nation, race, etc., the more entangled he becomes, like a fly which struggles to free itself from the spider’s web but simply succeeds in attracting his captor and thus guarantees his death. For such persons, escape from the clutches of material energy is not possible except by one means. Only if one who is already liberated comes and unties the hard knot of *anhaṅkāra*, false identification, can such bound-up living beings get free from the suffering conditions. If by good fortune one gets the fortune of a liberated soul, then immediately his good fortune begins.

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.2.15-16) nicely describes this:

*yad-anydhāsinā yuktāḥ karma-granthi-nibandhanam*  
*chindanti kovidās tasya ko na kuryāt kathā-ratim*  
*śuśrūṣoḥ śraddhadhānasya vāsudeva-kathā-ruciḥ*  
*syān mahat-sevayā viprāḥ puṇya-tīrtha-niṣvaṇāt*

**“With sword in hand, intelligent men cut through the labyrinthine knots of reactionary work \[*****karma*****\] by remembering the Lord. Therefore who will not pay attention to His messages?**  
**“O twice-born sages, by serving those devotees who are completely freed from all vice, great service is done. By such service one gains an affinity for hearing the messages of Vāsudeva.”**

One who sincerely desires to be freed from the sufferings of material existence is intelligent.
