That person who understands that the material world is *duḥkhālayam aśāśvatam*, a place of repeated misery, who concludes that happiness is not possible in relationship to his body and who therefore seeks to find some alternative to that condition, becomes eligible to hear from the pure devotee the messages concerning the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and His paraphernalia, pastimes, entourage, etc. The Supreme Personality of Godhead is not like the ordinary living being, as is misconceived by envious men. Being purely spiritual in His existence and possessing the six opulences of strength, wealth, beauty, fame, wisdom and renunciation, in full, to an unlimited degree, the Lord is never subjected to the influence of the three modes of material nature – goodness, passion and ignorance – which comprise His external, separated energy. Neither can He be considered equal to the living beings who, although spiritual, like Hari, are subjected to higher authority. This is explained in *Bhagavad-gītā,* Seventh Chapter, verses 4, 5 and 6.

*bhūmir āpo 'nalo vāyuḥ*  
*khaṁ mano buddhir eva ca*  
*ahaṅkāra itīyaṁ me*  
*bhinnā prakṛtir aṣṭadhā*

*apareyam itas tv anyāṁ*  
*prakṛtiṁ viddhi me parām*  
*jīva-bhūtāṁ mahā-bāho*  
*yayedaṁ dhāryate jagat*

*etad yonīni bhūtāni*  
*sarvāṇīty upadhāraya*  
*ahaṁ kṛtsnasya jagataḥ*  
*prabhavaḥ pralayas tathā*

**“Earth, water, fire, air, ether, mind, intelligence and false ego – altogether these eight comprise My separated material energies. Besides this inferior nature, O might-armed Arjuna, there is a superior energy of Mine, which are all living entities who are struggling with material nature and are sustaining the universe. Of all that is material and all that is spiritual in this world, know for certain that I am both its origin and dissolution.”**

Therefore, simply by hearing about the Supreme Lord and His glorious activities, the fallen illusioned soul revives his memory of his relationship with the Lord and cuts off his false identification with the temporary manifestations of material nature. As his remembrance increases, he becomes fixed in service to the Lord and gradually tastes the sweetness of a permanent, blissful, deepening relationship, which is never broken or lost under any circumstances, due to its transcendental nature. Thus, ultimately he attains the highest goal of a pure loving relationship in the absolute world, where birth, death, old age and disease are only noticeable by their absence. The Lord therefore appears within this world in the form of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā,* and other transcendental literatures which are nondifferent from Himself, just to give the fallen souls the opportunity to associate with Him and become respiritualized, be reviving their consciousness of Him. Thus such literatures are highly prized and renowned throughout human society because they present the real goal of one’s existence, which the human form specifically enables one to attain. Anyone who is learned in expounding them for the benefit of others becomes equally famous. Therefore it is not surprising that there are hundreds of thousands of translations of the *Vedas, Gītā,* etc., because due to the cheating propensity which originally puts the infinitesimal living being here in the material world, many unscrupulous rascals try to utilize the Lord and His property to attempt to glorify themselves as being supreme. Because *Bhagavad-gītā* is very respected and glorified, such fools and rascals think, “Let me present my interpretation of this philosophy, and in this way I shall become a big man with fame and following.” In this way, although the *Gītā* is meant for his liberation, due to his inimical attitude to the Lord and his desire to Lord it over, the *asura, mūḍha*, tries to use it for furthering his material situation, and all benefit he could have gained is lost. Rather than being elevated by his association with the Lord, he becomes more and more degraded, and those who are unfortunate enough to hear the messages of the Supreme Transcendence from the lips of such demons also miss the chance to go back home, back to Godhead. One should never hear such transcendental subject matter from such inimical nondevotees. Therefore Śrī Sūta Gosvāmī clarifies how proper hearing and remembrance of the Lord comes about.

He explains that it is only by hearing from someone who is a devotee of the Lord that one can properly understand His transcendental nature and thus enter into His service. A devotee of the Lord never tries to usurp the Lord’s position by declaring himself to be God, neither does he try to take advantage of the Lord’s fame for his own advantage. Rather, being a most confidential servitor of the Lord, a pure devotee simply presents the truth of the Lord’s existence and the living beings’ subordinate relationship with Him. Being empowered by the Lord to act as his external manifestation, the pure devotee knows perfectly how to act according to every time, place and circumstance, just for the benefit of the conditioned souls. The Lord Himself is perfectly competent to free the conditioned souls and is so kind that He expands Himself in His localized feature of Paramātma within the heart of the living being.

*īśvaraḥ sarva-bhūtānāṁ hṛd-deśe ‘rjuna tiṣṭhati*  
*bhrāmayan sarva-bhūtāni yantrārūḍhāni māyayā*

**“The Supreme Lord is situated in everyone’s heart, O Arjuna, and is directing the wanderings of all living entities, who are seated as on a machine, made of the material energy.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **18.61)**

Traveling with him throughout the material world, He waits for the opportunity to instruct him when he begins to make serious inquiry. He constantly tries to instruct the *jīvātmā*, but due to his fallen, illusioned condition, the living entity misinterprets the Lord’s instructions. Therefore the Lord manifests Himself externally as the *guru* and comes in a form which is more easily appreciable by the conditioned souls. He empowers His close associates to represent Him, and such *mahātmās*, being able to manifest the full potency of the Lord, are therefore to be worshiped as God, because they are as good as the Lord Himself and are perfectly competent to deliver anyone and everyone from the clutches of *māyā.* simply by their speaking, the full glory of the Lord is manifest, and the intelligent living beings can take advantage of the instructions of such liberated souls for their own welfare. By simply engaging in the service of the pure devotees and hearing with all submission and attention, the candidate becomes enlightened and freed from the modes of ignorance and passion. As he hears about the Lord, his heart becomes purified and washed of all material contamination. Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu glorifies this hearing and chanting in the following way: “Glory to the Śrī Kṛṣṇa *saṅkīrtana*, which cleanses the heart of all the dust accumulated for years and extinguishes the fire of conditional life, of repeated birth and death. This *saṅkīrtana* movement is the prime benediction for humanity at large because it spreads the rays of the benediction moon. It is the life of all transcendental knowledge. It increases the ocean of transcendental bliss, and it enables us to fully taste the nectar for which we are always anxious.” (*Śikṣāṣṭaka* 1)

He says, “All glories to the *saṅkīrtana* movement,” and exclaims that each name of the Lord is as powerful as the Lord Himself, *adhikārī* by constant contact with the Lord in this way the heart becomes completely cleansed. However, the fallen soul has no taste for hearing and chanting the holy names, and therefore the pure devotee acts to show how that taste is developed by his own perfect example. Such *ācāryas* enable us to advance in spiritual life by giving the opportunity to us to associate with them. It is generally said that man is known by his association. “Birds of a feather flock together.” This is explained in the *Gītā* (13.22), *kāraṇaṁ guṇa-saṅgo ‘sya sad-asad-yoni-janmasu:* “Due to his association with material nature, he meets with good and evil in the different species.”

One who wishes to become a doctor has to associate with one who is already qualified as a doctor, and similarly, one who wishes to become liberated from the clutches of *māyā* must associate with the devotees of the Lord. Caitanya Mahāprabhu confirms this:

*mahat-kṛpā vinā kona karme ‘bhakti’ naya*  
*kṛṣṇa-bhakti dūre rahu, saṁsāra nahe kṣaya*

**“Unless one is favored by a pure devotee of the Lord, he cannot attain the platform of devotional service. To say nothing of** ***kṛṣṇa-bhakti*****, one cannot even be relieved from the bondage of material existence.” (Cc.** ***Madhya-līlā*** **22.51)**

So liberation means to be engaged in the Lord’s loving service. If one wishes to leave the material universe, he must have somewhere to go, somewhere to take shelter. This shelter cannot be offered by anyone except a devotee because no one else has sufficient knowledge of the Lord’s spiritual existence, other than the pure devotees. The *karmīs*, *jñānīs* and *yogīs* have only partial knowledge of the Lord’s expansions and energies, but the devotees know the Lord in full. They are situated on the highest platform of spiritual life and are perfectly competent to enlighten everyone of the mysteries of spiritual life. Such pure devotees are very rarely found within this world. It is only by the greatest good fortune that the fallen souls get the chance to associate with a pure devotee.

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

**“According to their** ***karma*****, all living entities are wandering throughout the universe. Some are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Kṛṣṇa. By the mercy of both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of devotional service.” (Cc.** ***Madhya-līlā*** **19.151)**

Especially in Kali-yuga, the present age of dissent, when everyone is born less than *śūdra*, the lowest class of men are described by Śaunaka Ṛṣi as *mandāḥ sumanda-matayo manda-bhāgyā hy upadrutāḥ \[*SB 1.1.10\]*:* lazy, misguided, unlucky and always disturbed. It is especially, then, the most wonderful benediction that by some inconceivable good fortune we have met up with such a munificent, wonderfully glorious personality as our spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is only due to his mercy that we have now the opportunity to try to render some little service to the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and have the opportunity to go back home, back to Godhead. Had he not come here, then we would have continued to struggle in the darkness of ignorance for crores of lifetimes without any hope of respite, and our fate would be unknown. Who amongst us can estimate or appreciate the significance of this most auspicious day, when our Lord and master, His Divine Grace, made his appearance in this dark place. Certainly it is not possible for us to properly grasp the importance of this day, but in writing this humble and insignificant offering we hope to remind ourselves of the glorious nature of our Guru Mahārāja and our own complete dependence upon him, and we earnestly pray to Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa that we may ever be engaged in the worship and service of the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda. Of course, it is not now possible to be able to massage his head, hands, body and lotus feet with oil and look to his personal needs, nor bathe in the nectar of his personal association; but despite this, our attachment grows stronger because remembrance of his *vāṇī*, words, and execution of his instructions gives us his personal association in a different way, and his presence is always felt.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day, therefore, let us remember the lotus feet of our spiritual master with full attention and absorption and be always gratefully engaged in helping each other to spread his wonderful mission. In this way we can make our small attempt to give him pleasure, as he has so kindly given us.

108 glories to the great preceptor and topmost swanlike devotee of the Lord, His Divine Grace Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Goswami Mahārāja Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*From your most unqualified, undeserving, worthless, insincere, rascal menial servant,*  
Hari Śauri dāsa vanacārī

GBC – Australia, New Zealand, South Seas

## Hṛdayānanda dāsa Goswami

For my beloved spiritual master, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Goswami Mahārāja Prabhupāda, on the occasion of his appearance day, 1978.

To all the members of the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness I offer my respectful obeisances.  
Let us all take this opportunity of the first appearance day of our beloved spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, celebrated in separation, to try to understand and appreciate very deeply the unlimited transcendental qualities of this great soul, *maha-bhāgavata*, who came from Kṛṣṇaloka to exhibit a wonderful mission on earth.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

As we make the difficult attempt to manage the great ISKCON movement which we have inherited from Śrīla Prabhupāda, we see that this world is full of envious and opposing elements; there is danger at every step, friends become enemies causelessly, and there is quarrel for slight provocation, always due to the bodily concept of life. Yet Śrīla Prabhupāda, although occupying the exalted position of founder-*ācārya* of the great ISKCON movement, very expertly and wonderfully traversed the earth, and by his divine vision he was able to minimize and subdue the envy of the living entities and engage them in Kṛṣṇa’s service. In this world everyone is trying to be independent; each man is thinking himself all in all, and therefore he is envious of everyone else.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was so pure and brilliant that he could take the most exalted post in Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission and at the same time control the living entities by his expert dealings, traveling around the globe and managing all affairs. Simultaneously, Śrīla Prabhupāda was able to produce unlimited nectar in the form of his transcendental books. In these books we find all knowledge, all guidance for the conditioned soul entrapped in the illusory network of *māyā*. *Yasmin vijñāte sarvam evaṁ vijñātaṁ bhavati:* “In this way, by knowing that absolute truth, everything becomes known and nothing remains unknown.” (*Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad 1.3)*

Śrīla Prabhupāda is the Jagad-guru who typified the immortal words, *śrotriyaṁ brahma-niṣṭham* \[MU 1.2.12\]*:* he could produce unlimited nectar from each syllable of Vedic knowledge. Just as the desire tree can produce unlimited nectar or anything required for Kṛṣṇa’s service, so Śrīla Prabhupāda revealed that each syllable of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is a desire tree which can produce unlimited nectar for blissful life in eternal association with Kṛṣṇa.

By his preaching Śrīla Prabhupāda exhibited the compassion and vision of the purified *paramahaṁsas*, free from envy. Ignoring bodily distinctions of caste or color, highborn or lowborn, he went everywhere and gave everyone a chance to know Kṛṣṇa, to love Kṛṣṇa, to understand that there is a soul within this body, and to become happy.

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-*  
*trāṇaya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa-guṇārṇavasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master deliver the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities.”**

In Kali-yuga, all the living entities are simply burning in the fire of material existence. Śrīla Prabhupāda could see that within each body there is a spiritual spark, part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa. Therefore, unlike others, who discriminate, Śrīla Prabhupāda freely offered this *bhakti* cult to anyone who would chant the holy names of Kṛṣṇa, who would take Kṛṣṇa’s *prasāda*, who would purchase one book. He created so many novel means by which people touched the spiritual energy of Kṛṣṇa and purified themselves.

*Kṛṣṇa-prema-pradāya te* \[*Cc. Madhya* 19.53\]*.* Certainly it is not improper to also address His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda as *namo maha-vadānyāya kṛṣṇa-prema-pradāya te,* the most magnanimous great soul. *Saṅkīrtyamānāṁ munibhir māhātmabhiḥ.* As described by Nārada Muni, those great-souled sages, or *bhaktivedāntas*, are always chanting the glories of the Supreme Lord and purifying the universe: s*aṅkīrtyamānāṁ munibhir māhātmabhiḥ.*

*mahāprabhoḥ kīrtana-nṛtya-gīta*  
*vāditra-mādyan-manaso rasena*  
*romāñca-kampāśru-taraṅga-bhājo*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Śrīla Prabhupāda absorbed his mind in the ecstatic *kīrtana* of the Lord. With the nectar of pure devotion he uttered the holy words *jaya śrī-kṛṣṇa-caitanya, prabhu nityānanda, śrī-advaita, gadādhara, śrīvāsādi gaura-bhakta-vṛnda;* and overwhelmed with *kṛṣṇa-prema,* love of Godhead, he uttered the *mahā-mantra:* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. In innumerable songs of the Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas*  he deeply explored the transcendental nature of the appearance, activities and mission of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. In this way he produced the most blissful and glorious music for Kṛṣṇa.

*śrī-vigrahārādhana-nitya-nānā-*  
*śṛṅgāra-tan-mandira-mārjanādau*  
*yuktasya bhaktāmś ca niyuñjato ‘pi*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

All around the globe Śrī Gurudeva established *śrī-vigraha* (Deity worship). He loved the Deities, he knew who They were, and he revealed to us, “This is Kṛṣṇa. Be careful; you are dealing with Kṛṣṇa.”

*catur-vidha-śrī-bhagavat-prasāda-*  
*svādv-anna-tṛptān hari-bhakta-saṅghān*  
*kṛtvaiva tṛptiṁ bhajataḥ sadaiva*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Śrīla Prabhupāda showered us with Kṛṣṇa’s *prasāda*. He drowned the fire of material desires with a shower of *samosās, pakoras, purīs, hallava, subjīs,* so many things, and when he saw that we were simply overwhelmed by discovering Kṛṣṇa’s *prasāda*, he was greatly satisfied.

In this way, one by one we can see how the Servitor Godhead came among us. Now he has entrusted to us his mission for awakening the conditioned souls, beginning with ourselves: *jīv jāga jīv jāga goracanda bale, kata nidrā yāo māyā-pīścira kole*. He has given us all the task of becoming pure devotees of Kṛṣṇa and simultaneously awakening all the conditioned souls to the message of Godhead, to the message of the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu. It is not an easy task, but it becomes easy by surrendering at his lotus feet. He is the personification of *utsāhān niścayād dhairyāt* (*Upadeśāmṛta* 3)*.*

Śrīla Prabhupāda was always determined, always enthusiastic, always thinking of the goal with great confidence; therefore we must dedicate all of our energy to the service of his lotus feet, and also become determined, enthusiastic and patient. It is automatically understood that within this material world there will be so many impediments; there will be so many obstacles and difficulties. Therefore Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu has warned us, *tṛṇād api sunīcena taror api sahiṣṇunā:* “Be more humble than a straw in the street, more tolerant than a tree.” Śrīla Prabhupāda once said in a letter to His Holiness Satsvarūpa Mahārāja, “Unless we are more tolerant than a tree, we cannot spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” This means that one must understand that he is not his body. By understanding he is not this body even though he is surrounded by opposing elements and material difficulties, still he pushes on, satisfied and rejoicing in the pure soul, part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord. This is the mood of Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission. One should rejoice in the pure soul, which is eternal servant of Kṛṣṇa; one should rejoice in devotional service to the Supreme Soul, Kṛṣṇa, and be transcendental to the three modes of material nature, and in that joyful spiritual mood give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everyone with great energy, with great enthusiasm and with love. *Yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa* (Cc. *Madhya* 7.128)*.*

Now ISKCON is a great worldwide institution. Everywhere preaching is going on, book distribution is going on, *prasāda* distribution is going on. Personally I am a very insignificant, poorhearted beggar at the lotus feet of my spiritual master, Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Gosvāmi Mahārāja Prabhupāda, and at his lotus feet I am simple begging, pleading, “Please give me a drop of your mercy; please allow me to participate in your ISKCON movement, please give me the power to always satisfy you. Although you are self-satisfied, still if I cannot satisfy you then life is finished.”

In this mood I call upon all my Godbrothers and Godsisters as well as the new disciples who are now entering the house of *bhakti* to please work together cooperatively for the pleasure of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The perverted tendency of every living entity within this world is to desire his independence, and thus he becomes envious of other living entities. But let us give up this false independence and work for the pleasure of Śrīla Gurudeva. In this way we can do something wonderful for Śrīla Prabhupāda, and if he is pleased with us our lives will be successful and the world will become a happy place.

*signed,*  
Hṛdayānanda dāsa Goswami

Initiating *guru*  
GBC – South America (except for Guyana, Surinam, Colombia and Ecuador)  
BBT Trustee

## Jagadīśa dāsa

A poem to honor His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda on his appearance day, August 27, 1978

The Sun was shining for so long,  
Conferring mercy everywhere,  
A most unusual event  
Considering the atmosphere.  
The darkness of this time is dense;  
It’s very rare that one can see,  
As when the twilight’s almost done,  
Approaching night’s obscurity.  
For those of us who had the chance  
To bathe in his effulgent glance,  
It was a blessed miracle –  
He taught us how to sing and dance.  
He opened up Vaikuṇtha’s gate  
And stepped into this mortal place,  
Inviting us to come with him  
To meet with Kṛṣṇa face to face.  
Alas, but now he’s out of sight,  
And I’ve been dragging way behind  
In hopes that there was still a chance  
Of finding pleasure of some kind.  
While thinking I could find a way  
To have my cake and eat it too,  
I’ve lost my chance to see him smile  
At seeing something I might do.  
Damnation to this wretched mind  
Which dragged me from his lotus feet  
And caused me to ignore the truth  
And think blood-sucking thorns were sweet.  
Now I must make a firm resolve  
To give up such recalcitrance  
And all my foolish cherished dreams  
Which drag me into ignorance.  
My dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, help me please  
To take into my darkened heart  
Remembrance of his lotus feet,  
Permit me please to know this art.  
I dedicate my life and soul  
To help fulfill his wondrous plan,  
To beat back māyā’s influence,  
By Kṛṣṇa’s grace I’m sure we can.  
To all the villages and towns  
In every corner of the earth,  
We’ll take the message of the Lord  
To give the lifeless souls rebirth.

*Your servant,*  
Jagadīśa dāsa  
GBC – Minister of Education

## Jayapatāka Swami

Jaya Prabhupāda!

A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami,  
Known as “Prabhupāda” the world round,  
Established kṛṣṇa-nama-saṅkīrtana:  
Twelve years done what no one’s done.  
He translated, wrote a hundred books,  
Transformed meat-eaters with sacred sound  
He’s Śrī Caitanya’s mercy descent,  
Our Prabhupāda: “Brilliant as the sun!”

*āpane ācare keha,nā kare pracāra*  
*pracāra karena keha, nā karena ācāra*  
*‘ācāra’, ‘pracāra’ - nāmera karaha ‘dui’ kārya*  
*tumi—- sarva-guru, tumi jagatera ārya*

**“Some behave very well but do not preach the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, whereas others preach but do not behave properly. You simultaneously perform both duties in relation to the holy name by your personal behavior and by your preaching. Therefore you are the spiritual master of the entire world, for you are the most advanced devotee in the world.” (Cc.** ***Antya*** **4.102-103)**

Let me offer my prostrate obeisances unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by prostrating myself at your lotus feet thousands of times. You perfectly exemplify the above statements of Sanātana Gosvāmī. Your Divine Grace’s personal behavior and preaching capacity are flawless and superb. You are the ideal spiritual master, and therefore you are the actual spiritual master of the whole world. Everyone can only benefit themselves by surrendering unto your lotus feet and by following your divine teachings, both being self-effulgent and illuminating.

*kali-kālera dharma—kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*

**“The fundamental religious system in the age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement.” (*****Antya-līlā*** **7.11)**

Your Divine Grace has displayed the greatest miracle in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world. Not only in a short time, but to the most fallen and misled souls. This is greater than any magic – it is proof that you are empowered by Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Thus we can understand that in no way can Your Divine Grace be compared with an ordinary man. You are the empowered incarnation of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy! Your activities and words are transcendental. I pray that even birth after birth I may remain eternally in your service.

**“O best of kings, please get up! Get up! Just see this world surrounded by water and infested with rogues and so-called kings. This world is very much afraid, and it is your duty to protect her.” (*****Bhāg.*** **4.28.48)**

Your Divine Grace’s incomparable force in establishing and preaching strict principles in accordance with the disciplic succession had kept bogus principles in abeyance. Unauthorized principles of religion could not be easily introduced by so-called *svāmīs, yogīs,* philanthropists, welfare workers, and so on. Now, feeling your personal preaching absent, we fear for humanity falling into the hands of unscrupulous persons. May Your Divine Grace personally reside always in our hearts and empower us to establish your desires in the world. There is no other hope for bringing actual peace and happiness in the world we see except for your divine instructions and Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

*Jaya,* Śrīla Prabhupāda! Your association of *vapuḥ* has given us a glimpse of Kṛṣṇa at every moment. The brilliant moments engraved deep within our hearts decorate our minds with transcendental emotions progressively revealing the eternal nature of our relationship with Your Divine Grace. Your words and actions shine clear through the misty surroundings of this vacant material world. Certainly the moments spent in servicing your lotus feet are the only real moments of our existence – all else is void and vacant. When shall I realize how every instructions and pastime was said and performed simply to teach and help me to become perfect?

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are so kind to remain just by my side! Thus my debt to you increases beyond any hope of my repayment. The world’s state of illusion overwhelms, but your presence alone gives me strength to carry on. Although Your Divine Grace preached alone with your Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, Godbrothers did not assist. By your blessings, may we work as one, keeping Your Divine Grace simultaneously by our every side. May our love for your idea be ever stronger in our minds than *māyā’s* idea, lest we deviate from our working together, thus disobeying your holy *vāṇī*, keeping you away from our side and us from reaching your lotus feet again.

*vairāgya-yug bhakti-rasaṁ prayatnair*  
*apāyayan mām anabhīpsum andham*  
*kṛpambudhir yaḥ para-duḥka-duḥkī*  
*sanātanaṁ taṁ prabhum āśrayāmi*  
*(Vilāpa-kusumāñjali)*

O dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda! Although we were practically unwilling, you forced us anyway to surrender to Kṛṣṇa out of your unending causeless mercy! You teach the essence of *vairāgya* and give the nectar of devotion. You are most compassionate for others’ suffering and very kind upon the miserable. You are the greatest devotee. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I simply beg to remain eternally under the shelter of your divine lotus feet. Not only do I aspire to serve your lotus feet, but I pray for the benediction to serve the sincere servants of your lotus feet. Being dear to Your Divine Grace, they are dearer than life itself.

*Your most insignificant disciple,*  
Jayapatāka Swami

Initiating *guru*  
GBC – East India, Bangladesh and Nepal

## Jayatīrtha dasa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Eternal spiritual master, lord of my life, hear my humble prayer. You have left our vision, but you have not left our hearts. We feel your firm hand directing us, we hear your perfect orders guiding us, we rest in the security of the shelter of your lotus feet.

Of course when we think of how we sat at your feet and relished your divine company and we think that no more in this life will we have that opportunity, naturally we cannot help but weep. When I visited New Dvārakā recently and went into your garden, which incidentally you will be pleased to know is being kept very nicely for you, I felt like hurling my head against the fountain and giving up my life, remembering how you sat there in perfect tranquility – listening to *Kṛṣṇa* book being read, smashing the scientists, tolerating our nonsense, smelling the night-blooming jasmine.

However, like that husband that provides his wife with a son to protect her before he takes *sannyāsa*, you have left us with something to give value to our lives even after your departure – you have left us with your mission to complete. Although there is no doubt that you could have finished it all yourself, you have mercifully left us some work to busy us and to give us an opportunity to please you, just as Lord Kṛṣṇa in *Bhagavad-gītā* left Arjuna to carry out the fighting, although in fact by His potency the outcome was not in doubt.

*tasmāt tvam uttiṣṭha yaśo labhasva*  
*jitvā śatrūn bhuṅkṣva rājyaṁ samṛddham*  
*mayaivaite nihatāḥ pūrvam eva*  
*nimitta-mātraṁ bhava savyasācin*

**“Therefore get up and prepare to fight. After conquering your enemies you will enjoy a flourishing kingdom. They are already put to death by My arrangement, and you, O Savyasācī, can be but an instrument in the fight.” (*****Bg*****. 11.33)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my prayer is that I will always remember myself to be but an instrument in your hands, and that you will grant me the courage and the strength to fight on your behalf. Just as Kṛṣṇa said, “They are already dead by My arrangement,” so I know that by your arrangement all of the fallen souls of this age have already been saved. Still I see that, just as there was nothing more glorious Arjuna could do than to fight, there is nothing more glorious we can do than to carry on and preach your mission as you have directed us. Indeed, nothing within the three worlds or beyond is even the slightest bit worthwhile.

But one more request, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I know that it was Lord Kṛṣṇa’s desire that Arjuna become famous and enjoy the flourishing kingdom, and I see already how now, by your order, men are coming and bowing down before me, offering me gifts and all respects, but I do not really want any of this. In my heart of hearts, I only desire to somehow or other please you by service and somehow or other, somewhere or other, in some other garden in some other universe, please let me sit with you again. After all of the scientists have been vanquished by eternal time, let me remain with you while you relish the fragrance of the night-blooming jasmine and hear the *Kṛṣṇa* book being read.

*Your useless servant,*  
Jayatīrtha dasa

Initiating *guru*  
GBC – England, Midwest U.S.A., South Africa  
BBT Trustee

## Kīrtanānanda Swami

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

No one could ever be so fortunate as ourselves. We have obtained the priceless gift of your association, which cannot be purchased by anyone, however powerful or intelligent he might be. By the mercy of the pure devotee, one obtains the chance for pure devotional service, which is sought after by the greatest saints and sages. Yet you have chosen to bestow your gift on us, who are so fallen and unqualified.

Therefore, our debt to you is immeasurable. From the very dregs of animalistic life you have raised us to the most elevated standard of existence. You accomplished this in a very short time, and then suddenly you left, handing us the responsibility of carrying on your mission.

We are feeling ourselves all too incompetent to carry on your work. But you are all-powerful, and if you so desire it, by following your orders we can achieve all success. You are the puppet master, adhikārī we are but wooden dolls in your hands. Our dancing is all by your clever arrangement, and the intelligent man will see your hand in whatever we do.

You are never absent from us. It is only by your eyes that we see, and only because of your love that we can live. You are the eternal savior of the fallen souls, adhikārī we thank you for accepting us as your children. Please continue to engage us in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Kīrtanānanda Swami

Initiating *guru*  
GBC – Central U.S.A.

## Pañcadraviḍa Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, only by remaining a faithful servant of your instructions, by which one develops attachment for your lotus feet, can one advance and become fixed on the platform of pure devotional service. I am praying for the wisdom and the undeviating fidelity to never break my eternal connection with you and the disciplic succession. You have indicated that when the qualified *ācārya* leaves the planet, then things once again become disordered. We can all understand that the spell of the illusory energy is very strong (*daivī hy eṣā guṇamayī mama māyā duratyayā \[*Bg. 7.14\]), because even when you were present on the planet many apparently advanced disciples who deviated from your instructions met ignominious ends, being once again pulled under by the material whirlpool. This is undoubtedly because they left the shelter of your lotus feet. You have, however, indicated what we may do in the troubled and difficult days that lie ahead: “The perfect disciples of the *ācārya* try to relieve the situation by sincerely following the instructions of the spiritual master.” (Bhaktivedanta purport to *Bhāg.* 4.28.48) Daily everything else is becoming insignificant. You have said that this material world *is* insignificant, that in Kṛṣṇa’s creation this world is simply like a single mustard seed in a bucket full of mustard seeds. I pray that I may return to my real position by never forgetting your instructions for a moment. I want to make them my very life and soul, and I am praying to develop the unalloyed devotion to do this.

Due to enviousness one leaves his eternal home and wanders in the material world. In this way he turns his back on Kṛṣṇa and meets with good and evil amongst various specifies of life. But although one may choose out of envy and delusion to forget Kṛṣṇa, still Kṛṣṇa cannot forget the conditioned souls, and thus He is situated in everyone’s hart simply to direct the wanderings of all living entities (Bg. 18.61). in this way the Lord acts as *caitya-guru*, the spiritual master within. But you have warned us that although Kṛṣṇa is directing, still “He gives direct instructions only to the advanced pure devotees. In the beginning, when a devotee is serious and sincere, the Lord gives him directions from within to approach a bona fide spiritual master.” (Bhaktivedanta purports to 4.28.41) So in this way we have all become *bhāgyavān jīva,* or fortunate living entities, because we have come in contact with a true spiritual master like yourself, and we are receiving your mercy through your instructions and through the association of advanced disciples who are true to your instructions. Now one should not again return to the conditioned world by becoming falsely independent or mistakenly thinking that he alone is in a position to receive instructions from the Supersoul, and that only he can understand the desires of the spiritual master. This is to fall victim to a very deep and treacherous pitfall. On the other hand, you have indicated that for one who has been “trained by the spiritual master according to the regulative principles of devotional service and is situated on the platform of spontaneous attachment for the Lord (*rāga-bhakti)*, the Lord also gives instructions from within. This distinct advantage is obtained by a liberated soul.” (also from the purport to *Bhāg.* 4.28.41) To assist us in advancing to the platform of fixed devotional service, you have declared that “Kṛṣṇa is now present in this ISKCON movement,” and that ISKCON is nondifferent from you yourself. So if one in great delusion rejects the instructions of the spiritual master, and departs from the association of the loyal servitors of yourself who are endeavoring to push on your movement and Lord Caitanya’s prophesized mission, then it can only be understood that once again he has fallen prey to envy and become a victim of the deluding potency. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray that I will never turn away from you and my Godbrothers, who are your loyal disciples. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know that there will be many *pāṣaṇḍīs*, cheaters who do not know the real path of spiritual advancement, and that they will come forward to disrupt this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. In this way they unjustly belittle your endeavors my minimizing how you relentlessly endeavored to establish ISKCON. All these things will come to pass now that this unfortunate planet is no longer blessed with your footprints and the point of your cane. But those who are actually sincere will not become deluded, and free from envy, they will work with transcendental determination to establish this mission in every town and village. By your divine grace we will see every person as a recipient of your causeless mercy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I know that your accomplishments are innumerable, that your transcendental qualities defy description, but still it is my duty as your eternal servitor to constantly glorify you. You have explained that Kṛṣṇa is asking us to offer him a leaf, a little water, a flower, or some fruit. Actually Kṛṣṇa is not in need of anything. He is the Supreme Lord, and everything is coming from Him; He is the proprietor of everything, and in His creation there are vast oceans, great rivers, unlimited vegetation, and fruits and flowers of all descriptions. Still it is for our benefit, so that by such devotion we become purified, that Kṛṣṇa is requesting. In the same way, I am a simple, insignificant person, not very important at all. But if somehow or other I am given an opportunity to glorify someone as praiseworthy as yourself, and if I am thus able to say something, then that is another example of your greatness. In your humility, you have said that “I may not be perfect, but because I am repeating the words of my spiritual master, then what I say is perfect.” Whatever I have learned, that I have learned from you. Of course, I cannot simply imitate, speaking what you have instructed but not following your instructions, asking others to follow but myself not obeying. I do not want to be such a cheap disciple, but like you, an *ācārya* who teaches by his example. No one is barred from becoming a spiritual master, if he is able to follow you and thus present with realization this science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu commanded, “In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land.” (*Madhya-līlā* 7.129) You once told me, “At every moment I am seeing the lotus feet of my spiritual master.” So even in your glorification of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, you have taught us by your perfect example.

Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura has sung of the spiritual master, “He is my lord, birth after birth.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, I know that you are eternally perfect, *nitya-siddha*, and that you appeared in the material world out of causeless compassion for the forgetful souls entrapped within the false bodily conception of life. You have come to illuminate this dark world with knowledge. I, however, am one of the living entities who are called *nitya-baddha.* Life after life, since time immemorial, I have forgotten Kṛṣṇa. Actually there are innumerable living entities in this material world, and they are all marked with the propensity to try to Lord it over the material nature. Sometime such deluded living entities are garlanded and proclaimed as eminent scientists or proud philosophers or leaders among men. But they are all servants of the temporary, ignorant and miserable material energy, and they cannot relieve themselves or anyone else from the shackles of birth, death, old age, or disease. Birth after birth, I have followed such blind leaders, and thus I have tumbled head over heels into the ditch of ignorance, always forgetful of Kṛṣṇa and His causeless mercy. But somehow or other, by your divine grace, I have again become conscious. You are therefore my eternal spiritual master. I will always treasure the opportunity I had of seeing you, hearing you, and touching your lotus feet. But I am beginning to understand more and more that the real association of the disciple with the spiritual master takes place on the platform of service. I saw you before me, but now I can understand that whoever was in relation with you was so on the platform of eternal service. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī writes, “The materialistic demeanour cannot possibly stretch to the Transcendental Autocrat who is ever inviting the fallen conditioned souls to associate with Him through devotion or eternal serving mood.” (Foreword to *Śrī Brahma-saṁhitā*) Kṛṣṇa is eternal, His devotion is eternal, and so the spiritual master is ever existing on that transcendental platform of service, ever accepting the sincere endeavors of the fallen conditioned living entities to serve the Supreme Lord. In this way, your every gesture, your every word is simply reiterating the same thing: “Serve Kṛṣṇa.” What do I know of Kṛṣṇa? I only know you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and I am begging you to keep me occupied in your service, not just this lifetime, but in unending time and eternal existence.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Pañcadraviḍa Swami

GBC – Central America, Ecuador, Mexico, Colombia.

## Rāmeśvara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

O spiritual master, O giver of divine wisdom, O friend of the fallen, you are the giver of your own bliss and you are the only ocean of mercy. Although dwelling in Vṛndāvana, you have descended for the welfare of fallen souls like myself and you are preaching the divine love of Rādhā for Kṛṣṇa. Please be kind upon me. I am a tiny part and parcel of the Supreme Lord and am always and in every way His eternal servant. As I am always hoping for His mercy and kindness, I offer myself to His most dear servitor, my spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda. (*Arcana-paddhati)*

In the *Hari-bhakti-viveka* there is a statement about how one can offer his body in self-surrender. There the devotee says, “My dear Lord, as a sold-out animal has no need to think about his maintenance and sustenance, so, because I have given up my body and soul unto You, I am no longer concerned with my maintenance and sustenance.” Because you are the ocean of mercy, you have even accepted a rascal like myself as your servant, and by your causeless mercy, it is now my goal to become completely sold out to your lotus feet. The perfection of devotion to Kṛṣṇa is to make your desires and your mission my entire life and soul. You wrote in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta (Antya* 1.117) that the inner feelings of Śri Caitanya Mahāprabhu are His desires that His special mercy be spread throughout the world in this Kali-yuga. Therefore, your innermost desire is that these same transcendental feelings be spread purely all over the world. By your mercy I am becoming very anxious to be completely absorbed in trying to satisfy your innermost desire by using every ounce of energy of my body, mind and words to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. since I can’t achieve this position, I beg to throw myself at your lotus feet and beg you to let me have this service. You once wrote to me that I should “make program to distribute our books all over the world. Whatever progress we have made, it is simply due to distributing these books. So go on, and do not divert your mind for a moment from this.” I know that with your order comes all the power of the Supreme Lord to help us execute your order. I also know that to become completely surrendered to preaching is the highest state of pure *bhakti*. (“The internal devotees or potencies are all eternal associates in the pastimes of the Lord. Only with them does the Lord advent to propound the *saṅkīrtana* movement, only with them does the Lord taste the mellow of conjugal love, and only with them does He distribute this love of God to people in general.” Cc. *Adi* 7.18-19) That surpasses all types of liberation, including living on the same planet as the Lord, possessing the same opulences as the Lord, having the same bodily features as the Lord and gaining personal association with the Lord. If I purely endeavor to achieve this state of surrender then I am sure that you will guide me from within my heart at every step, and by being eternally linked with you in this way I will be able to go anywhere to preach for you, birth after birth. I cannot imagine any higher pleasure for the soul than to achieve this transcendental relationship with you by becoming absorbed in preaching and distributing your books. This process of distributing your books will liberate uncountable billions of souls and deliver them to the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa, and that is your greatest satisfaction. Please kindly take this worthless soul into your association this way.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Rāmeśvara Swami

Initiating *guru*  
GBC – Southern California, Denver  
BBT Trustee

## Rūpānuga dāsa

The fact that we remain Kṛṣṇa conscious without the physical presence of Śrīla Prabhupāda proves our eternal relationship with him.

Śrīla Prabhupāda always blessed everyone, and the feelings of separation are another blessing for our advancement. They will sustain us. They will grow into feelings of separation for Lord Kṛṣṇa, their ultimate cause and repose. They are the highest Vaiṣṇava conception of life.

Now it is our duty to help the world understand Śrīla Prabhupāda’s teachings and appreciate his glories – this special contribution his disciples can make to our *sampradāya.* Meanwhile, let me always glorify my precious Godbrothers and Godsisters and their followers, saintly persons all, pure Vaiṣṇavas, whose association is purifying the world. In them I shall always see my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, and thus life becomes bearable.

*Your eternal servant,*  
Rūpānuga dāsa

GBC – Mideastern U.S.A.

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

***“Homage to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda***  
***On the Occasion of His Eighty-third Appearance Day.”***

Offerings of flowers and poetic eulogies on your appearance day are certainly in the line of Vaiṣṇava etiquette. But the more crucial Vyāsa-pūjā homage is when we disciples dedicate our lives to strictly following your orders day after day, year in and year out. Śrīla Viṣvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura has stated that the order of the spiritual master should be the life and soul of the diciple. Although, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have disappearance from this material world in your physical presence, you live eternally, and the disciple lives with you – by your instructions.

*kali-kālera dharma – kṛṣṇa-nama-saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahi tāra pravartana*  
*tāhā pravartāilā tumi, - ei ta ‘pramāṇa’*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti dhara tumi, - ithe nāhi āna*  
*(Cc. Antya 7.11-12)*

Thus it was stated by Vallabha Bhaṭṭa in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* to describe Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu: “The fundamental religious system in the age of Kali is the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. Unless empowered by Kṛṣṇa, one cannot propagate the *saṅkīrtana* movement. You have spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore it is evident that You have been empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa. There is no question about it.” This is certainly indisputably true of Your Divine Grace. You spread the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* all over the world, and that means that you are the empowered *āveśa* incarnation of the Lord. Moreover, in your Bhaktivedanta purports, you have established such thorough understanding of the power of the disciplic succession that it is now possible for your selected representatives to also become pure devotees empowered with Kṛṣṇa *śakti*. How is this possible? Because as you have always stressed (and as is confirmed in *śāstra* and by *sādhu*), the real spiritual master is one who has become the wholehearted servant of his spiritual master. (Lord Caitanya also claimed to have become an ecstatic chanter of the holy name by submissively accepting Himself as a fool before His spiritual master, Īśvara Purī.) Your genius for propagating Kṛṣṇa consciousness is so great that you have transferred your own undisputed position of *guru* and pure devotee unto your disciples, provided that they act as your own humble servants. If they do so, then they also may fulfill the purport of the Eleventh Canto *śloka: ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyāt* \[*SB* 11.17.27\]*.* You yourself stated this among your last instructions in November 1977: “ ‘Leader’ means one who is a first-class disciple. *Evaṁ paramparā-prāptam.* One who is perfectly following. Our instruction is, *guru-mukha-padma-vākya.* Do you know this?” And were this not true, then how would future disciples be able to surrender in full faith to their spiritual masters and be assured of the via-media link to Kṛṣṇa by *paramparā*? This is another of those gifts of Your Divine Grace which we could not see at the time. You did so many things, Śrīla Prabhupāda, which are only becoming perceivable to us gradually.

This Vyāsa-pūjā, 1978, is the first year after your disappearance from the material world. You have bequeathed to us a solid and vigorous ISKCON tree. To provide for our growth, you have named eleven initiating *gurus*, all under your administration. Thus we can foresee a great flowering tree, spreading strong branches everywhere, a multiplying *sampradāya*, and all stemming from the empowered nourishing touch of Your Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. As early as 1969, you spoke of our future in a letter to Kīrtanānanda Swami: “I have got my disciples, so in the future these many disciples may have many branches of disciplic succession … the example is that of a tree, which has many branches. And you will find one leaf here and another leaf there. But if you take this leaf and the other leaf, and you press them both, you will see that the taste is the same. The taste is the conclusion, and from the taste you can understand that both leaves are from the same tree.” Just as your father, Gour Mohan De, had the genius to prepare you from boyhood with *mṛdaṅga* lessons, facility for Ratha-yātrā, and Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa worship, so you have prepared your ISKCON for a long life of vigorous pure devotion to Kṛṣṇa.

There is nothing to lament now that you are gone. We know that you are like the sun – gone from our vision but existing elsewhere in your own eternal orbit of pure devotional service. You have gone on by Kṛṣṇa’s desire, and that is not lamentable. I am lamenting only my own insincerity and rascaldom, which prevented me from serving you better when you were with us those twelve short years.

Now, by your mercy and the mercy of the ISKCON Vaiṣṇavas, I am studying your life and preparing a biography, the life of the highest *paramahaṁsa*. I am most unworthy to do this, but then you have always kindly given this fallen soul wonderful tasks to link me to you and to Kṛṣṇa. This is your continuing causeless mercy. I pray to never be so foolish as to abandon my position as your fallen, menial servant. In this position, you and the Supreme Personality of Godhead will be merciful on me and allow me the privilege of eternal devotional service at your lotus feet. All glories to you, supreme Gurudeva! I know you are with me and I am your servant. I have nothing to fear, being at the lotus feet of His Divine Grace Abhaya Caraṇāravinda Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

Initiating *guru*  
GBC – Dallas  
Editor in Chief *Back to Godhead*

## Svarūpa Dāmodara dāsa

*oṁ ajṅāna-timirāndhasya jṅanaṅjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*kṛṣṇe sva-dhāmopagate dharma-jñānādibhiḥ saha*  
*kalua naṣṭa-dṛśām eṣa purāṇārko ‘dhunoditaḥ*

**“This** ***Bhāgavata Purāṇa*** **is as brilliant as the sun, and it has arisen just after the departure of Lord Kṛṣṇa to His own abode, accompanied by religion, knowledge, etc. Persons who have lost their vision due to the dense darkness of ignorance in the age of Kali shall get light from this** ***Purāṇa*****.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.3.43)**

This is the priceless gift of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the noble son of Parāśara Muni and Satyavatī, who is the seventeenth incarnation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Today, we celebrate the most auspicious appearance day of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

Great Vaiṣṇavas are proclaiming the achievements of Your Divine Grace over a span of twelve years as “outstanding”, and “inconceivable”. In fact, there is no proper word of praise for what you have given to the world. You have left an immense wealth, a great ocean of transcendental science, and have built a gigantic transcendental empire just to enlighten every living entity all over the globe. Your only desire was to give everyone *kṛṣṇa-prema* in this dark age of science and technology. This is the matchless gift of Your Divine Grace. It would be most worthy of your qualities and glories that they be composed into the most perfect rhyme and sung in the sweetest poetic melody. However, this most unfortunate servant is unqualified to do so.

It is undoubtedly awful just to think about this Kali-yuga, and we actually see that in this age everyone is entangled in the material conception of life. Men behave like machines and have forgotten the higher principles of life. As a result the moral, ethical, and religious principles – the qualities of civilized men – are deteriorating day by day. Social and political ills prevail in the society. However, the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa is so merciful. By His sweet will Your Divine Grace was sent to this planet to save those who are drowning in the cyclonic waves of Kali-yuga. How fortunate are those who were in touch with your divine lotus feet. Even this most fallen and unfortunate servant is quite undeservedly blessed by having been allowed to take birth during Your Divine Grace’s appearance on this planet. You have so mercifully lifted me from the dense dark forest of material nescience and trained me in the science of Kṛṣṇa, the ultimate goal of knowledge.

You told me just before your departure that, “I want to make some human beings from animal civilization. This is my noble desire. I want you to know that.” You personally took so much time and patience just to train this most unintelligent and unworthy servant. I have no knowledge whatsoever, but I have learned this one thing: serving your divine lotus feet and your divine instructions will be the perfection of my life. Your love, feeling, deep concern and great compassion, the perfect qualities of a pure Vaiṣṇava, were so clearly exhibited toward all of us during your last days in Vṛndāvana. There is no one more merciful than you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Kindly allow me to serve the dust of your divine lotus feet birth after birth. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your most fallen servant,*  
Svarūpa Dāmodara dāsa

GBC – Maṇipura  
Director Bhaktivedanta Institute

## Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

*oṁ ajṅāna-timirāndhasya jṅanaṅjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yet-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dṇa-tāraṇam*

*Godbrothers and Godsisters:*

We stand before the threshold of a new ear, though not by our own desire. Our eternal father and beloved spiritual master, the great Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* who descended in our midst by his causeless mercy, has all too suddenly removed himself from our external perception. Today, Vyāsa-pūjā day, marks the eighty-third appearance day of His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Unlike previous Vyāsa-pūjā celebrations, we are now forced to remember His Divine Grace’s disappearance day as well. But, from his bed in Vṛndāvana, Śrīla Prabhupāda explained that there is no difference in the *ācārya’s* appearance and disappearance. Both are accomplished on the order of the Supreme Lord, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. The *guru* is non-different from the Supreme Lord: *ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyāt*. As such, his appearance and disappearance are for the same purpose of executing the eternal mission: *paritrāṇāya sādhūnaṁ vināśāya ca duṣkṛtām dharma-saṁsthāpanārthāya \[*Bg. 4.8\]*.* Actually, there is no question of disappearance, either for God or for His unalloyed servant. Like the sun, they come before our vision and leave on schedule for continuing elsewhere their mission of reclaiming the conditioned souls. Though not present before us, they exist eternally.

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
When thou art living still in sound.  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
To spread the holly name around!

As Kṛṣṇa is always present within the heart of his devotees, similarly Śrī Gurudeva is forever remembered by those who follow his instructions. “He lives forever by his divine instructions and the follower lives with him.”

At the time of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s departure, we felt that we had lost our very life. That person to whom we had dedicated all our words, our thoughts, our activities, had now gone. He who had reared us from spiritual birth, maintained and protected us – we could no longer run to find shelter at his lotus feet. Like newly orphaned children, we moved from one end of the day to the other, now without the steady hand of our father to guide us. The rising sun illuminating the contours of Vṛndāvana, giving the chance of service to all devotees, instead only revealed a great emptiness. The lord of our lives, the crest jewel of Vṛndāvana, the savior of the world, had now departed.

So it seemed. But Kṛṣṇa never forgets his devotees, nor does Śrīla Prabhupāda ever neglect his duty as our father. What we did not understand began to be revealed. His departure was as much planned by Kṛṣṇa as was his appearance. The activities of Kṛṣṇa and His unalloyed devotees are all perfect in every respect and are always pleasing to the devotees. The *gopīs* thought they had lost Kṛṣṇa as He rode away in the chariot of Akrūra, but as the chariot disappeared out of their sight, they were not left alone. Kṛṣṇa had not actually left Vṛndāvana. He had taken up residence in their hearts. And as they days unfolded, the *gopīs* remembered Kṛṣṇa more and more. Everything in Vṛndāvana reminded them of Kṛṣṇa. Similarly, we began to discover that Śrīla Prabhupāda had not really left us. True, we could no longer find him sitting in his room, calling us to come. His presence was felt in another way – in remembrance. Within our hearts, within the hearts of our Godbrothers, Śrīla Prabhupāda was unmistakably present, guiding us as surely as before. Even through the lips of senior Vaiṣṇavas, his instructions illuminated the path for us to safely tread. Spiritual life is characterized by the absence of duality. Everything created by the Lord is His energy and has its connection with Him. The devotee sees Kṛṣṇa everywhere.

*sarva-bhūta-stham ātmānaṁ sarva-bhūtāni cātmani*  
*īkṣate yoga-yuktātmā sarvatra sama-darśanaḥ*  
*yo māṁ pasyati sarvatra sarvaṁ ca mayi paśyati*  
*tasyāhaṁ na praṇaśyami sa ca me na praṇaśyati*

**“A true** ***yogī*** **observes Me in all beings, and also sees every being in Me. Indeed, the self-realized man sees Me everywhere. For one who sees Me everywhere and sees everything in Me, I am never lost, nor is he ever lost to Me.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*** **6.29-30)**

In his purport to these verses, Śrīla Prabhupāda writes:

A person in Kṛṣṇa consciousness certainly sees Lord Kṛṣṇa everywhere, and he sees everything in Kṛṣṇa. Such a person may appear to see all separate manifestations of the material nature, but in each and every instance he is conscious of Kṛṣṇa, knowing that everything is the manifestation of Kṛṣṇa’s energy. Nothing can exist without Kṛṣṇa, and Kṛṣṇa is the Lord of everything – this is the basic principle of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the development of love of Kṛṣṇa – a position transcendental even to material liberation. It is the stage beyond self-realization at which the devotee becomes one with Kṛṣṇa in the sense that Kṛṣṇa becomes everything for the devotee, and the devotee becomes full in loving Kṛṣṇa. An intimate relationship between the Lord and the devotee then exists. In that stage, the living entity attains his immortality. Nor is the Personality of Godhead ever out of sight of the devotee.

It is the mercy of Kṛṣṇa that He sends His most confidential servitor to represent Him. He is the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead. S*ākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śastrair uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ. Haritvena* means “with the quality of Hari.” Śrī Gurudeva thus is worshiped on an equal level with the Lord because he is empowered to act on behalf of the Lord in any way the Lord desires. It was not therefore out of sentiment that we began to find Śrīla Prabhupāda in all that we saw and within all our activities. *Here* was Prabhupāda, manifest throughout his creation (ISKCON), in the temples, the devotees, in his books and in the services he gave us. Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura confirms, *tomāra karuṇā sāra:* “Your mercy is essential to me.” Prabhupāda once stated: “I never forget my Guru Mahārāja for a moment. I feel him always present with me.”

Thus to maintain ISKCON becomes the most intimate personal service to Śrīla Prabhupāda. We all wished we could have had the opportunity to have rendered him some personal service. By maintaining our institutions, not allowing even one to deteriorate, we can feel the satisfaction of service his body, which he himself equated with ISKCON. Just as the body is only good if all of the limbs and organs are healthy, ISKCON will continue to hold its position so long as the individual devotees maintain their spiritual standard, and to expand ISKCON will mean still further pleasure for our spiritual master, for it was never his way only to maintain. *ānandāmbudhi-vardhanam,* the *saṅkīrtana* movement is by nature ever increasing.

Here, at Hare Kṛṣṇa Land, we are trying to both maintain and expand. Take, for example, the “morning walk”, practically a Prabhupāda institution in itself. Prabhupāda’s walks on Juhu Beach symbolized to Bombay-ites our movement’s continued strength. Therefore we have continued it, and as we pass our members and friends on the beach, they nod their heads appreciatively, for we are not only maintaining; it is increasing. As we would follow closely behind Śrīla Prabhupāda’s footsteps, now an ever-growing number of young men follow us. The goal is the same; the path is already trod upon; we simply have to follow. The threshold of his new ear is crossed by faithful adherence to the instructions of our Gurudeva. Boldly we march with Kṛṣṇa conscious banner unfurled. His words light up the way, back to home, back to Godhead. Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare!

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya!*  
Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya!*  
Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya!*

Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami

Initiating *guru*  
GBC – Western India, BTBD TSKP  
BBT Trustee.

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON centers around the world

## Adelaide

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

This is surely the most blessed of all events – the anniversary of our spiritual master’s appearance in this material world.

*oṁ ajṅāna-timirāndhasya jṅanaṅjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

To the spiritual master we owe the greatest debt, a debt which is impossible to repay, for he gives on eternal life and thus frees one from birth, death, disease and old age. Thus the *Vedas* have enjoined: *itad-vijñānārthaṁ sa gurum evābhigacchet*. There is no one else who can give such a matchless gift as the *guru* does. *Dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam.* Therefore I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.

To accept a spiritual master should not be taken as a fashion or a fad, but for gaining relief from the miserable condition of material life. He is directly empowered by the Supreme Lord to deliver the fallen souls. *Kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya –* “the bona fide representative of the Lord.”

Therefore we want very much to glorify our *guru*, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Who can say how exalted was his position? The most confidential servitor of the Lord. “Prabhupāda” – how rightly for him to have this title, “the feet at whom all masters sit”, an exalted personality. Simply by his every word and action he created in everyone he came across a long-lost desire for service to Kṛṣṇa, surrender to the Lord.

It is said that the pure devotee is like a touchstone: whosoever he comes in contact with becomes mad after Kṛṣṇa. With a touchstone one can turn even low-grade metals into gold. Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda turned us *mlecchas* and *yavanas* into Vaiṣṇavas.

It is said in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that the disciple of a bona fide spiritual master should give up his life when the spiritual master leaves this world if he cannot carry on the mission of the *guru*. We don’t want to give up our bodies yet. We want to push on the movement our Prabhupāda has established, for we know this is his desire. He wants to see us working to please Lord Caitanya. He wants us to deliver the fallen conditioned souls.

Actually his mercy is all that we are made of!

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give us the shade of your lotus feet! Please let us be your servants, birth after birth.

*Your most fallen servants of Gaura Hari Mandira (Adelaide, Australia),*

Kṛpākāra dāsa adhikārī / Jaleyu-devi dāsī / Karṇāmṛta dāsa brahmacārī / Gaura-manṇḍala-bhūmi dasa brahmacārī / Sarvalakṣana dāsa brahmacārī / Tribhuvana-pati dāsa brahmacārī / Śikhipiccha-dhara dāsa brahmacārī / Pūrṇacandra dāsa adhikārī / Bhakta Greg / Bhakta Joh / Bhakta Colin / Bhakta Adrian / Bhakta Chris / Bhaktin Tanya

## Ahmedabad

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our worthless prostrate obeisances. All glories to your divine appearance and all glories to your divine disappearance. Our lives and hearts were as dry as a desert and were hankering for happiness and satisfaction like a fish on the land. In such a state you appeared as a merciful angel and filled our hearts with supreme bliss and satisfaction. How can we forget our miserable condition before taking shelter at your lotus feet, which are saturated with transcendental love of God? It is a fact that you are present by your instructions and by your books, but it is also a fact that we can never forget your transcendental smiling lotus face, which can pacify the entire universe.

Once when Lord Kṛṣṇa was displaying His transcendental pastimes in Vṛndāvana in the midst of thousands of *gopīs*, He chose to disappear from amongst them, leaving the *gopīs* in complete agony and frustration. The *gopīs* started searching for Him in desperation, asking all the trees and the creepers, the flowers and the cows if they knew where Kṛṣṇa went. At that time Kṛṣṇa assumed the form of Nārāyaṇa. They offered their obeisances to Nārāyaṇa and continued looking for Kṛṣṇa. Similarly, we know our Śrīla Prabhupāda in his transcendental human form, and wherever we are we want to associate with your form as Śrīla Prabhupāda. Whatever little practice of devotion or spiritual life we have is due to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We did not know how much we would miss you until you actually left us physically.

Indeed, who can measure your glories or who can even attempt to begin appreciating them? Maybe Lord Śiva or Śukadeva Gosvāmī or Nāradaji knows your glory. I can just imagine how gloriously all the demigods in the heavenly planets must have worshiped you when you left for your real home of Kṛṣṇaloka. All the great personalities like Indra, Candra, Gaṇeśa, Varuṇa and all the great sages like Bṛghu, Marīci, Vyāsadeva, Nārada and Brahmājī must have showered incessant flowers and offered the most wonderful garlands and the most exalted worship on your way home. And Lord Śiva must have danced in ecstasy at your glorious departure. And we cannot even imagine how much love and affection you must have received in Goloka Vṛndāvana. Certainly you did not belong to this material world. Perhaps you are a cowherd boy or a *mañjarī* or *gopī*, but because of your intense love for the living entities, and intense mercy, Kṛṣṇa chose to send you to this world. Caitanya Mahāprabhu was addressed as *mahā-vadānyāvatāra* by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī. But you are the personification of Lord Caitanya’s magnanimity, therefore you are the supreme grace of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

There is no person superior to you in the entire cosmic creation because you are the most beloved of Kṛṣṇa. You are the sum total of the demigods. Just as Uddhava says in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.29.6):

*naivopayanty apacitiṁ kavayas taveśa*  
*brahmāyuṣāpi kṛtam ṛddha-mudaḥ smarantaḥ*  
*yo ‘ntar bahis tanu-bhṛtām aśubhaṁ vidhunvann*  
*ācārya-caittya-vapuṣā sva-gatiṁ vyanakt*

**“O my Lord! Transcendental poets and experts in spiritual science could not fully express their indebtedness to You, even if they were endowed with the prolonged lifetime of Brahmā, for You appear in two features – externally as the** ***ācārya*** **and internally as the Supersoul – to deliver the embodied living being by directing him how to come to You.”**

We have been wandering in the material universe for millions of lifetimes and there was no chance of deliverance. Who but Your Divine Grace could have delivered us? We do not have to go anywhere to see the proof of your potency, just as King Mucukunda says in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (10.51.53).

*bhavāpavargobhramato yadā bhavej*  
*janasya tarhy acyuta sat-samāgamaḥ*  
*sat-saṅgamo yarhi tadaiva sad-gatau*  
*parāvareśe tvayi jāyate ratiḥ*

**“O my Lord! O infallible Supreme Person! When a person wandering throughout the universes becomes eligible for liberation from material existence, he gets an opportunity to associate with devotees. When he associates with devotees, his attraction for You is awakened. You are the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the highest goal of the supreme devotees and the Lord of the universe.”**

Certainly you have the capacity to deliver the entire world. Unfortunate the people have become so sinful and degraded that even though you are distributing this highest benediction – love of Kṛṣṇa – so freely, they do not want to take it because they are so blind. Otherwise your very appearance in this material world has made the whole world auspicious. We are so fortunate to have lived in this miserable material world while you were here. Even a moment’s association with an exalted personality like yourself is beyond comparison to any other benediction in this world, as is said in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.18.13):

*tulayāma lavenāpi na svargaṁ nāpunar-bhavam*  
*bhagavat-saṅgi-saṅgasya martyānāṁ kim utāśiṣaḥ*

**“The value of a moment’s association with a pure devotee of the Lord cannot even be compared to the attainment of heavenly planets or liberation from matter, and what to speak of worldly benedictions in the form of material prosperity, which is for those who are meant for death.”**

There is no other way for conditioned living entities to get out of the material world but to take shelter of a *mahā-bhāgavata;* and you are not just a pure devotee, but you are the most beloved devotee of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself carried out His mission only in India and delivered one Jagāi and Mādhāi; but He deputed you to spread His mission throughout the world and deliver thousands of Jagāis and Mādhāis.

From the very beginning of life you displayed symptoms of pure love. Even when you were a child, while flying a kite you would pray to Kṛṣṇa, “My dear Kṛṣṇa, please let my kite fly high in the sky.” When you were a young man, under the shelter of *yogamāyā,* you appeared to be interested in Gandhi’s independence movement, but as soon as you met your eternal father, His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, immediately you manifested your real self. The world could not recognize you then as the would-be deliverer of the world, but Bhaktisiddhānta Mahārāja, being the direct representative of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, immediately recognized you and officially gave you the mission of delivering the Western countries, for which you appeared on the planet.

Many of Bhaktisiddhānta’s disciples were *brahmacārīs* and *sannyāsīs,* but you were a *gṛhastha.* Once one *brahmacārī* Godbrother criticized you, “O Abhaya Bābu! Why are you entangled in family life? Why don’t you give up and take *sannyāsa*?” Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Mahārāja overheard this, and he chastised the *brahmacārī*, “He is a thousand times greater than you. One day he will do something very wonderful and everyone will see.” Thus you kept the words of your Guru Mahārāja and spread the holy name of Kṛṣṇa around the world. That is the perfection of a disciple. Your determination and faith are unparalleled in the history of mankind. And we consider ourselves so fortunate to have taken shelter at the lotus feet of such a unique *ācārya* as yourself. Your whole life, right from childhood till disappearance, is as divine as the pastimes of Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Once in Bombay you were preaching to us, “The preacher devotee is the most dear to Kṛṣṇa because he is taking a challenge and he is taking risk for Kṛṣṇa. Someone may honor him or dishonor him, he does not care.” Then you went on describing how much you had to struggle to preach. You said, “I have not seen left or right, I have just gone ahead! I practically walked through fire for preaching.” That was your specialty. You always thought big and aimed big. Once I said, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, probably you did not imagine yourself that your attempt would be so successful. So many thousands of devotees, so many temples, so many activities.” Your Divine Grace said, “No, I always had this idea in my mind. I always wanted that so many people may join this *saṅkīrtana* movement and a big worldwide Kṛṣṇa conscious organization may start.”

You have always struggled and always came out triumphant. The Bombay temple is the symbol of the struggle and triumph. But you are so renounced that you left just before it opened. Now the glorious chapter of your achievements in India has started. Everyone is seeing these temples and realizing your glories. Here in Gujarat now we have started developing this farm, and maybe if you would bless, we will build something in the city also. You have done everything you could to preach. Now we have to follow in your footsteps and continue your mission.

Please give us the strength to always remember your lotus feet and your wonderful activities. Our lives have become auspicious just by having you as our eternal spiritual father. We do not have any other ambition in life. Lord Kṛṣṇa has fulfilled our life by giving us the shade of your lotus feet. What more can we expect?

*sthānābhilāṣī tapasi sthito ‘haṁ*  
*tvāṁ prāptavān deva-munīndra-guhyam*  
*kācaṁ vicinvann api divya-ratnaṁ*  
*svamin kṛārtho ‘smi varaṁ na yāce*

**(When he was being offered a benediction by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Dhruva Mahārāja said:) “O my Lord, because I was seeking an opulent material position, I was performing severe types of penance and austerity. Now I have gotten You, who are very difficult for the great demigods, saintly persons and kings to attain. I was searching after a piece of glass, but instead I have found a most valuable jewel. Therefore I am so satisfied that I do not wish to ask any benediction from You.”** ***(Hari-bhakti-sudhodaya)***

However, we have only one ambition. Just as you have glorified your Guru Mahārāja’s name all over the world, and just as you have pacified his heart by carrying out his order in completeness, let us also glorify your name and pacify your heart. We certainly have no qualifications to have such an exalted position to be your disciples, but somehow or other we have been awarded this benediction. So please also give us the strength to deserve this great fortune. We pray unto your lotus feet just as Sanātana Gosvāmī prays unto the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa:

*na premā śravaṇādi-bhaktir api vā yogo ‘thavā vaiṣṇavo*  
*jñānaṁ vā śubha-karma vā kiyad aho saj-jātir apy asti vā*  
*hīnārthādhika-sādhake tvayi tathāpy acchedya-mūlā satī*  
*he gopī-jana-vallabha vyathayate hā hā mad-āśaiva mām*

**“O my Lord, I do not have any love for You, nor am I qualified for discharging devotional service by chanting and hearing. Nor do I possess the mystic power of a Vaiṣṇava, knowledge or pious activities. Nor do I belong to a very high-caste family. On the whole, I do not possess anything.
