Still, O beloved of the** ***gopiṣ*** **because You bestow Your mercy on the most fallen, I have an unbreakable hope that is constantly in my heart. That hope is giving me pain.” (Cc.** ***Madhya*** **23.29)**

*Your eternal servants in Ahmedabad,*

## Amsterdam

**Glorification of the Paramahaṁsa - A white swan sailed into the night**

To show us who to love,  
His words gave light and moved the night –  
A blessing from above.

From above the dark and gloomy plane  
Of temporary life  
He is calling us, “Awake and rise,  
Give up this useless strife!:  
We all, we’re here, alive – but dead –  
Knowing neither why nor how.

We live, but know not future, past,  
And cannot enjoy now.

But now we know just what is true,  
He’s told us everything.

He knows the truth, he speaks it bold,  
No fear of anything.

Before we knew you, Prabhupāda,  
We’d met so many speakers  
Who gave us words and promises,  
But left us, still, as seekers.

Who could help us leave the pain  
Of happiness that ends?

Only one who lives above  
These fluctuating trends  
Could give us life again  
Through his own purity,  
And by his life of selfless love  
Prove why we’re meant to be.

He doesn’t speak except to give  
What is already known  
By those who are already free,  
Who have nothing but God as their own.

Cheaters give, but take from us  
Before they’re satisfied,  
But, Gurudeva, your wishing is  
That we’re again revived  
To our eternal state of bliss  
With Kṛṣṇa at our side.

You’re His servant, grave and kind,  
With nothing of your own to hide.

Without a light who can say  
Why or how we’re here?

But when we see how you are free  
Then everything comes clear.

Some say they know what life is for,  
Yet they go on living it  
Like dumb animals who stand in line,  
Waiting, their throats to be slit.

But you, O Prabhupāda, never wasted time,  
You said, “Life ends, so use it well,  
And make it now sublime.”

You live without hypocrisy,  
Your speaking is your life.

You’ve left all others far behind  
For you there is no fight.

And those who follow you  
Have nothing more to ask,  
For they are fully satisfied,  
They know no painful task.

You’ve given us a joyful life  
Of serving God who gives,  
According to one’s surrendering,  
All freedom, knowledge and bliss.

We know that thanking you  
Is our life’s work, much more,  
There is no end to this, we think.

Though with tongues galore  
We want to spread your glory  
All the world around,  
Pleasing you we’ll fill the place  
With Kṛṣṇa’s holy sound.

Now you’ve gone, O Prabhupāda,  
But just into our hearts.

You gave us other swanlike men  
To give the world new starts.

We now see you inside of them,  
The paramparā strong,  
We’ll turn the tide of Kali,  
And we’re not waiting long.

We are your lowly servants,  
And thus eternally  
We only want to stay as dust  
Under your lotus feet.

*108 glories and obeisances to the topmost swanlike devotee of the Lord, Śrī Śrīmad His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.*

*All the grateful devotees of Amsterdam Yātrā.*

## Atlanta-Tennessee Farm

All glories, all victory to Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who has translated the cream of the Vedic literatures into many languages on this planet for the first time. In his exquisite translations, he has not tolerated any statement which contradicts the proper conclusion of the *Vedas*, namely unmotivated and uninterrupted devotion in subordinate service to the Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the fountainhead of all other expansions and incarnations, and the source of the impersonal effulgence, the goal of the less intelligent impersonalists.

*tvaṁ naḥ sandarśito dhātrā*  
*dustaraṁ nistitīrṣatām*  
*kaliṁ sattva-haraṁ puṁsāṁ*  
*karṇa-dhāra ivārṇavam*

**“We think that we have met Your Goodness by the will of providence, just so that we may accept you as captain of the ship for those who desire to cross the difficult ocean of Kali, which deteriorates all the good qualities of a human being.”** ***(Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.22)**

The base upon which the spiritual master stands is his lotus feet, and this is the same base upon which the sincere and serious disciple builds his life’s offering to the spiritual master. The lotus feet of the spiritual master also have dust upon them. This dust is the same as the wind which blows the sails of the boat above mentioned in its quest to cross the all-engulfing ocean of Kali-yuga. These most wanted dust particles of the lotus feet of the spiritual master are Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions. It is only when one is given the shade of the lotus feet of the spiritual master, and this dust falls upon the head of the conditioned soul, that such a fortunate entity becomes truly reenlivened. Without receiving the dust of the lotus feet of the pure devotee upon his head, the conditioned soul is not more than a zombie – living yet dead.

The life’s offering of the disciple, which is always an incomplete attempt to pay his eternal debt to the spiritual master, may be likened unto the construction of a building. The finer and more elaborate the building the disciple is able to make, the better. Yet, it is also very important to carefully check to see that the construction has no inherent flaws in it, because, depending on the severity and the scope of such unwanted things, different degrees of pressure may inevitably destroy the building, either partially or totally. Without a base, the building will never last. If the base is set improperly, there will surely be cataclysm.

The lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda are the basis for the sincere and serious disciple to attain pure devotional service. Therefore, we offer our respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of the spiritual master. We know that pure devotional service is without material desires or aspirations. The bona fide disciple prays to Śrīla Prabhupāda. The disciple wants to be like Śrīla Prabhupāda in this regard, for the spiritual master can never become deluded into accepting a disguised material desire as a devotional initiative. The sincere and serious disciple knows well that attachment to the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda is the perfection that fulfills all spiritual desires. The dust of the lotus feet of the spiritual master is meant to purify the consciousness of the disciple, and the disciple’s only desire is to have his consciousness purified by these instructions.

The wind for our sails, the dust of the spiritual master’s lotus feet, also emanates from the lotus mouth of the spiritual master as his words of instruction. Śrīla Prabhupāda has forever inscribed these words in his books. With these lotus words, the spiritual master opens the darkened eyes of the sincere and serious disciple, and such a disciple finds his heart filled with transcendental knowledge.

We must therefore stick to whatever the spiritual master orders. This is particularly the case when the spiritual master leaves. When the spiritual master is no longer physically existing, the disciple is in the association of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. If we stick to the principle of following the words of Śrīla Prabhupāda, we will eventually see the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

When a disciple experiences the loss of his spiritual master’s physical presence, the disciple becomes bereaved and laments unabashedly. In such a condition, he may experience what appear to be inauspicious symptoms, types of shock, and bewilderment. He is anguished due to severe separation from the spiritual master and feels that the source of all happiness and mercy is forever gone.

When the disciple becomes perplexed about what to do in the absence of his spiritual master, the Supersoul immediately appears, provided that the disciple has purified his heart by following the instructions of the spiritual master. Actually, the disciple and Śrīla Prabhupāda are never separated. The spiritual master keeps company with the disciple as long as he strictly adheres to the instructions of the spiritual master. The spiritual master is not different from the Supersoul within the heart. They are our intimate and eternal friends and guides. In this way, the sincere and serious disciple is always helped, either directly or indirectly, but thus pleasing both the spiritual master and the Supersoul. When the disciple becomes serious to follow the instructions of Śrīla Prabhupāda, the disciple’s resolution becomes tantamount to seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead face to face, situated before the disciple. The second that this point is reached, we will immediately associate with Kṛṣṇa.

As the authorized representative of the Supreme Lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda established the principles of religion, which are Lord Kṛṣṇa’s instructions. As Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supersoul, and the spiritual master are all one in this connection, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions are nondifferent and as good as Lord Kṛṣṇa’s instructions. But, when the spiritual master disappears, things once again become disordered, both within and without. When the disciple becomes perplexed about what to do in the absence of his spiritual master, the Supersoul immediately appears, provided that the disciple has purified his heart by following the instructions of the spiritual master. The perfect disciples try to rectify the situation by sincerely following the instructions of the spiritual master. The sincere and serious disciples take shelter of the lotus feet of the spiritual master. We may see many facades crumble from the base, we may see many facades crack due to inherent flaws within and see a number of quickly concocted constructions destined to be condemned. There is no recourse but to try to rectify the situation by truly taking shelter of the lotus feet of the spiritual master and bathe in the shower of the dust from them. Without this mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda, nobody can make any Śrīla Prabhupāda advancement.

A fallen disciple may realize that he is not making any spiritual advancement. To once again regain his bearings, he will have to eradicate all flawed construction (in the event that it has not already been destroyed). He must return to the point where these deviations took root. It may even mean going all the way back to the very base of the project. As the raincloud is not selective in pouring its relieving rains everywhere, the dust of the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda is always falling everywhere. It can be had by anyone who is fortunate enough to recognize it and is properly motivated in the matter of its utilization. The dust of the lotus feet of the spiritual master is the only way by which we can attain pure devotional service.

If the fallen disciple cannot grab hold of this dust, he elects to die rather than fail to execute the spiritual master’s instructions. It is the duty of the disciple to take charge of the mission of the spiritual master and execute it properly. Otherwise the disciple is not able to maintain his life.

*gurudeva!*  
*kṛpā-bindu diyā, koro’ ei dāse, tṛṇāpekhā ati hīna*  
*sakala-sahane, bolo diyā koro’, nija-māne spṛhāhīna*  
*sakale sammān, korite śakati, deho’ nātha! yathā yatha*  
*tabo to’ gāibo, harināma-sukhe, aparādha ha’be hata*  
*kabe heno kṛpā, lobiyā e jana, kṛtārtha hoibe, nātha!*  
*karuṇā nā hoile, kāṅdiyā kāṅdiyā, prāṇa nā rākhibo āra*

O Jagad-guru, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please give to this servant just one drop of mercy. Let me be as you are, free from desires and aspirations. When will your mercy fall to such an unintelligent and weak disciple? Please allow me to be with you. In examining me, you will find no good qualities, as your mercy is all that I am made of. If you do not allow the dust of your lotus feet to fall upon me, I will not be able to maintain my wretched and unfortunate life.

All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

## Auckland

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die,  
When thou art living still in sound.  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
To spread the holy name around.  
He reasons ill who tells that you will die,  
By writing books the world you’ll save,  
Within your books you live on still,  
O Prabhupāda! O Gurudeva!

He reasons ill who tells that you will die,  
To all, the holy name you gave.  
Our lives complex you made sublime,  
O Prabhupāda! O Gurudeva!

He reasons ill who tells that you will die,  
With torch of knowledge you entered Māyā’s cave  
And you opened all our eyes with it,  
You forced them open, bit by bit.  
O Prabhupāda! O Gurudeva!

He reasons ill who tells that you will die,  
For you are not illusion’s slave.  
Free us all, by your mercy!  
O Prabhupāda! O Gurudeva!

He reasons ill who tells that you will die,  
Your ship of mercy on love of Godhead’s wave  
Has traveled far, to America,  
O Prabhupāda! O Gurudeva!

He reasons ill who tells that you will die  
You live not in an earthly grave  
But now at Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet  
In Goloka, that world so sweet.  
Go back to Godhead, back to home  
And never more again you’ll roam.  
O Prabhupāda! O Gurudeva!

O eternal father, we used to search for some hope somewhere, but alas, we became so parched and dry in our search for truth. Finally, gasping, looking, trying to find someone we could pray to, Lord Kṛṣṇa kindly supplied. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

*yasya prasādad bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ‘pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Now we are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. Thank you, Prabhupāda.

So kindly and firmly you treated us. Spiritually, we were blind as bats. Now by your kindness we are beginning to see that we are vagabonds who ran away to the kingdom of rascaldom.

O eternal father, that search for hope is now saturated with certainly.

*bhavantam evānucaran nirantara-*  
*praśānta-niḥśeṣa-mano-rathāntaraḥ*  
*kadāham aikzantika-nitya-kiṅkaraḥ*  
*praharṣayiṣyāmi sa-nātha-jīvitam*

**“By constantly serving you, one is freed from all material desires and is completely pacified. When shall we engage as your permanent eternal servants and always feel joyful to have such a perfect master?” (*****Stota-ratna*** **43)**

Your coming was pretold through time untold  
Like certified gold you came to unfold  
Caitanya’s mission throughout the world.

Your sweet mercy expands in soft saffron strands  
To America, Asia, England, and all other lands.

Philosophers and scientists – you’ve defeated them all  
Māyāvādī, Buddhist, Mammonist all made so small.

Seeming so stern to establish your mission  
You’re the softest of all, please stay in our vision.At your lotus feet please save us a place,  
But what can we do to ever repay Your Divine Grace?

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*

śāstra says “vāṇī, vapuḥ”

## Bangladesh

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We thank you so much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for you have saved us from missing the opportunity of human life, of becoming free from birth, death, old age and disease, which is the dream of every living entity! You experienced much personal hardship to bring us these instructions of Lord Kṛṣṇa for our own benefit, and so many times you forgave us for our offensive habits. Though we could not fully understand your teachings due to our impurities of mind and heart, still you continually encouraged us to come up to the standard of *kṛṣṇa-sevā* by your own virtues: patience, compassion, purity, forbearance, humility, vast knowledge … the list is endless! Now we can see the highest perfection of human ideals, which previously we had thought impossible to attain. No one else has ever given us so much knowledge of God, His creation and our relationship with Him; no one else cared so much, nor felt so much love as to become impervious to material impediments. You have given us a practical philosophy of life that will benefit not only ourselves but every single living entity in conditional existence. In twenty-eight languages the eternal knowledge (*sambandha-jñāna*), the eternal *dharma* (*bhakti*) and the eternal mood (*kṛṣṇa-prema*) have been elucidated by you in the most easily understood approach. Though we have no qualification, we take up your mission with eager hearts and fixed minds, praying that by your grace we may be able to please you, and thereby please the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. We beg to remain your eternal helpers,

The devotees of Bangladesh Yātrā

Vaiyāsaki dāsa brahmacārī / Ravi dāsa brahmacārī / Niṣṭula dāsa brahmacārī / Daṇḍakāraṇya dāsa brahmacārī / Jagad-ādījya dāsa brahmacārī / Rasika dāsa brahmacārī

## Barcelona

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajṅāna-timirāndhasya jṅanaṅjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*vande ‘ham śrī-guroḥ śrī-yuta-pada-kamalām śrī-gurūn vaiṣṇavāṁś ca*  
*śrī-rūpaṁ sāgrajātaṁ saha-gaṇa-raghunāthānvitaṁ taṁ sa-jīvam*  
*sādvaitaṁ sāvadhūtaṁ parijana-sahitaṁ kṛṣṇa-caitanya-devaṁ*  
*śrī-rādhā-kṛṣṇa-pādān saha-gaṇa-lalita-śrī-viśākhānvitāṁś ca*

O saint of saints, your greatness is so rare that no words can describe you. The greatest poet will fail in trying to chant your glories in all their extent. Like lightning you have come to illuminate our dark animal life, to bring us to the light of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. With your power you have broken through the stonelike covering of our hearts and placed the seed of devotion, and although our hearts were drier than the desert, you have irrigated them and made the seeds grow.

We were like cats and dogs, and now by your mercy we are conversant in transcendental knowledge; you have taught how to speak, how to eat, how to sleep, be clean, be gentle. In one word, you have taught us how to do everything. All that we have is given by you. We are made of your mercy. Our only hope is to always remember your instruction, to always take refuge under your lotus feet, your beautiful lotus feet that are our Govardhana Hill. Just as the inhabitants of Vṛndāvana took shelter there from the anger of King Indra, so we are taking shelter from the attack of *māyā.*

The rain of your transcendental instruction is pouring on our minds and hearts, extinguishing the fire of material desires. O *patita-pāvana,* mother earth is feeling great separation from the touch of your lotus feet walking on her, and we are feeling separation from the dust drifting from your lotus feet as in the morning when we used to follow you on your morning walk. The breeze is feeling separation from your sweet voice speaking nectarean topics, transcendental knowledge or intimate instruction. But you are so kind that every day you smile to us from the *vyāsṣana,* and your sweet words flow out of your books like liquid nectar giving more and more pleasure to our ears, more and more transcendental instruction to guide us in our march toward Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your power is so great that even without being personally here you keep purifying the whole planet by the mercy of your books, by the chanting of the holy name, by the distribution of *bhagavat-prasāda;* you are so great that you have given us, so low and unqualified, the power to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya all over the world.

Your power is expanding more and more, and the new centers are opening. New devotees are coming and everyone is receiving your mercy, because you are an ocean of mercy who can satisfy everyone. Please, our dear lord, give us the strength to push on this *saṅkīrtana* movement, to keep in line without deviation and without falling aside.

Please, our lord, give us just one drop of your strength to make us able to stay under the protection of your lotus feet so that we will be able to taste the nectar of seeing in the near future millions and millions of persons dancing in ecstasy, millions and millions chanting, “Jaya Gurudeva, Jaya Prabhupāda.”

## Baton Rouge

*svayaṁ samuttīrya sudustaraṁ dyuman*  
*bhavārṇavaṁ bhīmam adabhra-sauhṛdāḥ*  
*bhavat-padāmbhoruha-nāvam atra te*  
*nidhāya yātāḥ sad-anugraho bhavān*

**“O Lord, who resemble the shining sun, you are always ready to fulfill the desire of Your devotee, and therefore You are known as a desire tree (*****vañchā-kalpa-taru*****). When** ***ācāryas*** **completely take shelter under Your lotus feet in order to cross the fierce ocean of nescience, they leave behind on earth the method by which they cross, and because You are very merciful to Your other devotees, You accept this method to help them.” (*****Bhāg.*** **10.2.31)**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

The above verse perfectly describes your divine appearance on this unfortunate planet. Although you have crossed completely over the ocean of nescience, leaving us to burn in the fire of separation, you have mercifully left behind the boat of your crossing – this International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness. With its many worldwide centers, programs, devotees, books, Deities, and even *ācāryas* to carry on your mission, it is a perfectly sound vehicle for crossing the vicious ocean of material life and approaching the shelter of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s safe lotus feet. By your transcendental arrangement, you have provided your disciples with complete facility for peaceful life even in this turbulent world; all that remains is for us to take it up in full measure, so that those who follow us in their turn may find the boat of transcendental knowledge intact and seaworthy.

This vitally important responsibility includes the duty of glorifying your most auspicious appearance in this world, Vyāsa-pūjā; and although due to our many sinful activities and our deep-rooted envious nature we have absolutely no qualification for such work, nevertheless we have faith that we can successfully execute this task, like so many others you have given us, simply by taking shelter of your merciful instructions. As you once very kindly wrote to us, “Just study very carefully our books and then simply repeat whatever you have learned in your own words. This will automatically make you a very successful preacher.” So be it.

Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī states in the famous opening verse of *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*:

*vande gurūn īśa-bhaktān īśam īśavatārakān*  
*tat-prakāśāṁś ca tac-chaktīḥ kṛṣṇa-caitanya-saṁjñakam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto the spiritual masters, the devotees of the Lord, the Lord’s incarnations, His plenary expansions, His energies, and the primeval Lord Himself, Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya.” (Cc.** ***Ādi*** **1.1)**

Here, along with the *pañca-tattva*, or fivefold manifestation of the Caitanya *avatāra*, obeisances are first offered to the spiritual masters. The word *gurūn* is significant here, indicating by its plural form the *guru-paramparā*, or disciplic succession of authorized teachers coming from the Lord Himself. Thus Kṛṣṇadāsa indicates the importance of the *guru.* Why? Because the *guru* is identical to the other five manifestations of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and one cannot know Kṛṣṇa without approaching the *guru*:

*guru kṛṣṇa-rūpa hana śāstrera pramāṇe*  
*guru-rūpe kṛṣṇa kṛpā kareṇa bhakta-gaṇe*

**“According to the deliberate opinion of the revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is identical with Lord Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the spiritual master delivers his own devotees.” (Cc.** ***Ādi*** **1.45)**

This describes how the Supreme Lord appears as the bona fide *guru*. How the *guru* represents Kṛṣṇa’s energies is revealed by Kṛṣṇa Himself to Uddhava in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.17.27):

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvamanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddhyāsūyeta sarva-devamayo guruḥ*

**“One should know the** ***ācārya*** **as Myself and never disrespect him in any way. One should not envy him, thinking him to be an ordinary man, for he is the representative of all the demigods.”**

And in the words of Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī:

*yadyapi āmāra guru – caitanyera dāsa*  
*thatāpi jāniye āmi tāṅhāra prakāśa*

**“Although I know that my spiritual master is a servant of Śrī Caitanya, I know him also as a plenary manifestation of the Lord.” (*****Cc. Ādi*** **1.44)**

There is no contradiction here, because Nityānanda Prabhu, the original spiritual master in the line of Śrī Caitanya, is nondifferent from Śrī Balarāma, the first plenary expansion of Lord Kṛṣṇa. The spiritual master is an expansion of Nityānanda’s potency. Thus the spiritual master is known as both the energy and the plenary portion of the Supreme Lord. The Lord is present in the form of the *ācārya* in so many ways. Just consider the following statement made by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself:

*jñāna-śakty-ādi-kalayā yatrāviṣṭo janārdanaḥ*  
*ta āveśa nigadyante jīvā eva mahattamāḥ*

**“Whenever the Lord is present in someone by portions of His various potencies, the living entity representing the Lord is called** ***śaktyāveśa-avatāra*** **– that is, an incarnation invested with special power.”**

This verse appears in *Laghu-bhāgavatāmṛta* by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, and also in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta (Madhya* 20.373*).*

Finally, the following verses establish the absolute necessity of the association of pure devotees in order to approach the Supreme Personality of Godhead:

*tato duḥasṅgam utsrjya satsu sajjeta buddhimān*  
*anta evāsya chindanti mano-vyāsaṅga-muktibhiḥ*

**“One should therefore avoid bad company and associate only with devotees. With their realized instructions, such saints can cut the knot connecting one with activities unfavorable to devotional service.” (*****Bhāg.*** **11.26.26)**

*satām prasaṅgān mama vīrya-samvido*  
*bhavanti hṛt-karṇa-rasāyanāḥ kathāḥ*

**“The spiritually powerful message of Godhead can be properly discussed only in the association of devotees, and it is greatly pleasing to hear in that association. (*****Bhāg.*** **3.25.25)**

Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as a bona fide spiritual master, you are all of these – pure devotee, incarnation and plenary portion of the Lord, the Lord’s powerful potency, and the Lord Himself. Who, then can fully understand or properly glorify you? We can but apply ourselves diligently to the great task you have set before us, dedicating ourselves to the realization of your fond hopes for the spiritual welfare of all beings through the worldwide propagation of the *saṅkīrtana* movement, and endeavoring with great determination to worship you by following your profound and timeless instructions. Then by Kṛṣṇa’s grace, we may deserve the intelligence to know you as you are, for certainly no ordinary man can understand a pure devotee by his own endeavor. No, our position is to sincerely try to install you as the great-grand *guru* of all the peoples of the world by just repeating your message without adulteration. That alone will make a suitable glorification of Your Divine Grace to be a Vyāsa-pūjā offering to your lotus feet.

O Gurudeva, my only wish is to have my heart purified by the words emanating from your lotus mouth. Attachment to your lotus feet is the perfection that fulfills all desires. The Vedic scriptures sing of your character. Your fame is spread all over the three worlds. I take shelter of your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your worthless servants,* ISKCON Baton Rouge

Dāsānudāsa dāsa brahmacārī / Candraśekhara dāsa adhikārī / Jaya Rādhā-devī dāsī / Mr. & Mrs. Surinder Paul

## Berkeley

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

In the *Science of Self-Realization* you have stated, “’Animal’ means that one has no memory. So when your memory of God is reducing, that means that our human qualities are reducing.” Stricken with amnesia and concerned only with the animal technology of eating, sleeping, mating and defending we were nothing more than two-legged animals – *dvi-pada-paśu*. Being deeply absorbed in the bodily conception of life, completely unaware of our true identity as spiritual beings, we have been engaged in a hard struggle for existence in this material world since time immemorial. In such a fallen condition, with our real knowledge veiled by unlimited lusty desires, we have just begun to be able to perceive the evils of birth, death, disease and old age by dint of your merciful salutary instructions. You have described this struggle for existence as the living entities’ attempt to enjoy pleasurable experiences while simultaneously avoiding miserable ones. You have taught us that modern society is simply engaged in patchwork activities meant to cover over the perpetual miseries of material existence and have compared this foolish attempt to “blowing on a boil” to get relief, which obviously is only symptomatic and extremely ineffective. With undefeatable reasoning and logic you have revealed to us the uselessness of endeavoring for material happiness, which is destined to come our way, even unsolicited, as does distress. “Happiness and distress appear and disappear in due course, just as the winter and summer seasons,” you would say, repeating the words of Lord Kṛṣṇa, “so simply tolerate them without being disturbed.” Or you would teach us a poem by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura in which he writes:

Enjoyment, sorrow, what but lots  
To which the flesh is heir?  
The soul that sleeps alone concludes  
In them he hath a share.  
The flesh is not our own alas  
The mortal frame a chain –  
The soul confined for former wrongs  
Should try to rise again!!

Although we were tightly bound to the world of birth and death by the chain of material consciousness, you have carried us to the spiritual world on the wings of your ambrosial words. “Preaching,” you say, “means to convince others that without becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious their lives are simply wasted.” One who has life can preach. No one can preach with as much conviction as you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are the most merciful of all. To give a diseased man what he desires to eat even though it is not good for him is not merciful but rather cruel. You give only Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the total physician for our composite disease of material life and its miseries. You have given us the internal medicine – *bhakti*, devotion to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Untainted by selfish motivations, you are the only one who has delivered the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa purely, without interpretation. So many “show bottle” *gurus* have come to the West to take selfishly for themselves money, followers, etc. But you came to give, not to take. To give *kṛṣṇa-prema-mahā-dhana* – the greatest treasure of love of Kṛṣṇa. The other so-called *svāmīs* and *gurus* have taught only bits and pieces of the *yoga* process and are compared to the goldsmith, who uses a small hammer and taps the gold many times. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are like the blacksmith, who uses a large hammer and with one stroke his job is finished. You have taught us to take the big hammer of *bhakti-yoga* (*kecit kevalayā bhaktyā*) and finish all material life.

The pathway to the spiritual world has been revealed by the intensely bright effulgence radiating from your transcendental words, which have been made available to conditioned souls around the world via the purports of your wonderful books.

You have given to us the message of the living world – as taught by Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu – that we are all eternally the servant of the servant of the servant of the servant of the servant of the servant of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

You have taught us that the secret of success is to use everything in the service of the Lord, especially our tongue. Simply by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* and eating *kṛṣṇa-prasāda* we can go back home to Godhead. Please help us to realize how fortunate we are.

You have said that our love for you will be shown by how much we cooperate with one another in your absence. Please help us to become free from the propensity to find fault with one another. We pray for the ability to see in each other the good qualities and to combine together like a bunch of sticks, so as to be a strong force in the battle of re-establishing religious principles.

*yasya deve parā bhaktire yathā deve thatā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitāḥ hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the tenets of Vedic scriptures automatically revealed.”**

If one claims to have faith in a bridge he must be willing to walk across it. Activity is the proof of one’s faith. You have stated in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.3 purport) that it is only by faith that one can advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. you have given us the science of self-realization. Please help us to follow your instructions, for knowledge of theory does not good unless applied. Does a lamp reveal anything to a blind man although in the palm of his hand?

We, the residents of New Jagannātha Purī Dhāma beg to be allowed to eternally assist you in spreading the message of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu to every town and village.

## BBT (India)

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

By your compassion, we have all come into contact with Kṛṣṇa. You taught us to serve Kṛṣṇa and also to serve His devotees. You are our beloved spiritual master and eternal father. Your *vapuḥ* association may not be accessible now, but you always remain with us through your *vāṇī* eternally. You constantly gave preference to the publication and mass distribution of your books. By your mercy, we now have the opportunity of serving your Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, and through our distribution of your books, you have allowed us to be instrumental in helping others to become as fortunate as you have made us. We are all so fallen, but you have transformed our unreceptive hearts and elevated us by accepting our paltry service. We do not possess even a trace or hint of qualification; whatever we do is simply a result of your causeless mercy. By increasing book production and distribution, you have permitted us to glorify your unlimited achievements. We are begging at your lotus feet to be blessed with all protection from *māyā*. we pray for the spiritual strength to remain your perpetual servants. Caitanya Mahāprabhu predicted that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement would spread to every town and village. By your sincere devotion to the order of your Guru Mahārāja, you have spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world through your books; and distribution continues to expand unlimitedly. Throughout your life you spent every moment in the service of the lotus feet of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, devising so many plans by which to bestow Kṛṣṇa’s service upon all the fallen souls.

We are not interested in becoming liberated or in acquiring material benedictions from the Supreme Lord. We are only anxious to remain as His servants through service to your divine lotus feet, birth after birth. And therefore we humbly beg to remain always engaged in enthusiastic and forceful propagation of your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

*Bhaktivedanta Book Trust – India:*

Bhakti Premā Svāmī / Prasannātma dāsa brahmacārī (BBT Manager) / Pūrṇabrahmā dāsa brahmacārī / Śyāmasundara dāsa brahmacārī / Patita-pavana dāsa brahmacārī / Rajiv Gupta / Pārvatī-devī dāsī / Anavadyaṅgi-devī dāsī / Dāmodara-priyā-devī dāsī

## BBT Library Party (India)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most auspicious occasion, we, the members of Your Divine Grace’s BBT Library Party, would life to offer our repeated *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet. Your Divine Grace has assured us that you will continue to live forever in the message contained in your spiritual literatures. By your grace alone, universities, colleges, religious institutions, temple libraries, public libraries, big business establishments and cultured individuals continue to order complete sets of your “Encyclopedia,” to the amazement of all other commercial booksellers. Now thousands and thousand of standing orders have been established all over India, Southeast Asia, Australia and the South Pacific.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your inconceivable limitless spiritual energy, you have instilled in us the transcendental realization that your words carry your potency. We have practically experienced that your potency is the very same potency possessed by Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You are His representative, and your words are His words. You are His instrument, and Lord Caitanya is thus enlightening all the countries of the earth through His purest, most selfless, and most qualified representative – Your Divine Grace. If Lord Caitanya Himself were to prepare English commentaries on the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, His message would be the same as your message. Hence, Lord Caitanya, and Lord Kṛṣṇa also live in your words.

Out of His great affection for you, Lord Caitanya has bestowed upon you all the laurels of becoming the first world-*ācārya* on this earth. He rightly knew that only you could be trusted to remain one-hundred-percent steadfast in His service. And now the stage is set; the world will rush to follow your example. They fail miserably in life who fail to read the books and follow the teachings of Your Divine Grace, A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. The head of that person who is not always bowed down at your lotus feet is fit only for the *nāgapāśa* of the Yamadūtas or the scimitar of Lord Kalki.

By touching, reading, speaking on behalf of, and distributing these potent *astras* (weapons) against *māyā*, we hope we rank among those fortunate servants of yours who are first in line to receive your causeless mercy. Please continue to bless us more and more. Magnanimous Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our only shelter, our only hope. We have no chance in the world of attaining even slight genuine success without your constant grace. Through your mercy, the BBT Library Party has become a blissful team of real Godbrothers. By our accepting upon our lowly heads your imparted instructions that we spread this movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in a spirit of cooperation, you have enabled us to experience the unparalleled transcendental pleasure of pleasing you in some little way. Having never tasted anything so wonderful before, we are now experiencing a fresh, new and ever-increasing hunger which can be appeased only by placing standing orders of your beautifully bound *śabda-brahma* in libraries all over this planet. Just as the sixty thousand sons of King Sagara left no stone unturned in their search for the lost sacrificial horse, so we will leave no library untouched and no librarian unswayed by the transcendental potency of your books, until we again taste that nectar. You are blessing us and empowering us with the same spiritual energy that enabled Your Divine Grace to purify and completely turn about the hearts of so many meat-eating *mlecchas*, which is the most inconceivable achievement within or without the science of *yoga*. Who can know your real identity? Who can place limits on your transcendental glories?

In these Southeast Asian countries, our work often takes us into the very darkest regions of ignorance. Some libraries belong to universities and colleges, some are national, some private, some cultural, some purely “technological and scientific”, and all are repositories of complete ignorance, with perhaps a glimpse of passion and goodness. Many are directed by literally snake-eating atheists. Let it be. They will all take your mercy somehow or other. There is no stopping this movement. Who can stop a tidal wave, an avalanche, or the wind? Matter is the dictionary of the spirit, says Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda. The will of the Lord is similarly irresistible. And by your sublime mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have become the instruments through which this inevitable tide will continue to swell. Therefore we pray again and again, hoping against hope that we may become simply empowered to please Your Divine Grace, Śrīla Prabhupāda. All glories, all glories, all glories to your most worshipable *vāṇī* (written spiritual guidance) by which everyone now has that rare opportunity of intimate association with an unalloyed pure devotee of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

*Your insignificant servants,*

Abhinanda dāsa brahmacārī / Satyanārāyaṇa dāsa brahmacārī / Bahuśirā dāsa brahmacārī / Yajña dāsa brahmacārī / Pitā dāsa brahmacārī / Patita-uddhāraṇa dāsa brahmacārī / Jīva Gosvāmī dāsa brahmacārī / Jaya dāsa brahmacārī / Cānūrāri dāsa brahmacārī / Rāmaśaraṇa dāsa brahmacārī / Bhīma dāsa brahmacārī / Anavadyaṅgī-devī dāsī

**Appendix:** Since hundreds of Asia’s foremost leaders, educators adhikārī scholars have sent in glowing letters of sincere appreciation of your books, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have included a typical review from a Sanskrit scholar along with our humble homage. It speaks on behalf of all scholars of the world who are now beginning to see the absolute necessity of accepting the teachings of Your Divine Grace.

*Review from The Hindu Dharma Prathishthanam, Tirumala*

“I have had the pleasure of perusing some volumes of the edition of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, and in particular the Fifth Canto, Volume Two, which deals with the cosmogony of the universe, translated from Sanskrit into English and commented upon by His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Scholars know full well that the *Bhāgavatam* is an encyclopedia of Vedic knowledge. What is most thrilling about this particular edition is that His Divine Grace, whom I look upon as nothing short of an *avatāra* of Godhead, born into a mortal coil with the very purpose of cleansing the present-day world of its profane subhuman mundaneness of outlook and spiritualizing it, wields a pen which seems as though monitored by the very Bhagavān Vyāsa. Apart from the various noteworthy qualities of this Bhaktivedanta Book Trust edition, which includes original Sanskrit verses in Devanāgarī script, a transliteration thereof, *pratipadārtha* which a layman requires, a translation in the most spirited language, and ultimately a delineation of the purport which illumines the very heart and soul of Bhagavān Vyāsa, this monumental work, I am sure, is going to survive millenniums just the same way as the very Sanskrit *Bhāgavata* could survive to this very day, overcoming all rampages of time.

“What thrills me further about this edition is that His Divine Grace puts fire into his pen and writes so that he pours molten lead over the heads of the earthbound nations of the world which are destined to die their death if they do not care to heed the message contained in these volumes (cf. purports).

“Schools, colleges and universities of the world at large fail in their job if their libraries do not get adorned by these volumes, which are capable of floodlighting the dark caves of the hearts of the present-day students who have been groping in darkness as it were, for want of light. It is not revelation to say that all education is a waste if it does not contact and illume the hearts of students who are coming as though for bread and are being treated with stones in the education institutes nowadays. In the entire curricula of the universities which could cater to the needs of students in this behalf, be they of science or the humanities, there are no books comparable with this edition of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to serve a holy purpose and a divine mission.

“As one who has ordered all these editions for the Annamāchārya Kalā Mandir library, of which I am the custodian, as one who has had the real light from these editions, I announce to the public at large that it is their duty as human beings to save their souls from perdition by benefiting from a study of these editions. It is a pleasure to record that day-in and day-out, hundreds of the local elite personages have been benefiting by a study of these volumes.

“just one more sentence about the Fifth Canto, Part Two, which I have read with great interest. This volume enlightens us on the cosmogony of the universe which was revealed by the sage Bhagavān Vyāsa as per his spiritual intuition. The hierarchy of the celestial globes have their sequential positions herein not as per a spatial vision of flesh but in accord with a Śrīla Prabhupāda eye that comprehends the universe as a whole. As it is said in the *Veda, pado ‘sya viśvā bhūtani, tripādasyāmṛtaṁ divi:* ‘The totality of cosmic manifestation is only a part of Godhead; much more lies beyond in the immortal heavens.’”

Dr. D. Arka-Somayaji  
M.A., Ph.D. (Maths)  
Recipient of President’s Award as a Sanskrit Scholar  
Ecstasy-Principal: D.N.R. College, Bhimavaram  
Retired Reader in Astronomy: Kendriya Vidyapeetha, Tirupati  
Award-winning author of Sanskrit astronomical works, and Sanskrit poet.

## Bhaktivedanta Institute

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dedicated to the pursuit of knowledge through the manipulation of their material minds and senses, modern scientists are becoming progressively more entangled in a network of complexities. In place of knowledge, science has yielded a senseless catalog of facts and intricate theories; in place of practical guidance for human life it has yielded a mad avalanche of technological applications, each more disruptive and artificial than the last; and in place of wisdom it has offered only the sterile doctrine that the world is a play of atoms in the void.

In the midst of this blazing fire of confusion and anxiety, only you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have been able to provide the real solution. You have shown that the way to obtain knowledge is to approach the eternal source of all knowledge, the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

*jñānaṁ te ‘haṁ sa-vijñānam idaṁ vakśyāmy aśeṣataḥ*  
*yaj jñātvā neha bhūyo ‘nyaj jñātavyam avaśiṣyate*  
*\[Bg. 7.2\]*

From the absolute source, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, all knowledge can be obtained – both of practical activities and the ultimate purpose of life. It is simply necessary to approach Him submissively through His bona fide representative, the spiritual master.

This process is very simple, and in place of the dubious pleasure of mastering some thorny scientific problem, it offers one the nectarean realization of his eternal loving relation with the Supreme Lord. Owing to this deceptive simplicity, hard-boiled empiricists are likely to reject this process of devotional service, thinking that it cannot compare with their intricate systems of theoretical speculation. But you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are so kind that you have made this transcendental process of knowledge readily available to everyone in these Western countries. You have made every effort to present it in such a way that it can be understood even by persons dominated by the philosophies of empiricism, positivism, and impersonalism. For this causeless mercy and compassion, we are eternally indebted to you.

Svarūpa Dāmodara dāsa brahmacārī / Rūpānuga dāsa adhikārī / Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa adhikārī / Mādhava dāsa brahmacārī / Sadāpūta dāsa adhikārī / Brahmatīrtha dāsa adhikārī / Jñāna dāsa adhikārī

## Bhaktivedanta Swami Gurukula TSKP

*vṛndāvane ramaṇa-reti-prasiddha-bhūmau*  
*tatrāpi kṛṣṇa-balarām-supāda-mule*  
*jñānaṁ paraṁ parama-kṛṣṇa-sudharmīty uktam*  
*dantas tu deva prabhupāda namo namas te*

**“I offer my obeisances to Śrīla Prabhupāda, who is preaching the glories of Kṛṣṇa at the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma in the place known as Ramaṇa Reti, Vṛndāvana.”**

Falling like sticks on the ground, we, your insignificant servants, fall before your lotus feet to beg with all the humility at our command for just a drop of your causeless mercy.

Out of compassion for the fallen conditioned soul dwelling in the darkness of illusion, you have so kindly come into this material world to spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Circling the globe fourteen times, blessing its inhabitants with the dust of your lotus feet, you have fulfilled the purport of *jagad-guru*.

Always remembering your lotus feet, glorifying your transcendental activities and carrying your nectarean instructions upon our heads, we, the servants of *jagad-guru*, offer our homages unto you.

*Your eternal servants,*  
Jagad-guru Svāmī / Raghunātha dāsa dāsa / Śāśvata dāsa / Padmanābha dāsa

## Bhaktivedanta Traveling Book Distribution

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

No one can understand the activities of a pure devotee of the Lord. The nature of his appearance and his pure motive which forms the basis for his every expression are a mystery to the common man. Like the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, you appear differently to different people according to their different mentalities. Kaṁsa saw Kṛṣṇa at Mathurā in the wrestling arena as death personified. And to Muṣṭika and Cāṇūra He was exactly like a thunderbolt. To the people in general He appeared to be the most beautiful personality. For Nanda and Yaśodā He was the most loving child, and to the *yogīs* He appeared as the Supersoul. For the research scientist Your Divine Grace is a boot in the face. For the self-made gods and *gurus* you are the real standard whose character the Vedic scriptures sing of. To the scholars you are in a class of your own; honorary degree, Ph.D … they are forced to appreciate. Amongst the pure devotees you stand out as the full moon. To the Kali-yuga statesmen and politicians you are impeachment personified, as your words expose their cheating mentalities. For the people in general, you are the only hope. Only you can save them. For your Guru Mahārāja, you are his pride and joy. You have carried out his instructions and thus we can all know you for what you are. You are the most dear to Kṛṣṇa. You have given us entrance into the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya. You have foregone all self-comforting and come to the West to preach the message of Lord Caitanya. By your mercy only we are all engaged in this glorious mission.

You are a mystery to the common man, but the secret has been disclosed. You have revealed your pure mind and mission in your Bhaktivedanta purports. Thus everyone can know you as the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead and simultaneously know Kṛṣṇa the Supreme Personality of Godhead served. You are nondifferent from your books, and by taking the essence from them to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, to preach your glories, I am coming to know your heart. It is melted by love of Godhead, and from your heart compassion for all of Kṛṣṇa’s parts and parcels is pouring out. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you know and love me. Although I am fallen and my heart is full of material dirt, please engage me in your transcendental loving service and cleanse my heart. I want to make it a temple where you will always reside. When all the dirt is cleansed away by your instruction, and I am free from lust and greed and can always think of you, that will be perfection. Then I will sit down and hold a great festival.

Devotees of the BTBD Party

## Bubaneswar; Bhadrak

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I returned to Vṛndāvana after two weeks in Orissa. I was determined this time to stay until he had recovered completely or he left. I did not realize at that time how things would turn out and in great anticipation and anxiety sat in the station in Delhi waiting for the train to Mathurā. When I arrived there in Mathurā and then finally vrindavan I was greatly excited. I jumped out of the car and offered obeisances to Vṛndāvana-dhāma. I arose and saw Akṣayānanda Mahārāja coming toward the temple with Bagaji from Bagaji’s house. I inquired from him (after offering obeisances) the condition of Prabhupāda’s health. “The doctor has just pronounced that he has five hours left,” said Akṣayānanda Svāmī. I was thunderstruck. I could not believe it. I dropped all my luggage in the lobby and ran inside, filthy from two days’ ride on the train. Śrīla Prabhupāda was lying there in his bed, glowing like a thousand suns. His bodily luster was incomparable. I reached out my hand to touch him and then I felt surcharged with spiritual ecstasy. But I was overwhelmed with grief. I just stood there, very numb in shocked condition, repeating over and over again, “Isn’t there any way it could change? Could he not stay?”

Then I was amazed as all the devotees were massaging him. I was standing at his head massaging his head, rendering the last bit of personal service, when suddenly he began to roll from side to side, flailing his arms in the air back and forth and then roaring very loudly, then belching with tremendous force. Then again he was rolling and roaring and then still completely, as if stunned, then trembling and crying, tears gliding down from his eyes. I was thunderstruck. He had shown us the most astounding display of transcendental ecstasy that we had ever seen or ever would see in the future. It was amazing. I thought to myself that everything I had read about in his books was now being revealed.

It was hard to imagine, but now Śrīla Prabhupāda was showing us everything. He was writing the last chapter of the book. He had shown us how to live in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and was showing us how to die in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I was very much confused. I shook with mixed emotions, crying in agony and ecstasy that I could not communicate with him this last five hours. It was most painful. How could I live without Śrīla Prabhupāda’s presence? Especially the last two years I could remember that I was always thinking, “When will I see him next?” It was keeping me alive and happy, anxiously awaiting his association from time to time. But now he was leaving, and I was feeling that I could no longer live.

That evening he passed, leaving of his own volition, and I was feeling greatly disturbed. At first it was shock, then shamelessly crying at his feet. I abandoned all sense of sanity. Bhavānanda Mahārāja was such a great hero. He was the most aggrieved, but he kept his head in all of this confusion of crying and screaming and banging of heads and fists against the walls. (If he had not, I cannot imagine the catastrophe that would have taken place.) “I’ve got to dress Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please stop now,” he pleaded. Then, after collecting myself, I noticed how the room was permeated with a wonderful fragrance. Then we watched Bhavānanda Mahārāja change Śrīla Prabhupāda’s clothes. As he took off the old garments each one was more fragrant than the next. When finally we removed his cloth diaper which we kept between his legs to catch the oozing urine, I was overwhelmed with the fragrance of a bouquet of flowers as I put it up to my nose. The pure devotee had passed in a blaze of glory during the most ecstasy *kīrtana* that was ever held by the assembled devotees. Pure and perfect, his transcendental body lay there peacefully retaining its everfresh effulgence, full of spiritual bliss and knowledge. The atmosphere was surcharged with transcendental emotions and ecstasies.

After this we carried him on a palanquin around the temple and then to his v*yāsaśana,* where we worshiped him all night with *kīrtana* and *bhajana*. The next day, after a *parikrama* of Vṛndāvana, we lowered Śrīla Prabhupāda’s graceful, transcendental form into an ocean of salt. We covered his body, never to see him again. It thought, never to see him again.

Now I was returning to Orissa from Vṛndāvana. Śrīla Prabhupāda had just left us. I was trying to take courage. I had been there by some miracle for the last five hours of his appearance on this planet, and I was deeply struck by the pain of separation. I could hardly think straight. I was trying to find some consolation, but it was most difficult. I had remembered that one phrase – *prabhu-datta deśa* – that your *guru* is in the land where you were told to preach, in your place of preaching which was given to you by the *guru*. That’s where he is. So I kept repeating over and over again - *prabhu-datta deśa*  But it was difficult. I could hardly understand it. I kept thinking, “No, Śrīla Prabhupāda is not gone, he’s here. This did not actually take place in Vṛndāvana. He must still be here.” But it was almost maddening.

When I arrived in Bhubaneswar that evening my mind was spent. I was distraught and tired from the long journey. When I met the devotees I was a little relieved and happy. I entered our grass hut and offered my obeisances to all the devotees, and then, in the next room, to the Deities. Then I went back to the first room and related the story to all the devotees about what had happened: how you had disappeared, how the ceremonies were carried out, how everything happened. It was most difficult. Then I showed them the sheet which was on your body when you passed away which was given by Tamāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami. Then I showed the shirt which was given and the pen for Gaura Govinda Mahārāja, the flowers from your palanquin which were given to all the devotees as *prasāda.* Then after distributing everything I took some hot milk and laid myself to rest.

That is when it really hit me. I was back in Bhubaneswar, and I was no longer going to see Śrīla Prabhupāda. He wasn’t waiting in Vṛndāvana. He wasn’t waiting in Māyāpur. I wasn’t going to be able to go see him there any longer. When I realized that I began to cry. I’m only twenty-eight, I thought. I only had his association for eight or nine years – I spent the major part of my life without him. It’s very difficult. What can I do? In that sorrowful mood I went to sleep. The next morning I awoke and began my morning duties. As I was cleansing my *loṭa* with mud I could not help but remember how he had personally instructed me here in Bhubaneswar how to do this. He told me, “This place is very healthy. You rise early every morning, evacuate in the field, cleanse your *loṭa* with mud, take a bath, and you’ll never get sick. You’ll always be healthy.” By following a simple instruction I had been able to remember him. He was there with me. Then throughout the morning program I kept remembering how you had personally instructed in so many ways to follow all these devotional practices, and I was beginning to be filled with joy because I was getting your association again by following your instructions. And all of the Godbrothers were experiencing the same feeling. We were all just beginning to understand and realize more and more in the pangs of separation how only by following your instructions cent per cent could get your association and mitigate these pangs.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you left a great legacy here in Orissa – to build a temple in Bhubaneswar, to build a temple in Bhadrak and to build a temple in Purī. To translate and print all your books into Oriya and to travel to every village of Orissa and preach to distribute them. And you’ve given us Gaura Govinda Mahārāja for association, inspiration and guidance. You’ve given us Jayapatāka and Bhavānanda as our GBCs, and all of our wonderful Godbrothers all over the world for association and spiritual happiness. I am just starting to realize that you had written the last chapter in the book, but you have only brought to a close the first canto, and there are many more cantos to be written for ten thousand years to come, all by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who are ever present in your instructions. I beg you on this day, please always allow me and all of us to follow these instructions perfectly to your desire, and just as Arjuna became the sword in the hand of Kṛṣṇa to fulfill the story of *Mahābhārata*, let us all become pens in your hand, writing the history of the greatest cultural revolution the world has ever known.

All glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who lives forever in his instructions, and the disciple lives with him.

Bhubaneswar

## Bogota

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances again and again at your lotus feet.

You taught us by your example that the devotee never fears or laments anything, but the occasion of your leaving this world was different. For many months we were afraid that at any moment “that” phone call would arrive. Of course, there was always some hope: “Prabhupāda is getting better,” “Prabhupāda has decided to stay with us,” “He’s being attended by a wonderful *kavirāja*,” “He’s eating normally,” etc. However, we were still fearful that sooner or later “that” phone call would arrive. “My dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, if You so desire, please cure Śrīla Prabhupāda!” We could not understand the plans of Śrī Kṛṣṇa and His pure devotee. November 12, November 13, November 14 …

ring … ringg … rinnggg …

“Jaya, Prabhu, please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda. Prabhu, Śrīla Prabhupāda has ended his pastimes on this earth! Do you understand? Prabhupāda has left us!!! …”

It was a question of immediately taking a philosophical and rational attitude. “After all, Prabhupāda is not his body,” “*Vāṇī* is more important than *vapuḥ*.” “Prabhupāda is in his books and his instructions,” “Prabhupāda has already told us everything,” “Prabhupāda went to assist the *gopiṣ,*” etc….But how was it possible to substitute with philosophical explanations the nectar of your personal association, your voice, your laughter, your gait, your absorption in Kṛṣṇa, your determination to satisfy your *guru*, your mercifulness toward the Vaiṣṇavas, your compassion for the fallen, your iron-handedness towards the *asuras,* your unlimited wisdom, and that atmosphere which surrounds the eternally liberated souls who do not know anything other than pleasing Kṛṣṇa? What nectar!

Now you have gone to Goloka Vṛndāvana, in order to force us to follow you there. If we want that nectarean association once again, we must be ready to become pure devotees, totally dedicated to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, just as you always are. Otherwise, if such determination does not arise, we will again be devoured by *māyā*, to continue indefinitely our existence in this temporary, miserable world of death. Please help us to gain your association. Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your eternal servants,*

Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Svāmī / Mṛgeśa dāsa brahmacārī / Līlānātha dāsa brahmacārī / Akhilapati dāsa brahmacārī / Haricakra dāsa brahmacārī / Askhalita dāsa brahmacārī / Dṛḍha-vrata dāsa brahmacārī / Alakṣya dāsa brahmacārī / Haricaraṇa dāsa adhikārī / Harigadā dāsa adhikārī / Bhakta Juan / Bhakta Javier / Bhakta Roberto / Bhakta Abelardo / Bhakta Orlando / Madre Tirthanidhi / Madre Tṛna / Madre Elizabeth / Madre Gloria / Lalitā / Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma / Bhakta Edgar / Madre Blanca / Kālindī

## Bombay

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

Much has happened since I last wrote. The most significant event has been your passing from this world. Now as I sit before your photo, I realize that you are still with us, as always. But at the time of your disappearance I was completely griefstricken, and could hardly contain myself, feeling separation from such a divine personality.

*kaḥ paṇḍitas tvad aparaṁ śaraṇaṁ samīyād*  
*bhakta-priyā ṛta-giraḥ suhṛdaḥ kṛta-jñāt*  
*sarvān dadāti suhṛdo bhajato ‘bhikāmān*  
*ātmānam apy upacayāpacayau na yasya*  
*(Bhāg. 10.48.26)*

**“Who can surrender to anyone other than Yourself? Who is as dear, truthful, friendly, and grateful as You? You are so perfect and complete that even though You give Yourself to Your devotee, You are still full and perfect. You can satisfy all the desires of Your devotee and even deliver Yourself unto him.”**

The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, is the most beloved personality. You are just like Him, and we love you as the dearest personality, the life of our soul.

You came from the spiritual world to tell us of our real life with God, and to show us the kindness of God you passed so many days with us in this miserable world. You are a divine personality. You are not of this world, and you were never of this world. You came to show us God. Everything about you was perfect, and your sublime presence demonstrated perfection in every respect. Your beautiful body, your sympathetic face, your compassionate glance, your sweet words, your joyful smile, your relishful eating, your profound voice, your peaceful resting, your ecstatic dancing – all were perfect, all from another world. You were everything to us. You were our mother, father, friend, companion, guide, master, child, and life itself. How could we bear being separated from you? How could we tolerate such a loss?

Now you have left us here to carry on your mission, waiting for the day when we will again be with you, again to have the chance to serve you personally. We are suffering in this material world away from you, and we find that there is no cause for living. This world is deadly without Your Divine Grace; without you we have nothing to live for.

“You are the life and soul of the inhabitants of Vṛndāvana. So because You have left Vṛndāvana, all the servitors of Your lotus feet there are suffering. It is as if the lake filled with lotus flowers dried up from the scorching heat of separation from You.” (*The Nectar of Devotion)*

Now you have given us a grave responsibility – to carry on this movement in separation. You have invested us with your mercy to continue this great work, and we only pray that we will be able to accept your divine grace properly. The whole world is feeling your absence, praying for your mercy, and you have left a very insufficient vessel to carry your mercy, but still we pray that you will make us fit to carry your divine grace.

You were with us for so few years. We hardly had a chance to appreciate you. Your every moment with us was full of life, but we were so dull we could not appreciate you, and suddenly you were gone. One devotee said that your appearance was like a flash. You came of your own sweet will to deliver us, and of your own accord you left; but future generations will note how your brief presence changed the entire course of history. I am not elevated enough to think of humanity. Mainly I think only of myself. But I also felt that your appearance and disappearance were like a flash, so brief, but that it completely altered my life.

Actually, you were just like Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa’s complexion is compared to a cloud, His yellow *dhotī* to lightning, and His merciful pastimes to rainfall. Similarly, you too appeared with your swarthy complexion, your saffron *dhotī*, and you loving exchanges, just to enliven our transcendental senses, to remind us of Kṛṣṇa in your form of the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead.

When lightning flashes across the sky, the whole world is lighted in all directions. The dark night is illuminated, and we see the entire landscape of fields, trees, houses, roads, persons – everything. In this way your divine presence enlightened an entire spiritual realm, giving us a vision of our real life, our real home – a transcendental world with *kalpa-vṛkṣa* trees, *cintāmaṇi* stones, *surabhi* cows, and liberated personalities like yourself, all engaged in pastimes of love with the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Your electric presence sent a shock through our hearts and we will never be the same again.

When lightning strikes, only a few persons may see it, but soon thereafter deep sounds of thunder rumble through the sky, and everyone hears those vibrations. Although only a few may have been fortunate enough to perceive you directly, everyone is hearing the thunderous roar of your message – your books, your *vāṇī*, the big *mṛdaṅga,* the printing press, rolling on, vibrating everywhere. “If you go on distributing my books, I’ll never die. I will live for centuries.” A sleeping person will miss a flash of lightning, but the sound of thunder will awaken him from his slumber. Similarly, although we were so engrossed in nescience we could hardly realize who you are and what you showed us, but the thunderous vibration of your message of love is reverberating, resounding in our hearts.

After the deep vibrations of thunders, the sky opens up and pours down rain. Similarly, your rainfall of mercy is showering down upon the fallen souls. You opened our eyes with light and implanted the seeds of *bhakti* with transcendental sound. Now your rainfall of mercy is showering down upon us, giving water to the devotional creeper and helping it to grow up to your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

The instructions of the *ācārya* are always accompanied by his mercy, his helping hand. When you were thinking of leaving us in May, you commented, “The trouble is, you are all like children.” When we countered, “If the father leaves the children, is that very good?” you replied, “The children will be provided for.” Now we are experiencing practically how you are arranging our care and maintenance in every way, although not visible to our eyes. “I may be going, but Guru Mahārāja and Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura are there. I have asked them to kindly take care of all of you, my transcendental children. The grandfather always takes care of the children much better than the father. Do not fear.”

Actually, even when you were with us, we were always afraid that you would leave us. This transcendental anxiety increased our attachment for you even more. Even while meeting you we feared the threat of separation.

My dear Lord, when we are away from You we become so anxious to see You again, and there is great misery in our lives. But then, when we do see You, there immediately comes the fear of separation. Under the circumstances, both when we see You and when we do not see You, we are subjected to different kinds of tribulation.

You made us so attached to you that we cannot imagine living without you. Now we have to follow you back to Kṛṣṇa. You gave us a taste of nectar, and now you have taken it away. What is there for us to do? We can only chase after your footsteps, shouting “Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa!” Otherwise what attraction did we have for Kṛṣṇa? You came to free us, and now also you have left us to lead us back to Godhead.

As the swan loves to dive into the water amongst the lily flowers and would die if he were taken from the water, so we only wish to be with You. Our limbs are being shrunken and faded because You have been taken away from us. (*N.O.D. 38: Indifference and Separation*)

When the rain falls, persons collect the rain in pots, so they can later drink and distribute the water to others. In the same way, you have made receptacles for your mercy, so that others may also drink. You have created Vaiṣṇavas, *sannyāsīs, brāhmaṇas, gurus,* all for the sake of distributing your mercy. Actually, simply by your desire the entire universe is liberated.

Whatever a pure devotee wants from his master, Lord Kṛṣṇa doubtlessly grants because He has no duty other than to fulfill the desire of His devotee. If you desire the deliverance of all living entities within the universe, then all of them can be delivered even without your undergoing the tribulations of sinful activity …. Whosever welfare you desire immediately becomes a Vaiṣṇava, and Kṛṣṇa delivers all Vaiṣṇavas from the reactions of their past sinful activities. (*Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*)

The rainfall of your mercy is inundating the entire universe. What is the value of our pots? Only to enliven us in devotional service, to free us from the material absorption, you have given us so much responsibility for spreading this movement. Just as a father can easily accomplish great feats but arranges so that his son appears to be doing the work, so even now you are continuing to spread this movement all over the world, but arranging for us to get the credit, just as a loving father takes more pleasure in his son’s credit than in his own. “I think this is what Kṛṣṇa desires. I have come to you, but now in this old age I may be going to Vṛndāvana, and you will be coming there to me, and be trained up, and we will spread this movement all over the world.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We are eternally yours.

*Your eternal servants,*

## Boston

*jīvera svarūpa haya kṛṣṇera ‘nitya-dāsa’*  
*(Cc. Madhya 20.108)*

The living entity is an eternal servant of Kṛṣṇa. He is originally situated in the spiritual world, where he enjoys an ever-increasing blissful *rasa* with the all-attractive Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. That transcendental world is known as *cintāmaṇi dhāma* and is far beyond the range of material vision. In the distant abode there are gardens of desire trees and palaces built of rubies. There the gates and archways are inscribed with pictures fashioned from spiritual gems. These jewels are so lustrous that they compete with the effulgence of ten million suns. Yet even more radiant than this is the primeval Lord Himself, Govinda, who is tending the cows, whose blooming eyes are like lotus petals, whose head is bedecked with a peacock feather, and whose unique loveliness charms millions of Cupids.

Since the living entity is awarded some minute independence, he may foolishly choose to abandon such blissful association in the spiritual world, thus condemning himself to accept a material body. Now he associates with the modes of material nature and becomes disillusioned. He proclaims himself the enjoyer and controller of all he surveys, but ironically, he is only its servant. Despite his hankering for enjoyment, he is frustrated at every step. Although probing for some new and hidden pleasure, the embodied being is merely chewing the chewed. He creates one illusory goal after another in a vain attempt to find peace and happiness. He becomes addicted to sense gratification and is forced to act in abominable ways to gratify the increasingly demanding senses. As he degrades himself, he falls down to hell and succumbs to the ever more powerful grip of *māyā’s* illusion. Thus he helplessly remains burning in the blazing fire of material existence, life after life ….

*saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka-*  
*trāṇaya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa-guṇārṇavasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master deliver the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities.”**

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. We are making some humble attempt to please and to honor you by this Vyāsa-pūjā presentation, but how can we glorify you? What can be said of one who has given us liberation, who is returning us to the lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa?

By your own causeless mercy, you have appeared just to deliver such unfortunate conditioned souls as ourselves. Hard as a thunderbolt, your challenge has ignited the spark that is kindling a revolution in the impious lives of the world’s misdirected civilization. Soft as a rose, your compassionate words, more comforting than the soothing beams of the moon, relieve us from the fearful condition of material existence. Without a doubt, you are bringing us back to home, back to Godhead, by guiding us to our eternal *rasa.*

O Gurudeva, our only lamentation is that even while speaking these words we are not fully surrendering and are not fully appreciating your unlimited transcendental qualities. If we may ask some blessing, kindly free us from the thorny forest of material enjoyment. Please allow us to take this *saṅkīrtana* movement as our life and soul, in full knowledge of its dearness to you. Please give us the inspiration and the intelligence to preach its glories around the world, thus giving your blessings to others and making them fortunate.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we will never forget the bitter anguish, the sadness of material existence. Therefore, we are all the more indebted to you as our eternal shelter. Although we are now feeling the burden of separation from your physical presence, by following your divine instructions our understanding of your transcendental personality, our love for you and realization of our eternal relationship with you all expand at every moment.

We are simply beggars for your mercy; therefore, being beggars, what can we offer you? Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept our humble service.

*The devotees at Boston ISKCON.*
