## Brazil

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajṅāna-timirāndhasya jṅanaṅjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

That personality who delivered the treasure of *prema-bhakti*, who was so intense with compassion, where is such a personality to be found as His Divine Grace? The loving devotional service in the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness is coming from the ocean of love and mercy Śrīla Prabhupāda gave us. Śrīla Prabhupāda was never that adorable body, but he was the love, the *śravaṇaām kīrtanam,* the *saṅkīrtana,* the Sunday feasts, the unlimited books, letters, lectures with which he penetrated the iron shields of impersonalism, voidism, atheism and all other nonsense which covers our hearts.

We will never be able to forget your shining face, glancing over the stone, melting it and injecting the desire to achieve Lord Caitanya’s lotus feet. We could never really understand you because of our mundane eyes and ears – you, who come from Goloka Vṛndāvana. Now we must cast off our sentimentalism and march on boldly. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us remain at your feet and serve your transcendental body, the International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness. Let us be pure to fulfill your desire and make ISKCON the biggest movement in the world, if not the only one.

Where are the saviors of the fallen souls. Where is our Śrīla Prabhupāda? Where is Svarūpa Dāmodara, and where are Rūpa Gosvāmī and Sanātana Gosvāmī? Where is Raghunātha dāsa to be found? Where are my Raghunātha Bhaṭṭa and Gopāla Bhaṭṭa Gosvāmī. Where am I to find Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja now? All of them bestowed their mercy on the conditioned souls to bring them back home, back to Godhead. When will we be able to join their company, which is not comparable to millions of bathings in all the holy rivers? All at once they have gone to join Lord Gaurāṅga, the great dancer. We put our lives into your hands. O our lord, you shine brightly, guiding us back to our eternal position. O *ācārya,* O faultless person, you are so wonderful that you can instruct us from within – “This is *māyā*, don’t do this,” or “This is Kṛṣṇa consciousness, do this immediately.” We, your servants here in Brazil, wish to give up all desires which do not please Your Divine Grace. Please let our lives be engaged in your service and somehow or other we will help your work to become the most famous all over the world.

Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!

## Buenos Aires

*akṣṇoḥ phalaṁ tvādṛśa-darśanaṁ hi*  
*tanoḥ phalaṁ tvādṛśa-gātra-saṅgāḥ*  
*jihvā-phalaām tvādṛśa-kīrtanaṁ hi*  
*sudurlabhā bhāgavatā hi loke*

**“My dear Vaiṣṇava, seeing a person like you is the perfection of one’s eyesight, touching your lotus feet is the perfection of the sense of touch. Glorifying your good qualities is the tongue’s real activity, for in the material world it is very difficult to find a pure devotee of the Lord.” (*****Cc*****.** ***Madhya*** **20.61)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda! How great is your mercy upon us. We are so lowly and contaminated, but by your causeless mercy you have saved us from this horrible materialistic way of life and have engaged us in devotional service to the Supreme Lord. O Śrīla Prabhupāda! Only by your mercy can we understand the message of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Please, we beg of you, do not ever stop enlightening us. Anything that we are able to do in this world is done by your mercy alone. If you neglect us, we will all become like dogs again. O Śrīla Prabhupāda! Your physical presence is no longer with us, but we can perceive your presence pervading this movement. You are the center of ISKCON, and the potency of this *saṅkīrtana* is your very self.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda! Let us fall at your lotus feet. Without your mercy we are nothing.

*Your eternal fallen servants,*

Rukmiṇī-pati dāsa brahmacārī / Kaivalya-svarūpa dāsa brahmacārī / Mahābāhu dāsa brahmacārī / Bhakta Ruben

## Calcutta

Never mind everything that happened before! It was simple the dance of *māyā*–an unparalleled burning fire, scorching and destructive, sheer and utter ignorance. As an animal in a burning forest had no way out, similarly, we were rendered helpless, faithless and ridden with doubt.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was dispatched by the almighty Lord Kṛṣṇa as His divine grace. “Divine” connotes absolute power and “grace” connotes mercy. Prabhupāda represents the powerful mercy of God, Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Person. “Power” means capacity to purify; like the mighty sun purifies a dirty place, Śrīla Prabhupāda cleansed the contaminated hearts of his disciples.

His Divine Grace approached us with unprecedented kindness and tolerated our sick condition. We, blind as we were, didn’t know what a great soul had entered our midst. Prabhupāda persisted in his transcendental duty – to enlighten the lowest class of men, to pull us to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. With painstaking care, step by step, he preached and preached and preached and proved the glory of Kṛṣṇa: *kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ.* “Let any nonsense come to me, and I will prove the existence of God,” Śrīla Prabhupāda boldly challenged. Our attachments became shattered, our material lives were disrupted, and our souls became excited at the prospect of holding on to the lotus feet of Prabhupāda and being restored to our natural glory: *dāsa-dāsa-dāsa-anudāsa.*

Never mind the abominable ocean of pain where we had bewilderingly loitered since time immemorial. That’s all over now; simply a nightmare from which His Divine Grace has awakened us. No repayment of that transcendental debt is possible, but by the grace of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, may we somehow or other become inclined to eternally wash the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda with tears of love and devotion.

Real love is not the sentimental and passionate lust of material desperation. Real love is reserved for God and His representative. That’s why we love Śrīla Prabhupāda. It is not artificial and pretentious, but springs from the soul and makes us dance in joyful thankfulness. There was not one shred of hope, but Prabhupāda has picked us up. *Śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma, kevala bhakati-sadma.* Let us not dare to forget even for one moment the two lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda, because *māyā* is no joke, and she will fill in any gap if opportunity is given. Let us rather worship each moment of association with Prabhupāda and carry his instructions faithfully on our heads.

Never mind the ill logic and delusion of *māyā*. Ignore her, even if she may relentlessly, it seems, approach us in our weakness. Rather, accept the science of God as the all-good common sense. Thus remembering and taking shelter of Prabhupāda’s *vāṇī*, the unlimited nectarean volumes of books churned in the vast ocean of his spiritual ecstasy, we shall find continuous strength.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please allow us now to offer you a seat within our minds and hearts, though we are not so advanced, but by your grace we may do so. Such continued association with you will certainly carry us back home, back to Godhead.

## Caracas

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas thatā-bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu probhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

Prabhupāda,  
We just want to hear of you eternally. You have given us everything. You have taught us everything. You have made us in your own image.

Prabhupāda,  
We just want to chant about you eternally. We can’t stop from crying. There is no one like you.

Prabhupāda,  
We just want to remember you constantly. Separation is burning us to ashes. We are nothing without you.

Prabhupāda,  
We just want to serve your lotus feet eternally. When will the day come of our full surrender?

Prabhupāda,  
We just want to worship you eternally. You are our inspiration. You are our life air.

Prabhupāda,  
We just want to offer you prayers eternally. You are our eternal father. Forgive us, please.

Prabhupāda,  
We just want to become your servants. Let us know how to please you eternally. You are our master.

Prabhupāda,  
You are our best friend. We can never repay you. Never will there be someone like you. You have given us so much love! You are our ever well-wisher.

Prabhupāda,  
We just want to surrender everything unto you. All that we have comes from you. Let us know how to please you eternally.

Prabhupāda,  
We just want to be with you eternally, ,at the feet of your vyāsāsana, listening to your classes ….

Give us your shelter! Give us your shelter! Give us your shelter!

*Your uncivilized servants of Caracas Mandira.*

## Carriere

*he guro jñānada dīna bandho svānanda-dātaḥ karuṇaika-sindho*  
*vṛndāvanāsīna hitāvatāra prasīda rādhā-praṇaya-pracāra*

**“O spiritual master, O giver of divine wisdom, O friend of the fallen, you are the giver of your own bliss, and you are the only ocean of mercy. Although dwelling in Vṛndāvana, you have descended for the welfare of fallen souls like myself, and you are preaching the divine love of Rādhā for Kṛṣṇa. Please be kind upon me.”**

You were once living in Vṛndāvana, Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental abode, nicely situated in your own blissful Kṛṣṇa consciousness. But because you were very compassionate upon us and loyal to the order of your Guru Mahārāja, you came here to pick us up, undergoing so many difficulties preaching to the lowest classes of men in abominable places like New York City. Before even reaching this wretched country you became extremely ill, and as you wrote in your diary, “But I am feeling separation of Śrī Vṛndāvana and my Lords Śrī Govinda Gopīnātha Rādhā-Dāmodara.” Also, you wrote, “I have left *bhārata-bhūmi* just to execute the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī in pursuance of Lord Caitanya’s order. I have no qualifications, but I have taken up the risk just to carry out the order of His Divine Grace.”

Your coming to the West has changed our lives completely, not by showy or cheap “miracles”, but by a real transformation of cats and dogs into human beings capable of helping you serve Kṛṣṇa. One time when you were trying to get a residence visa for the U.S., you quipped that perhaps you could get it if your disciples adopted you as an old child. The real case is, however, that you have adopted us as your children, and as our eternal father you have given us everything of value.

Your Divine Grace traveled tirelessly, around and around the world, sowing the seeds of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, to Moscow, Stockholm, South Africa, Caracas, Fiji, Tokyo, and to Carriere, Mississippi. You accomplished more to alleviate the world’s miseries and problems in twelve years than anyone else has done in thousands of years (except Lord Caitanya). You made places of pilgrimage all over the seven continents. We thank you for your overwhelming kindness upon us and the world.

And now it is our greatest pain that you have departed from this material world and return to Goloka Vṛndāvana beyond our vision. But although we do not have your corporeal presence, you are still present. Your disciples, books, instructions, temples, and memories are still available for the benefit of all the conditioned souls. Now some of your older students are writing of your glories and transcendental activities, and we are very anxiously awaiting the appearance of these books, like a desert traveler’s desiring to quench his great thirst.

Everything you did simply increased our Kṛṣṇa consciousness, even by your leaving us, you have increased our desire to go back home, back to Godhead, because that is where you are now, and we want to be with you. Until Kṛṣṇa takes us back we want to carry on this movement – expand and develop and spread it all over the planet. And we want to do it precisely according to your desire and instructions, which we can understand from your elder disciples.

Therefore we consider your appearance day to be the most auspicious day of the entire year for us, because your coming to get us out of the material entanglement was the only good thing that ever happened to us. We would not have been able to appreciate spiritual life or devotional service or Lord Caitanya’s movement, had it not been for Your Divine Grace, who was so expert in rekindling our dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*Your servants,*

## Chicago

I bow down to the beautiful lotus feet of my spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Taking the sacred dust of those lotus feet, I sprinkle it over my head in great awe and reverence.

By his causeless mercy I have obtained the greatest treasures of the Vaiṣṇava-sampradāya. In the beginning he taught me to dance before the Deity of the Lord with upraised hands, and then he taught me to chant the holy names of Hari in *kīrtana* and in *japa*.

When I grew hungry he gave me a taste of sacred *prasāda* and taught me to eat *samosā* and sweet rice. Then for work he gave me the service of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the association of devotees for my company. For my guidance he gave me the shelter of Rūpa Gosvāmī and Sanātana Gosvāmī, and for my play he gave me the Ratha-yātrā festival and other holy days.

For reading he gave me the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and for worship the divine forms of the *arcā-vigraha.*

In an empty world he gave me the supreme abode of Bhauma-vṛndāvana and the transcendental land of Navadvīpa. For a place of bathing he gave me the Gaṇgā and Yamunā, and Rādhā-kuṇḍa to worship. By his grace I have seen Govardhana Hill.

Within my heart he has established a desire for the loving service of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and a compassion for the welfare of my fellow men.

When I was tired he taught me to rest, and for service he wakes me again.

By the touch of my spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I walk and I talk, sit and dance. He makes me do as he likes; I am simply a puppet in his hands, and I bend to his desire. He is my eternal master, friend and well-wisher, and now when I can no longer see his beautiful smile, my heart cries in the agony of separation. By his words I am comforted, and I feel that he is always here, within my heart.

By the grace of my spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have obtained everything, although I have nothing to give in return. He has purchased me with his love and kindness, and I have no desire aside from his pleasure. May he confer upon us all his blessings to follow in his footsteps and execute his instructions, that again one day we may walk by his side in the groves of Vṛndāvana and breathe the fragrance of his association.

## Cleveland

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who is glorified as the most munificent and merciful of all incarnations, has stated in His *Śikṣāṣṭaka* the feelings of love between the devotee and Kṛṣṇa. The sincere disciple absorbed in service to a *Bhagavad-gītā* spiritual master experiences the same feelings. A similar emotion is expressed in this material world when a mother finds that her son desires to leave home.

“O Govinda! Feeling Your separation, I am considering a moment to be like twelve years or more. Tears are flowing from my eyes like torrents of rain, and I am feeling all vacant in the world in Your absence.

“O son of Mahārāja Nanda \[Kṛṣṇa\], I am Your eternal servitor, yet somehow or other I have fallen into the ocean of birth and death. Please pick me up from this ocean of death and place me as one of the atoms of Your lotus feet.

“I know no one but Kṛṣṇa as my Lord, and He shall remain so even if He handles me roughly in His embrace or makes me brokenhearted by not being present before me. He is completely free to do anything and everything, for He is always my worshipful Lord unconditionally.”

Here in Cleveland we are not so scholarly or expert in ritualistic activities which are mentioned in the V*edas*, nor can we deliver poetic stanzas, but I’m certain the devotees feel a tiny speck of affection for you, and would like in a humble way to repay you for your causeless mercy.

We can do this simply by following your instructions, cooperating with one another, with the GBC and so on, and in this way always feel your presence.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all that you are doing.

*Your servants,*

## Columbus

Prabhupāda,  
you have come and gone  
from our worldly view  
but your activities  
have left us amazed.  
You appeared before us  
seemingly as an old man,  
but your vigor and determination  
surpassed even that of a youth.  
You came here alone  
but soon had captured the hearts  
of thousands of us,  
who were charmed  
by your humility and knowledge.  
Your teachings have brightened our day.  
We no longer suffer  
the uncertainty and frustration  
of impersonalism and voidism.  
You have given us Kṛṣṇa.  
No one loves us more than you  
because you are a love of God;  
and now this love is ours.  
We have no way of repaying you  
Śrīla Prabhupāda  
Our only desire is  
to render some small service  
and to see you smile

## Costa Rica

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O blessed Gurudeva, lord of my life and lord of your beloved disciples, you have now gone off to join your Guru Mahārāja to assist him in the eternal loving exchanges between Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

You were here in this world a very short while, but long enough to establish throughout the world eternal loving service unto the feet of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who the world had entirely forgotten. But you came and revived these dead bodies with new life and hope. All of your wonderful qualities I cannot describe in full due to your being like the ocean. I am unable to understand the depth of your greatness. I am deeply saddened not to be able to see your divine form and presence before me anymore. I pray that by your grace you will help me in becoming stronger and stronger and purer and purer so that I can once again gain your association.

I am indeed a great fool – this much you already know. During your stay here you were always kind enough to encourage me to go on with my service, but I, being so selfish and meager, would not part with the pleasure of my senses to please you. Your mercy and kindness and gentleness I never before saw in anyone, anywhere. Prabhupāda! I can only weep in agony and shame of how I’ve wasted the years of my life not taking advantage of your presence on this earth, and now you are gone. I have no sincerity, I am simply like an animal always engaged in animal propensities, so I am fit to be kicked and beaten endlessly.

Although I am in such an abominable condition and unfit to associate with you, I still feel that you eternally encouraged me, even though I was in such a wretched condition. That is your great mercy – to show kindness to such a rascal as myself. Although I know I did not give myself wholeheartedly to you while you were present here on this earth, still I want to change my life and help you in establishing your message of spreading love of God throughout the world. I think I can do that best by cooperating with and serving those who actually loved and served you. All respects and all glories to all my Godbrothers and *gurus*, I fall at your feet begging your mercy.

The devotees of Costa Rica Mandira, New Vaikuṇṭha Dhāma, all send their respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda.

## Cusco

The light of your teachings increased day by day, continually, a if it were a new sun being added to purify our contaminated souls. As the sun rises above the mountains and illuminates the valleys, the shadow of night dissipates, and we can see the real forms that surround us. In the same way, you, who are situated on the zenith of the ocean of mercy, are illuminating our hearts with the light of your transcendental instructions. You are constantly a source of loving ecstasy, absolutely dedicated to the glorification of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, and always chanting and preaching the glories of the holy name of the Lord at the gateways of our unfaithful hearts, giving us separate devotional activities in order to bring us to the platform of pure devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

You are a great kind, wisely guiding us toward the eternal *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu, of whom you are the dearmost servant because you have personally taken up the task of fulfilling His desire to inundate the face of the planet with Kṛṣṇa consciousness. you are the visitor that arrived from the spiritual world, bringing the ointment of devotional service to the tired wandering souls rotting in material existence. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, where are you now? Where do you place your lotus feet, O master, where? You illumine our lives like one thousand suns. Permit us to continue to advance toward the shade of your lotus feet, serving you in a small temple in the high Andes, trying to flood the valleys and mountains in all directions with the sound of the Lord’s holy name.

## Dallas

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Ever since the big Gurukula left Dallas, accompanied by many of its teachers, administrators and *saṅkīrtana* soldiers, the servants of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kālacandajī have been living in the wake of the Gurukula’s fame and productivity without developing anything glorious ourselves. It is the sincere desire of all the devotees here, however, that we can one day put Dallas ISKCON on the map again. This is your temple, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You personally selected these Deities in India and named and installed Them yourself. They and you deserve to be glorified much more than is being done.

We have tried to increase book distribution here in many different ways: we have preached like anything, worked long hours, organized *saṅkīrtana* marathons, formed a women’s *saṅkīrtana* party, traveled to other areas, utilized all available spots in the airport, heard expert distributors give pointers in *saṅkīrtana* classes, etc. We have also tried to decrease our physical needs and expenses in order to increase book distribution. Somehow or other, though, our level of book distribution and remittance to the BBT is still abominably low.

Therefore, we have decided to accept the conclusion of the scriptures as passed on to us by you: that everything is contained in the holy name. Despite the temporary appearance of failure, the holy name will bring ultimate success. If we sincerely and enthusiastically take up your instructions to follow the four regulative principles, associate with devotees, take *prasāda*, avoid offenses, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and preach, then how will there not be success?

“Wherever there is Kṛṣṇa, the master of all mystics, and wherever there is Arjuna, the supreme archer, there will also certainly be opulence, victory, extraordinary power, and morality. That is my opinion.” (Bg. 18.78)

We are thinking that Kṛṣṇa is simply testing our sincerity, patience and dependence on Him. You have told us that purity is the force. We thus conclude that if we are not meeting the standard of Kṛṣṇa conscious productivity, this is an indication of our impurity. You have told us that a thing is judged by its result, as you have proven in your own prolific writing of books and creation of big temples united together in a worldwide movement consisting of thousands of devotees. How did you start this movement? Simply by sitting down in Tompkins Square Park and chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa and by holding discourses on *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and distributing *prasāda*. So this method has been proven successful. Without resorting to a big jewelry business, or wearing coat-pants-hat, or compromising the principles, you have magnificently displayed your purity in depending on the holy name.

So we are thinking that if we can just follow in your footsteps, desiring to become pure like and to go back home, back to Godhead to be *with* you, that Kṛṣṇa will make all the arrangements necessary. After all, the material nature is working under Kṛṣṇa’s direction. What is there that He cannot do? Especially, there is no reason to think that He will not give your disciples full facility. It is not that we are thinking we need not endeavor … simply sitting down, chanting, and reading your books. Rather, we must endeavor with enthusiasm and determination, but tempered with patience. Without patience, our endeavors will be only passion, and the result will be lamentation. Thinking like this, we are trying to surrender, inspired by your example and the example set by your pure representative, our resident GBC, His Holiness Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami.

The spiritual master is expert, and thus you have taught us how to do everything. More than anything else, though, you have attempted to teach us how to surrender to Kṛṣṇa and depend entirely on Him. Some of us have become expert cooks, *pūjārīs*, managers, writers, book distributors, etc. But the ability to surrender is the desirable quality that you have most emphasized, although it is the most difficult for us to acquire. In spite of our difficulties, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we would like you to feel assured that we are trying. We are trying, and by your mercy we will succeed. All glories to you.

*Your servants,*

## Delhi

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our beloved Guru Mahārāja, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who left this mortal world on November 14, 1977, deserves the most glorious place in our own hearts and in the annals of the most illustrious figures of all humanity. His entire profile has left an imprint upon man’s soul which will take generations to reveal. We regard him as the one true eternal spiritual master for this universe who has personally rescued us from the most dangerous condition of material existence. To him we make this humble offering of Vyāsa-pūjā on behalf of our very lives which we pledge to give solely in the service of Kṛṣṇa.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda  
Where thou has moved  
only saints and pure devotees know.  
A realm of pure consciousness  
and untainted devotion,  
Beyond the range of one million suns,  
the abode of Kṛṣṇa you have found.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda  
In the wake of your life  
your prowess of intellect remains,  
Striking as a shaft sent from Gāṇḍīva  
severing the shadow of protected illusions  
As lightning shatters the monsoon night.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda  
The oceanic web of conclusions falsely contrived,  
asserted by mental mercenaries  
to contain the primal atomic theory surmised,  
successfully exploded by you,  
the eternal shastric luminary.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda  
Your courage was sheathed  
in the discipline of wisdom,  
Bold as a hunter who has spied his prey  
but staying his arrow confident  
Waiting … the proper moment to slay.

O most blessed Guru  
Full with courage and mercy  
You are ever as a beautiful rain cloud.  
Selfless in your mission,  
dauntless and ablaze, you vacated  
the prison of mortal frame,  
having wreaked havoc in Māyā’s domain.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda  
Forgive us our weakness under strong fire.  
Māyā weaves strange flames of illusion,  
Born of ignorance so ruthless,  
Crippling our visionary hopes of absolute knowledge.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda  
May Lord Caitanya,  
overburdened with mercy and light,  
know the extreme plight of our condition.  
Just like fearful children  
seeking shelter in the night,  
We crawl beneath the lotus  
of your all-embracing example.

Śrīla Prabhupāda has rendered the greatest service to the fallen souls of this age by enlisting them in the mission of *hari-nāma-saṅkīrtana.* On this very special day we are feeling grateful that we have been placed in this city of Delhi (Hastināpura), where His Divine Grace first began his rigorous preaching work and printing of books. Despite many difficulties, his intense devotion and perseverance necessitated a successful worldwide movement. We pray that perhaps we will be able to grasp a fraction of his dynamic spirit and carry out his divine orders. The result can only be glorious in this world and the next.

*Sincerely bowing down and taking shelter at his lotus feet,*

## Denver

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Several months have passed since you departed from this world, and I am acutely feeling your absence. At special times, my mind retrogresses to your room in Vṛndāvana where you lay close to death on the morning of November 14. At around 10:30 A.M., you began making ominous signs that hinted death, and the continuous *kīrtana*, usually very soft, now became loud and urgent. As an unarticulated sense of impending catastrophe welled up, increasing numbers of your disciples flooded the room, gathering around your bed, packed up tight. As the hours passed and the pervasive mood of impending disaster became an invisible tidal wave, the chanting became frantic, the tears fell in torrents, and prayers, both spoken and silent, gushed unashamedly. Standing very close to you, I prayed to you with intense fervor: “Śrīla Prabhupāda, please don’t leave my heart; remain within my heart always. You may leave your body but you cannot leave my heart. You have given me many instructions and I have followed them. Now I will give you an instruction and you must follow it: Remain eternally within my heart. Please don’t leave me, Śrīla Prabhupāda! I am your most fallen disciple. I have no good qualities. I am the worst rascal. But please don’t neglect me! If you do not neglect me, your *most* fallen disciple, how glorious you will be!” In this way, I was calling to you with great feeling.

While the emotional pitch in the room intensified more and more, becoming almost unbearable, some disciples placed framed pictures of your Guru Mahārāja, Śrī Pañca-tattva and Lord Kṛṣṇa on either side of your head. Your head, anointed with sandalwood paste and encircled by colorful flower and *tulasī* garlands, appeared as a rare, effulgent celestial jewel. Your two forearms, think like sticks, waves mysteriously this way and that, as if conducting some mystical even beyond our earthly view. All the while, devotees were gently rubbing your chest, head, arms, legs and feet our of intense affection, bathing your body with their tears.

As the evening twilight descended upon the room, intense feelings of anguish, fear, love and devotion thickened, devotees chanted, weeping and praying. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I was feeling a great intensification of devotional sentiments for you – whole avalanches of spontaneous realization of your greatness and glory, and my own tinyness and total, eternal dependence on your lotus feet – realizations of your profoundly inconceivable, unlimited mercy upon all of us, your disciples; how you are actually saving us from this dark, miserable material world and taking us back to our original home, back to Godhead. This deep, transcendental meditation upon your greatness, kindness and mercy forcefully dragged my rotten mind to a higher, transcendental platform, characterized both by intense feelings of anticipated separation and of transcendental bliss. “Our only real friend in this world, our benefactor, our father, our protector, our guide, well-wisher and savior is leaving us! How can this be?!” Such feelings and realizations descended in oceanic volume, and I was crushed under its devastating and purifying power.

Then, at about 7:25 P.M., Delhi time, I was intently watching your face, and one devotee cried out, “Śrīla Prabhupāda can’t breathe!” and all the horrified devotees could see that you were opening your mouth as if to take air, but you could not actually take air. You were suffocating right before our eyes! I could not even attempt, now, to describe my disbelief. But you were not struggling. The opening and closing of your mouth was a slow and strangely graceful motion, and you face was totally undisturbed and radiant with peace, your eyes slightly open and staring into space, staring beyond all mundane dimensions. After about thirty seconds you stopped breathing, your mouth remained open, eyes slightly open and staring up, and you departed your body and from this world.

Those who could still chant continued to chant in furious unison, completely unable to stop, as if chanting was life itself. Loud cries and howls filled the room and appeared to be filling the entire universe. One cannot imagine hearing such resounding and pitiful cries from so many people without having been in your room at that moment. For several minutes, there was complete pandemonium. Devotees, blinded by tears, lay down on the ground or wandered aimlessly this way and that, wailing and sobbing as if the universe were being destroyed or as if the most dear object of life had been taken away; and indeed you had. This catastrophic scene is forever emblazoned on my consciousness.

Quite wonderfully, however, beyond being a scene of unspeakable sadness, it was a scene of unspeakable magnificence and glory as well. You departed from your mortal body as a great soldier, a great king, a great demigod, a great saint, a most glorious personality! If we had eyes to see, we would surely have beheld the residents of the higher planetary systems showering flowers, and heard them beating on drums and playing on transcendental stringed instruments, as when General Bhīṣma left his body, lying on a bed of arrows on the battlefield. It was all glory, all victory, opulence, eternal auspiciousness, infinite glory! This inconceivable mixing of great lamentation and profound bliss was total overwhelming. It was as if we all had to be endowed with infinitely great facility to feel and perceive in order to tolerate this experience without being totally crushed and annihilated.

Now, my eternal divine master, several months later, I am trying to better understand my relationship with you, my responsibility in that relationship, and how I am to carry on in your absence. By your mercy and transcendental foresight, you have so carefully and authoritatively explained everything in your books, and so it is there I go for shelter. I gratefully invoke your self-illuminating words. In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* you explain that for the sincere disciple there is never any question of separation from the *guru* as long as he is strictly following the *guru’s* instructions:

The disciple and spiritual master are never separated because the spiritual master always keeps company with the disciple as long as the disciple follows strictly the instructions of the spiritual master. This is called the association of *vāṇī* (words). Physical presence is called *vapuḥ*. As long as the spiritual master is physically present, the disciple should serve the physical body of the spiritual master, and when the spiritual master is no longer physically existing, the disciple should serve the instructions of the spiritual master. (*Bhāg.* 4.28.47purport)

When the spiritual master disappears, you further explain, the devotee, if he is pure in heart, is immediately helped and guided by the Lord in the heart, the Supersoul, the spiritual master within:

One who is sincere and pure gets an opportunity to consult with the Supreme Personality of Godhead in His Paramātmā feature sitting within everyone’s heart. The Paramātmā is always the *caitya-guru*, the spiritual master within, and He comes before one externally as the instructor and initiator spiritual master. The Lord can reside within the heart, and He can also come out before a person and give him instructions. Thus the spiritual master is not different from the Supersoul sitting within the heart ….When the *guru* has physically departed, the Supersoul immediately appears, provided the devotee is purified in heart by following the directions of the spiritual master. A sincere devotee who follows the instructions of the spiritual master certainly gets direct instructions from his heart from the Supersoul. Thus a sincere devotee is always helped directly or indirectly by the spiritual master and the Supersoul. (*Bhāg.* 4.28.50purport)

Then you tell us that it is essential for the disciple to be fully dedicated to properly executing the mission of the spiritual master:

A devoted disciple of the spiritual master would rather die with the spiritual master than fail to execute the spiritual master’s mission. As the Supreme Personality of Godhead comes down upon this earth to reestablish the principles of religion, so His representative, the spiritual master, also comes to reestablish religious principles. It is the duty of the disciples to take charge of the mission of the spiritual master and execute it properly. Otherwise the disciple should decide to die along with the spiritual master. In other words, to execute the will of the spiritual master, the disciple should be prepared to lay down his life and abandon all personal considerations. (*Bhāg.* 4.28.50purport)

Finally, you advise us that the disorder which may follow the disappearance of the *ācārya* can be relieved by his genuine disciples, by their sincerely following his instructions:

Whenever the *ācārya* comes, following the superior orders of the Supreme Personality of Godhead or His representative, he establishes the principles of religion ….. Unfortunately, when the *ācārya* disappears, rogues and nondevotees take advantage and immediately begin to introduce unauthorized principles….The *ācārya*, the authorized representative of the Supreme Lord, establishes the principles of religion, but when he disappears, things once again become disordered. The perfect disciples of the *ācārya* try to relieve the situation by sincerely following the instructions of the spiritual master. (*Bhāg.* 4.28.48purport)

Let us meditate deeply upon these profoundly important and relevant instructions. When Arjuna was feeling great sorrow due to Lord Kṛṣṇa’s departure from this world, he remembered the Lord’s transcendental teachings and felt great solace, in the same manner, we your disciples find solace, in your absence, in the numerous instructions you have left us, which are meant only for guiding us back to our real eternal home, back to Godhead.

Falling at your eternal lotus feet, our divine master, we your disciples fervently pray that you may forever guide us along the effulgent path of your perfect instructions, not letting us stray even a minute degree. Let us remain eternally fixed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness by constantly remembering and sincerely executing your divine instructions, utterly free from selfish, fruitive motives. And let us demonstrate our love for you, practically, by cooperating together amongst ourselves in the execution and spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. If we cannot do this, then let us give up our lives, for what is the use of lives uselessly spent?

Falling again at your lotus feet, we humbly beg you to maintain us, your disciples, who are so devoid of good qualities. Without you, there is no meaning to our small lives. If we forget your lotus feet, our lives become vacant and void. Please, our lord, help us to remember you constantly, to feel you by our side. Guide us, instruct us, keep us on the proper path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, don’t neglect us or forget us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We cannot go on without your mercy! Please, our glorious lord and master, let us always be cognizant of our total dependence upon your mercy and show your mercy to us insignificant *jīvas*. Let us participate, however unqualified we may be, in your sublime and glorious pastime of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world. Give us the intelligence, the clarity of vision, and the deep spiritual intuition to understand your desires and try to satisfy them. We rest in your hands. Protect us or kill us! Guide us as you see fit. Make us surrender fully unto you. Please remain as our eternal master, guide, preceptor, friend, well-wisher, protector. Without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are lost, useless. Save us! We love you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Keep us with you eternally!

*Your disciples at New Badarikāśsrama (Denver)*

## Dublin

**To An Angel**

1\) O where should we find such  
an angel as you?  
So loving, so kind, so effulgent  
and pure!  
We have never known man so  
ennobled of mind;  
You are certainly one of a rare  
special few.

2\) O how could we list all the  
virtues you house?  
You are the personification  
of grace;  
For the sight of your face or  
the sound of your voice  
Does the slumbering virtue  
in rascals arouse.

3\) O why did you come here to  
this filthy place?  
Did you really appear out of  
freedom of choice?  
You must have compassion  
so unboundedly great,  
That you dare to descend  
‘midst this dismal rat race.

4\) O who will receive you with  
reverence apt?  
For you are as good as the  
Godhead Himself,  
And we are so poor, so condemned  
and unclean,  
That our souls by material  
pelf are enwrapped.

5\) O what can we give you?  
We give you our love,  
We give you our life and our  
breath and our soul.  
Be pleased to accept it: it’s  
all that we have.  
Please take it and give it  
to the Lord God above.

6\) And now, please enlighten  
us as to our role  
And how we should act to  
achieve the Supreme.  
Please tell us of Kṛṣṇa, the  
Absolute Whole,  
And dissolve the beguiling  
material dream.

7\) O please, unto us this  
philosophy teach,  
And give us the power to go  
out and preach!  
All glory to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*From all the members of Dublin Yātrā as follows:*

Narahari dāsa adhikārī / Janakarāja dāsa adhikārī / Adikartā dāsa adhikārī / Sarvasāgara dāsa adhikārī / Medhari dāsa brahmacārī / Citraketu dāsa brahmacārī / Indrānuja dāsa brahmacārī / Līlā-mohinī-devī dāsī / Bhakta Mathew / Bhakta Gavin / Bhakta Peter / Gokularāṇī-devī dāsī / Subhadrā / Jagaddhātrī / Rucirā-devī dāsī / Rukmiṇī / Liza

## Fiji

**If the World Only Knew**

If I climbed upon the rooftops, would the people come to know?  
If I screamed from the tallest building, would they hear me below?  
That the greatest living being, most dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa,  
has put down his cane,  
And left this world no sooner than he came.  
Not one soul knew where he had gone  
Nor any a man what he had done.  
The Vaiṣṇava devotee’s footsteps moved swiftly over our head  
And the dust that fell kept the bhaktas well fed.  
The Lord’s mercy was manifest as Śrīla Prabhupāda  
The prolific transcendentalist churned a library on God  
I ate one śloka and a taste was acquired  
So sweet to the ears  
That I never grew tired.  
Your nectarean mercy fills our sacred Mandira  
Yet selfish as I am I have refused my peer  
Though he wallows in a cistern of foul-smelling pleasure  
I hesitate to save him from the hell no one should endure.  
Would that I had compassion upon these fallen souls  
As Śrīla Prabhupāda did grant to so many poor fools  
To preach, to teach, to move out on the town  
With Gītā, Bhāgavata and hari-nāma all around.  
Door-to-door, house-to-house, shop-to-shop  
Śrīman Mahāprabhu’s saṅkīrtana will never stop  
For wherever there is Kṛṣṇa the master of all mystics,  
And wherever there is Śrīla Prabhupāda, the supreme ācārya,  
There will also be opulence, victory, extraordinary  
power and morality.

*Vāsudeva dāsa – GBC, and the devotees of Kāliya-Kṛṣṇa*

## Gainesville

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept our fallen obeisances at your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Although we have very little understanding of your glories, it is our duty, as your eternal servants, to chant them to the best of our ability. Before you came we were wandering aimlessly through life looking for happiness and meaning. I shudder at the thought of where we would be now if you had not made your appearance.

Your accomplishments are incomparable. You have induced thousands of bewildered living entities who were previously addicted to meat-eating, intoxication, illicit sex, and gambling to abandon these habits and to accept a life of purity and renunciation. You have made the holy name of Śrī Kṛṣṇa a household word. You have defeated the teachings of the greatest atheists of the world and have attracted the attention of prominent personalities in every country. All this you have done in twelve short years.

It is obvious from your accomplishments that you are not just another *guru* or *ācārya*. Your ability to deliver the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa is unparalleled in the history of this world. Just as Lord Rāmacandra conquered Laṅkā and Rāvaṇa with His army of bears and monkeys, so you have liberated the whole plant with your army of *mlecchas* and *yavanas*. You were empowered by Lord Gaurāṅga Himself just to save the suffering living entities of Kali-yuga. Lord Kṛṣṇa says in *Bhagavad-gītā* (4.7–8), “Whenever and wherever there is a decline in religious practice, O descendant of Bharata, and a predominant rise of irreligion – at that time I descend Myself. In order to deliver the pious and annihilate the miscreants, as well as to reestablish the principles of religion, I advent Myself millennium after millennium.” Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that was your only business. Out of your infinite compassion you have given us a way out of this confusing world. You have given us the key to finding eternity, bliss and knowledge.

Parīkṣit Mahārāja said of Lord Kṛṣṇa, “The burden of the earth was certainly diminished by the Personality of Godhead and by others as well. When He was present as an incarnation, all good was performed because of His auspicious footprints.” (*Bhāg.* 1.17.26). Śrīla Prabhupāda, you also diminished the burden of the earth by your activities. Anyone who has a little knowledge can see the auspicious results of your spreading the chanting of the holy name all over the world. Now that you have left us we are wondering how we will carry on. Once Lord Caitanya asked Rāmānanda Rāya, *duḥka-madhye kona duḥka haya gurutara?* “Of all the kinds of distress, what is the most painful?” And Rāmānanda Rāya replied, *kṛṣṇa-bhakta-viraha vinā duḥka nāhi dekhi para:* “Apart from separation from the devotees of Kṛṣṇa, I know of no unbearable unhappiness.” (Cc. *Madhya* 8.248)

Now you have gone to take part in Lord Caitanya’s pastimes, and we are left feeling helpless and bewildered. But actually, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have not left us. Just as Kṛṣṇa never left the inhabitants of Vṛndāvana, you have not actually left us. Your *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Nectar of Devotion,* essays, tapes, and innumerable other divine instructions remain with us. We also have your appointed representatives to guide this movement according to time, place, and circumstance. You have burned a lasting impression within the hearts and minds of your sincere disciples. May we never forget your lotus feet.

Rāmānanda Rāya told Lord Caitanya, *āmi – naṭa, tumi – sūtra-dhara / yei mata nācāo, taiche cāhi nācibāra:* “I am just like a dancing puppet, and You pull the strings. Whichever way You make me dance, I will dance.” So we are your puppets, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we will dance just as you desire. The only value to our lives is our service to you. Any person who thinks he can carry out the mission of Lord Caitanya without remembering you and your desires does not understand Kṛṣṇa consciousness, for you are our connection to the spiritual world.

In the Fourth Canto you have informed us that, after the disappearance of the spiritual master, it is better for the disciple to give up his body than to live not executing the order of his *guru*. So our only business now, as always, is to cooperate with each other (unity is strength) and to continue what you have started. Purity is the force, and our purity lies in our ability to follow your instructions without adulteration.

We thank you for another year of devotional service. Our only desire is that we can give up all self-motivation and thus always remember your lotus feet as you always remember the lotus feet of your Guru Mahārāja.

*Your servants of ISKCON Gainesville.*

## Germany

Let us offer our respectful obeisances again and again unto the lotus feet of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

*te vai vidanty atitaranti ca deva-māyāṁ*  
*strī-śūdra-hūṇa-śabarā api pāpa-jīvāḥ*  
*yady adbhuta-krama-parāyaṇa-śīlaśikṣās*  
*tiryag-janā api kimu śruta-dhāraṇā ye*

**“Surrendered souls, even from the groups leading sinful lives, such as the women, the laborer class, the mountaineers, the Siberians, etc., or even the birds and beasts, can also know about the science of Godhead and become liberated from the clutches of the illusory energy by surrendering unto the pure devotees of the Lord and by following in their footsteps in devotional service.” (*****Bhāg.*** **2.7.46)**

Germany is a nation which lies divided, half-devoured by the tiger of atheism. Its people are classified in the *śāstras* as *hūṇas*, or hill tribesmen, who, being addicted to all sorts of sinful behavior, exist far, far outside the purview of the Vedic standard of civilization. They comprise just another unfortunate group of lost souls completely victimized by the age of Kali, in which religion, truthfulness, cleanliness, mercy, memory, bodily strength and duration of life are reduced to almost nil. Overwhelmed by the horrors of natural and unnatural disasters on one side, and fettered by their own slothful ignorance on the other, even the greatest thinkers of modern times have given up hope for salvation from the black whirlpool of Kali-yuga. Germany in particular has produced many such prophets of despair (e.g. Karl Marx, Adolf Hitler) whose philosophies have periodically pushed the world to the brink of cataclysm.

But now – suddenly – a visible change in consciousness has spread across the face of Germany and the face of the world. The iron grip of Kali is loosening from around the hearts of men. The tinsel-and-plastic prizes of materialistic civilization have lost their attraction, and even the most fallen persons are developing a sincere interest in knowing the truth which lies beyond the illusory display of *māyā*. Why?

It is due to Your Divine Grace.

Just by your brief visitation to our wretched planet, you have unleashed a spiritual revolution which is consuming the inauspicious qualities of this age and opening the way back home, back to Godhead, for everyone. *Sadyaḥ punanty upaspṛṣṭāḥ* – “at once sanctifying all who come in contact with him” – this is your quality, for you are *viṣṇu-pāda,* situated at the lotus feet of Viṣṇu, and *kṛṣṇa-preṣṭha*, very dear to Kṛṣṇa. And because you are the spotless representative of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, no one in this age of Kali can resist being drawn to your lotus feet. Therefore we see a new society rising from ruins of the old, a society of “surrendered souls, even from the groups leading sinful lives,” who know the science of Godhead.

What is that science? It is *sādhu-saṅga,* association with devotees of the Lord; *kṛṣṇa-sevā,* rendering service to Lord Kṛṣṇa as directed by the revealed scriptures; *bhāgavata,* regularly hearing and discussing the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā*; *nāma,* chanting sixteen rounds of the *mahā-mantra –* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare – and *vrajavāsa,* living in the holy *dhāma* of the Lord. By executing these scientific principles of *bhakti-yoga* one becomes immediately free from the bodily conception of life and can gradually understand his eternal spiritual relationship with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

This science of love of God was delivered five hundred years ago by Lord Kṛṣṇa in His *avatāra* feature of Lord Caitanya, and it has been protected since that time by the *paramparā* disciplic succession, headed by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda. But of all the great *acaryas* to whom this precious knowledge has been entrusted, it must be admitted that it is you, His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who have astounded the world the most. You have compiled the message of Mahāprabhu into a veritable library of transcendental literatures, each volume of which is exactly like your own touchstone association, which sanctifies all that it contacts. And to distribute these wonderful books and demonstrate their teachings for all to see, you have assembled an army of disciples from all corners of the earth. Though we may be German or American, African or Chinese, we have surrendered to you only, impelled by your causeless mercy. We are praying desperately on this day of your appearance for the spiritual power needed to push ahead your mission to all peoples of all nationalities and languages.

“Śrīla Prabhupāda! You are inconceivably powerful! You carry within your heart Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Madanamohana *vigraha*, and your form is the embodiment of devotional instruction! You are *jagad-guru,* the spiritual master of the universe, who spreads the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya throughout the three worlds! You are Prabhupāda, the master at whose feet all other masters sit, and you are *śaktyāveśa-avatāra,* who descends among us with Lord Caitanya’s full potencies! Feeling hindered by your aged body, you cast it off and accept the youthful bodies of your sincere disciples! Thus you are expanding yourself without limit, for ‘the *guru* is one, though manifested in an infinite variety of forms.’ We fall at your lotus feet again and again, desiring your association! We pray that your spiritual form will spring forth from our hearts and ignite the fire of devotional service within the hearts of all conditioned souls! O eternal preacher of *bhāgavata-dharma,* let us understand your eternal form by our preaching!”

*We remain your surrendered children and grandchildren:*

Schloss Rettershof

## Gītānagarī – RDTSKP

*My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. This year on your appearance day, your quality that looms largest in my mind is your extraordinary magnanimity. Your life is exemplary for every human being. It was easy to see, as you lay on your deathbed, so glorious in your humility and magnanimity, that you were unaffected by your body. Your only concern was for us, the fallen souls searching for Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. Your service was uninterrupted, despite your bodily inconvenience. You kept guiding us, comforting us, reassuring and encouraging us to continue our quest, and it was so obvious that you would also continue, that this apparent deathbed was really no deathbed at all, but a powerful affirmation of the eternal life of the spirit soul. Thanks to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am convinced that I am not this body, and thanks to you, I have an idea what it is like to be loved. I humbly pray for the strength to carry on your work.

*Your servants,*

## Guadalajara

In spite of our inability, whatever we have been able to hear from the spiritual master and whatever we could assimilate, we are now describing in glorification of our beloved spiritual master.

There are two things – *prakāśa* and *aprakāśa,* appearance and disappearance – in the spiritual realm. Of course, there is no such thing because we are eternal. That is the first teaching of Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhagavad-gītā.*

*na tv evāhaṁ jātu nāśaṁ na tvaṁ neme janādhipāḥ*  
*na caiva na bhaviṣyāmaḥ sarve vayam ataḥ param*

**“Never was there a time when I did not exist, nor you, nor all these kings; nor in the future shall any of us cease to be.” (Bg. 2.12)**

From this affirmation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead it is to be understood that we are eternal. That is also confirmed in the *Kaṭha Upaniṣad* (2.2.13): *nityo nityānāṁ cetanaś cetanānām.* We are all eternal, and there is a supreme eternal.

From these verses we understand there is no appearance or disappearance. The so-called appearance and disappearance pertain to the body, not the soul.

*na-jāyate mriyate nā kadācin nāyaṁ bhūtvā bhavitā vā na bhūyaḥ*  
*ajo nityaḥ śāśvato ‘yaṁ purāṇo na hanyate hanyamāne śarīre*

**“For the soul there is never birth nor death. Nor, having once been, does he ever cease to be. He is unborn, eternal, ever-existing, undying and primeval. He is not slain when the body is slain.”**

In order to learn all these subject matters, it is advised in the *Muṇḍaka Upaniṣad* (2.2.13):

*tad-vijñānārthaṁ sa gurum evābhigacchet*  
*samit-pāṇiḥ śrotriyaṁ brahma-niṣṭham*

**“In order to learn the transcendental science, one must approach the bona fide spiritual master in disciplic succession, who is fixed in the Absolute Truth.”**

We have to note there is a form of a Sanskrit verb in the verse *abhigacchet* which means that you *must* do. Sometimes we drive on the highway and there is this way or that way, but in the spiritual path there is no alternative. You must do: *abhigacchet.*

In the *Bhagavad-gītā*, Thirteenth Chapter, it is clearly stated that one should execute devotional service and advance on the path of spiritual knowledge by accepting the *ācārya*. *ācārya-upāsanam:* one should worship an *ācārya*, a spiritual master who knows things as they are. Therefore it is said that the devotee should execute devotional service by giving the greatest respect to the spiritual master. The spiritual master must be in the disciplic succession from Kṛṣṇa, as it is said in the *Padma Purāṇa: sampradāya-vihīnā ye mantrās te niṣphalā matāḥ.* “Unless you are initiated by a bona fide spiritual master in the disciplic succession, the *mantra* that you might have received is without effect.”

This acceptance of the spiritual master is not a matter of fashion. It is compulsory. One who is serious about understanding the goal of life requires a *guru*. It is advised by Kṛṣṇa Himself:

*tad viddhi praṇipātena paripraśnena sevayā*  
*upadekṣyanti te jñānaṁ jñāninas tattva-darśinaḥ*

**“Just try to learn the truth by approaching a spiritual master. Inquire from him submissively and render service unto him. The self-realized soul can impart knowledge unto you because he has seen the truth.” (*****Bg.*** **4.34)**

It is essential, because without the instruction of a bona fide spiritual master one cannot progress in the spiritual science. One should approach a spiritual master with all humility and offer him all service so that he will be pleased to bestow his blessings upon the disciple. Because *Bhagavad-gītā* spiritual master is a representative of Kṛṣṇa, if he bestows any blessings upon his disciple, the disciple’s goal of life is fulfilled immediately.

Now we are celebrating the appearance day of our beloved Jagad-guru, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but our eyes feel tears because of his recent departure from this material plane. This is the first year we are celebrating his appearance day without his transcendental manifested form. In the years before, we were offering our homage, Śrīla Prabhupāda was sitting on his *vyāsṣana,* and all of us were looking at him. Now we are offering our homage by following his instructions. Now we are looking at his books, and in this way we can see Prabhupāda sitting within our hearts.

On this glorious day, Śrīla Prabhupāda chose to come to the material plane, not by force but by his own sweet will. We have waited many, many births for this auspicious day. We have passed through so many bodies before getting this human form. It is stated in the *śāstra*:

*labhdvā sudurlabham idam bahu-sambhāvante*  
*mānuṣyam arthadam anityam apīha dhīraḥ*

This human form of life is obtained after many, many births, and although it is not permanent, it can offer the highest benefit. What is that benefit? To get association with the pure devotee of the Lord.

*milata nāhi aisa durlabha-janama*  
*kisi ko milate aho bhāgya se hari-bhakta he darśana*

Wandering through the fourteen worlds, the rare opportunity of a human birth is not easily obtained, and out of so many human beings only a few get the opportunity of meeting the devotees of Śrī Hari.

On this glorious day, His Divine Grace has arisen to disseminate the rays of the sun in order to dissipate the darkness of the ignorance of the universe. He has descended to teach us the real knowledge of devotional service and detachment. He has descended because he is an ocean of transcendental mercy. He has compassion for the poor and suffering souls. On this auspicious day we must remember his transcendental qualities. *Kṛṣṇa-kṛpā kī ānanda-mūrti dīnana karuṇā-nidha:* Śrī Gurudeva is the personification of the bliss of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. He is the storehouse of compassion for the poor and suffering souls.

*titikṣavāh karuṇikāh suhrdaḥ sarva-dehinām*  
*ajāta-śatravaḥ śāntāḥ sādhavaḥ sādhu-bhūṣaṇāḥ*

He is tolerant, merciful and friendly to all living entities. He has no enemies, is peaceful and abides by the scriptures. *Jñāna-bhakti-prema tina prakāśata śrī-guru patita-pāvana:* he reveals divine knowledge, pure devotion, and ecstatic love of God, and he is the savior of the fallen souls.

*mayy ananyena bhāvena bhaktiṁ kurvanti ye dṛḍhām*  
*material-kṛte tyakta-karmāṇas tyakta-svajana-bāndhavāḥ*  
*(Bhāg. 3.25.22)*

He is engaged in staunch devotional service to the Lord without deviation. For the sake of the Lord he renounces all other connections such as family relationships and friendly acquaintances within the world.

*mad-āśrayāḥ kathaā mṛṣṭāḥ śṛṇvanti kathayanti ca*  
*tapanti vividhās tāpā naitān mad-gata-cetasaḥ*  
*(Bhāg. 3.25.23)*

He is always chanting about Kṛṣṇa, and in this way is aloof from the material miseries because he is constantly filled with thoughts about Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes and activities.

In the *Bhāgavatam* (11.17.27) it is said:

*ācāryaṁ māṁ vijānīyān nāvamanyeta karhicit*  
*na martya-buddyāsūyeta sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*

**“One should understand the spiritual master to be as good as I am,” said the blessed Lord. “Nobody should be jealous of the spiritual master, who is the sum total of all demigods.”**

*yāhāra prasāde bhāi, e bhava tariyā yāi*  
*kṛṣṇa-prāpti haya yāhā haite*

By his mercy we cross over this dangerous material existence, and by his mercy Kṛṣṇa is obtained. Unless one is favored by the pure devotee, he cannot attain the platform of devotional service. To say nothing of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti,* one cannot even be relieved from bondage of material existence.

On this Vyāsa-pūjā day, we are worshiping Prabhupāda through his divine instructions. We must become deeply absorbed in understanding his desire by reading his books and thinking of his divine lotus feet and carrying out his desires. In this way, all the misconceptions and doubts of duality can be completely cut off by his merciful instructions. We can be free from the three modes of material nature. No longer will there be any chance of becoming entangled in birth and death.

We can bring within our minds the words of Śrīla Viṣvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura on this auspicious day. *Dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśaḥ:* we should always remember and praise the spiritual master. This is the best means of advancement.

*tasya tvaṁ tamaso ‘ndhasya duṣpārasyādya pāra-gam*  
*sac.cakṣur janmanām ante labdhaṁ me tvad-anugrahāt*  
*(Bhāg. 3.25.8)*

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the only means of getting out of the darkest region of ignorance, because you are my transcendental eye, which, by your mercy only, I have attained after many, many births.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are just like the sun, for you illuminate the darkness of the conditional life of the living entities. Because our eyes of knowledge were not open, we were sleeping eternally in that darkness without your shelter, and therefore we were falsely engaged by the actions and reactions of our material activities, and now we are very fatigued.

*atha me deva sammoham apākraṣṭum tvam arhasi*  
*yo ‘vagraho ‘ham mametity etasmin yojitas tvayā*  
*(Bhāg. 3.25.10)*

Now be pleased, my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda to dispel great delusion due to the feelings of false ego. We have identified the self with the body. We have taken shelter of your lotus feet because you are the only person of whom to take shelter. You can cut the tree of material existence.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s divine lotus feet are the abode of pure devotion. Therefore we have to bow down to him with great care so as not to commit any offense, because he is the greatest of transcendentalists. Śrīla Prabhupāda, now your glories are proclaimed throughout the three worlds.

*Guadalajara Yātrā.*

## Guatemala

*oṁ ajṅāna-timirāndhasya jṅanaṅjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept our humble obeisances unto your divine lotus feet. All glories to your divine grace, because due to it we can now be engaged in your transcendental loving service.

The Vedic scriptures state that the relationship between a disciple and spiritual master is eternal. And the relationship of a disciple with his spiritual master is as good as the relationship with the Lord.

Therefore, thank you thousands and thousands of times for your unlimited mercy, through which you are enlightening us and delivering us from the deep darkness of ignorance, and at the same time you are reestablishing our eternal relationship with God and the loving transcendental service of the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Thank you thousands and millions of times for your unlimited opulence, which is displayed by picking us to help in your eternal engagement – the transcendental loving service to Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Thank you again and again, because you have given us the intelligence to understand your exalted position, although we are *mlecchas,* the most fallen of mankind. Lord Caitanya once said, “You must at least have enough intelligence to find out who is your spiritual master and who is not.” O beloved ācāryadeva, you are the incarnation of *bhakti* to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. O Gurudeva! We are praying to retain our service attitude to the glorious Lord. We don’t want to fall again into the abominable materialistic life to identify ourselves wrongly with the body, which is the source of all miseries, because in such darkness one cannot know the ultimate goal of life, which is to come to knowledge of God, our relationship with Him, our duties in relation with Him, and, at the end, to return to Him after we give up our present material body.

O Gurudeva, indeed you are the bona fide representative of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Let us take shelter of your lotus feet. Bestow upon us the transcendental loving service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, accept this Vyāsa-pūjā offering from your very fallen servants of Guatemala Mandira.

## Guayaquil

*oṁ ajṅāna-timirāndhasya jṅanaṅjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkest ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torch of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

All glories to you, beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda. You kindly pulled us out of this material ocean, enthusing us in spiritual life and engaging us in loving service to Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You have enabled us to go back to our eternal home.

## Guyana

To our eternal divine spiritual master, His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda 108 Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktivedanta Swami Mahārāja Prabhupāda.

*Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,*  
Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this blessed Vyāsa-pūjā day we cannot help but reflect on the transcendental nature of your auspicious appearance and disappearance, being as it is a manifestation of Kṛṣṇa’s internal energy. In our fallen condition of nescience it is difficult for us to understand that both your appearance and disappearance cannot be compared to that of any of the conditioned souls who are bound to the wheel of *saṁsāra*, or repetition of birth and death and their concomitant miseries in this material world, but nevertheless it is so.

As a *nitya-siddha*, eternally liberated soul, you are appearing only for the purpose of executing the mission of the Supreme Lord; that is, you reawaken the *nitya-baddhas,* eternally conditioned souls, from their sleeping condition of eternal forgetfulness to their constitutional position as it is enjoined in the scriptures. *Jīvera svarūpa haya, kṛṣṇera ‘nitya-dāsa’.* Therefore we must understand that you are not under the control of the powerful external energy. You have come to teach us how, by surrendering to the lotus feet of Govinda, we can get out of the influence of the material energy. As it is stated in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (7.14):

*daivī hy eṣā guṇamayī mama māyā duratyayā*  
*mām eva ye prapadyante māyām etāṁ taranti te*

**“This divine energy of Mine, consisting of the three modes of material nature, is difficult to overcome. But those who have surrendered unto Me can easily cross beyond it.”**

You also showed us at the time of your disappearance that you were not under the influence of the material energy. This was seen when one doctor who was attending to you was completely baffled as to the regularity of your heart and pulse beat, even though you were supposed to be materially dilapidated in bodily health. This showed a direct superiority over the laws of material nature. You showed us too, how one has to live and spend his time in this material world engaging in total Kṛṣṇa consciousness (devotional service), and at the time of death of the material body how one should remember Kṛṣṇa and meditate on His holy names as explained by Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (8.5).

*anta-kāle ca mām eva smaran muktvā kalevaram*  
*yāḥ prayāti sa mad-bhāvām yāti nāsty atra saṁśayaḥ*

**“And whoever, at the time of death, quits his body remembering Me alone at once attains My nature. Of this there is no doubt.”**

Therefore, we can understand that you have gone back to Kṛṣṇa in the supreme planet, Goloka Vṛndāvana, to associate with the Supreme Lord directly. Now we have to associate with you by following your instructions more strictly than ever before. Simply by performing *saṅkīrtana-yajña* we can resume our *sac-cid-ānanda* state in that eternal kingdom of God, and thus we will be able to meet with you there again.

You are so mercifully allowing unqualified and lowborn fools like ourselves the opportunity to assist you in your mission. You are showering your mercy on all the conditioned souls by leaving a vast amount of pure spiritual literature, especially *Śrīmad-Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.* Just like the monsoon clouds that pour torrents of rain everywhere without any discrimination, you have similarly showed us how to distribute these great literatures all over the world, so that the entire human society may benefit from them. This is your desire. But as your disciples we must consider ourselves especially fortunate to have been awarded the opportunity to assist you directly in some way or other in your eternal mission to reclaim all the fallen souls back home, back to Godhead. We pray that you engage us eternally as your menial servants in distributing more profusely your books. This book distribution is your chief desire and chief means of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all around the world. We beg you to forgive all the offenses that we have committed at your lotus feet while you were physically here with us and all that we might commit in your physical absence. You have said that you are eternally present in your instructions, so we can also understand that we can attain perfection by following strictly your instructions, which are the same as yourself. Therefore we thank you for especially empowering and blessing these eleven confidential disciples, who have now become spiritual masters by your grace, also the GBC representatives of ISKCON and all other bona fide disciples passing on your divine instructions in the strict disciplic succession. In this way you have given us the assurance that the blessed Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement that you started will be continued until the whole of the planet earth is changed into a Vaikuṇṭha planet in this age of Kali.

*Your disciples of Guyana ISKCON,*

Siddhajana dāsa / Bhūtādi dāsa / Bhakta Richard / Bhakta Suresh / Bhaktin Joan / Bhaktin Shirley / Bhaktin Vijaya

## Haridaspur

He reasons ill who tells that Vaiṣṇavas die  
when thou art living still in sound!  
The Vaiṣṇavas die to live, and living try  
to spread the holy name around.  
—Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Thakura

(This verse is placed upon the *samādhi* of Śrīla Haridāsa Ṭhākura in Śrī Puruṣottama-kṣetra, Jagannātha Purī.)

The passing of a Vaiṣṇava like our *nāmācārya* Śrīla Haridāsa Ṭhākura, and like our Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Mahārāja Prabhupāda, is a moment of intense emotional ecstasy, of contradictory feelings, of inconceivable separation and meeting. But like Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who was brought to Kurukṣetra to see Lord Jagannātha and lost His Kṛṣṇa of Vṛndāvana, thus experiencing *divyonmāda*, transcendental madness in separation from Kṛṣṇa, it is our duty to understand it as far as possible.

*kṛpa kari’ kṛṣṇa more diyāchilā saṅga*  
*svatantra kṛṣṇera icchā – kailā saṅga-bhaṅga*

**“Being merciful upon Me, Kṛṣṇa gave Me the association of Haridāsa Ṭhākura. Being independent in His desires, He has broken that association.” (Cc.** ***Antya*** **11.94)**

Such is the moment of millennium where the torrents of rain during our Bengali Āṣāḍha-Śrāvaṇa season try to separate us from the order of Śrīla Prabhupāda, O Ṭhākura, O Govinda. In such a moment of separation, one’s final meeting is crystallized into the ever-fresh *paramparā.*

From a corner of West Bengal, at Ṭhākura Haridāsa’s holy sitting place, with our flag of *tulasī* strong and high above the Nayana-bhaṅgā River, we see in the distance the shape of great devotion in reviving these holy spots. By the remarkable tolerance of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Mahārāja Prabhupāda, our crown jewel, and his ability to electrify a band of *mlecchas* into a row of valuable jewels, let us dance out into the streets with our *śāstras*, into the jungles with our *prasāda* relief, claiming every *tīrtha* like Ṭhākura Haridāsa Śrīpat, Paṇīhāṭī, Lord Nityānanda’s Ekacakrā village, Rāmakeli, etc. Let us unit again, O Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava brothers! O Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya!

In such a mood of separation, let the three worlds learn the lesson ISKCON has learned: “There is such wonderful power in seeing A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Mahārāja Prabhupāda!”

*The imitation devotees of Ṭhākura Haridāsa Śrīpat Haridaspur.*

Bhakti-purabhāva Svāmī / Dīna-dayālu dāsa / Tirthapāda dāsa / Nārāyaṇa prabhu / Prasanna prabhu / Bhakti-prasāda prabhu

## Honolulu

To touch the one eternal gift  
Of love at Kṛṣṇa’s feet,  
We had not even hope to lift  
Above depths of conceit.

As fool or teacher, rich or poor,  
In one abyss we dove –  
Absorbed in matter, we were sure  
Of only death below.

Until Your Grace and uncalled self  
Alerted us en route,  
We though of freedom, but to hell  
Our senses did pursue.

You save the soul a million times  
By giving Kṛṣṇa once –  
For death on death just multiplies  
If elsewhere someone hunts.

By chanting Kṛṣṇa’s holy name  
You showed the path sublime;  
No higher mercy can be gained,  
Nor blissful love, than thine.

Today we worship this event  
When you appeared on earth,  
This age is blessed by blessing’s self:  
Your transcendental birth.

Smṛti, Purāṇas, Mahābhārata –  
Chronicle all cosmic time;  
Yet, search the spectrum, near or far,  
We find no deeds like thine.

For you are Lord Caitanya’s own,  
And his gift was most rare;  
And you, like He, have Kṛṣṇa sown  
Among us everywhere.

In this great age of sin and war  
The world is parched of good,  
Yet by the mercy that you’ve poured,  
The drought has been withstood.

To thirsty millions you gave life  
By what you lived and taught;  
No other savior equals you  
Or brings what you have brought:

A one-man ocean of relief,  
You gave the holy name,  
Immersing all at Kṛṣṇa’s feet,  
And drowning mankind’s pain.

Book after book you gave us proof  
Of Kṛṣṇa – nothing stands  
Against your science and your truth  
You’ve conquered Māyā’s lands.

We, disciples, set in motion  
By your waves of grace,  
Only pray for more devotion  
To work your mercy’s ways.

## Houston

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāni-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāśatya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajṅāna-timirāndhasya jṅanaṅjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. You are the most glorious personality and you are the savior of the entire world. From your lotus mouth emanates the knowledge which destroys all the ignorance of this material world.

It is by your mercy that we, as disciples, make advancement in devotional service. Therefore we are constantly begging for you to be merciful unto us. Our fervent prayer is, “Please give us the shade of your lotus feet.” As a tree provides soothing shade to whomever steps underneath I, similarly you provide soothing shade from the heat of material life to whomever steps underneath your divine instructions, i.e. to chant sixteen *good* rounds and follow the four regulative principles. “Crooked” means to disregard these instructions. We will never feel separate from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so long as we stay simple.

“He lives forever by his divine instructions and the follower lives with him.”

*Your humble servants at Houston ISKCON,*
